## Phase 7 — Recording Performance, Export Pipeline & Video Output

### Objective
Measure the Phase 2 recording pipeline and fix what the measurements show (Aadhi's clips stalling
while a lesson is recorded), choose the frame rate, resolution and bitrate from data, make the start
and end of a recording exact, and add the outputs around the WebM: an MP4 copy where a real encoder
exists, WebVTT subtitles from the lesson's own narration, and chapters from the scene timing.

### Baseline (start of Phase 7)
Backend 110/110, frontend 78/78, Phase 1 mascot 27/27, Phase 3 assets 26/26, Phase 4 visuals 24/24,
Phase 5 AI cache 11/11, Phase 6 Visual Review 17/17, Phase 2 export 30/30, export with library
assets 35/35 — all passing, none allowed to regress.

**Why the old smoothness failure went away (recorded here as required).** The Phase 2 export check
"Aadhi is never still" failed from the end of Phase 3 to Phase 6. It was not a recording problem:
exports started from a saved lesson's start card recorded the whole lesson under that card's
full-screen blur, so the mascot area looked still. Phase 6 fixed the card (the export's play step
hides it) without touching any recording setting, and the check has passed since. Phase 7 keeps
that fix and now also verifies it in the recording itself: the recorder only starts once the card
is hidden and the lesson is visibly playing.

### Audit (recording pipeline before Phase 7)
- `getDisplayMedia` (this tab, with its sound) → `MediaRecorder`, `video/webm;codecs=vp9,opus`,
  **60 fps requested, 15 Mbit/s**, 1 s timeslice, 1.5 s tail after the last scene, resumable 8 MB
  chunk upload, server-side validation (ffprobe; remux) in `/complete`, `video_exports` table.
- The recorder started *before* the lesson was started, so the first second could show the page
  before playback; the start card bug above had hidden this.
- Nothing measured the run: no record of delivered frames, stalls, fallbacks or timing.
- Aadhi's clips (`video_template/`) are H.264 High, 1280×720, 24 fps, 8 s, one keyframe at t=0,
  with AAC tracks; the page plays them muted.
- Audio: every sound of the page (narration `<audio>`, AI video sound, the Web Audio effects, all
  to the page's normal output) reaches the recording once through the tab's captured audio. The
  mascot clips are muted, and nothing routes captured audio back into the page, so there is no
  duplicate track and no feedback loop. The 1.5 s tail keeps the last words.

### Architecture (after Phase 7)
```
Lesson ─► Visual Review decisions (Phase 6) ─► prepare (preload media, report missing, AI cache only)
  ─► choose tab (getDisplayMedia: this tab + its sound; >1080p scaled down, never up)
  ─► recording mode (controls and panel hidden, glass blur off) ─► start playback ─► confirmed playing
  ─► MediaRecorder (VP9/Opus WebM by capability, 30 fps, 8 / 12 Mbit/s, 1 s slices; scene and
     caption times on the recorder's clock) ─► last scene + 1.5 s ─► stop ─► file
  ─► resumable 8 MB upload ─► /complete: validate (ffprobe, remux) + subtitles (.vtt), chapters and
     timeline from the page's log ─► COMPLETED: WebM downloadable, listed after refresh
  ─► background: WebM → Asset Library; MP4 copy (libx264 + AAC, subtitles and chapters embedded)
     → validated → Asset Library   (MP4 failure = mp4 output "failed"; WebM untouched)
```
Modules: `export.js` (recorder, flow, panel, API client), `index.html` (export hooks, recording mode,
caption log), `exports.py` (API, outputs, background MP4), `export_outputs.py` (subtitles, chapters,
encoder detection and conversion), `mascot.js` (playback measurements).

### Measurement instrumentation (added)
- `mascot.js`: `resetMetrics()` / `getMetrics()` — clip switches, fallbacks (count, reasons, time
  in fallback), watchdog stalls (how long frozen, tab hidden or not), `waiting` / `stalled`
  events, reloads, play failures, plus the last 300 events with the video's ready state, network
  state, position and buffered time ahead.
- `export.js`: `LessonRecorder.metrics` (requested and delivered track settings, MIME type,
  bitrates, chunks, bytes, start and stop latency, duration) and a frame monitor over the page
  during recording (frames, frames over 50 ms, longest gap). `ExportFlow.lastMetrics` combines
  recorder, page and mascot figures for each export.
- `tests/export_performance_browser_check.mjs` runs a whole export and reports all of these plus
  the file's own facts (ffprobe frame count, duration, bitrate, audio peak) and Chrome's CPU time.

### Aadhi stall diagnosis
- **Normal playback has no stalls** (0 in 45 s of scene changes, 720p and 1080p windows).
- **During recording the stalls happen with the clip file fully loaded** (network idle, 1.9–4.6 s
  decoded data ahead of the playhead, frozen 3.5–4 s until the watchdog's fallback): the clip's
  decoder is starved of time while the tab is captured and encoded. It is not buffering,
  preloading or network.
- Headless Chrome on this machine is **GPU-accelerated** (ANGLE on the Intel UHD Graphics, hardware
  compositing, rasterization, video decode and encode — checked in `chrome://gpu`), so the results
  are the machine's real limits, not a software renderer's.
- **At 60 fps** Chrome could not deliver the requested frames (~40 fps) and Aadhi stalled 3–4 times
  per lesson, 2–3 of them long enough for the Phase 1 fallback.
- **At 1080p** the page itself was the bottleneck: without any recording a 1920×1080 lesson page
  ran at **30 fps** (720p: 61 fps). With the glass blur (`backdrop-filter`) of the lesson board
  and panels disabled, the same 1080p page ran at **85 fps**: the blur is recomputed for every
  frame of the moving studio video behind it. Recording on top of that starved Aadhi's decoder
  (5–6 stalls) whatever the capture size or frame rate (scaling the capture to 720p, or 24 fps,
  did not help).

### Recording mode
`NORMAL` → `EXPORTING` (`body[data-mode="exporting"]`, from the play step) → `RECORDING`
(`body[data-recording]`, from the moment the page's controls hide until they return, also for
manual ● recordings). While recording:
- the start card is hidden, the lesson has been started and is confirmed playing before the
  recorder starts; reviewed visuals, removed visuals, narration and Aadhi behave exactly as in
  normal playback (the same code paths);
- **the glass blur is switched off** (`backdrop-filter: none` under `body[data-recording]`). The
  blurred board and panels are 82–90% opaque, so the recording keeps their look (pixel check of
  the board: SSIM 0.96, the difference being faint background particles showing through); the
  blur returns when recording ends, including after errors and cancellation (the hook runs in
  `finally`). Normal lesson playback is unchanged. This follows the phase rule to apply
  recording-specific optimizations only during recording.

### Start / stop synchronization
Prepare lesson (Visual Review decisions applied, media preloaded, missing media reported) →
choose the tab → hide the page's controls and the export panel → **start playback** (start card
hidden, intro started) → **wait for confirmed playback** (the intro's logo video fires `playing`;
3 s safety limit) → **start the recorder** → the recording clock starts (`recordingStarted`:
scene, caption and Aadhi measurements are timed from the recorder's real start) → last scene →
1.5 s tail → stop the lesson and the recorder → build the file → upload → validate and save.
Measured: the recorder's own start (from `start()` to its `start` event, i.e. encoder set-up) takes
0.45–0.73 s while the lesson is already playing, and every time is measured from that event; the
finished file is ready 4–17 ms after the stop request.

### FPS experiment and decision (Step 4/33)
Seven-scene benchmark lesson (chart, a clip chosen in Visual Review, a removed picture, a quiz,
Aadhi moving between placements), 1280×720 window, VP9 at the old 15 Mbit/s unless noted:

| Setting | Runs | Aadhi stalls / fallbacks | Delivered fps | File (≈68 s) | Chrome CPU s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 60 fps (old) | 3 | 3–4 / 2–3 | 39–41 | 154–159 MB | 162–172 |
| **30 fps** | 2 | **0 / 0** | 28.6–29.0 | 116–121 MB | 129–162 |
| 24 fps | 2 | 0 / 0 | 22.8–23.2 | 96–98 MB | 141–155 |
| 30 fps, 8 Mbit/s | 1 | 0 / 0 | 28.7 | 64 MB | 123 |

**Decision: 30 fps.** 60 fps was not deliverable (it produced ~40 uneven fps) and caused every
stall; 30 fps delivered nearly every frame with no stall; 24 fps was equally stable but offers no
reliability gain over 30 fps and less motion smoothness (Aadhi's clips are 24 fps, the lesson's
own animations are not). Configurable per browser for experiments (`localStorage`
`aadhi_export_fps`, 10–60) and per test run (`EXPORT_TEST_FPS`).

### Codec / MediaRecorder configuration
Capability detection in order: `video/webm;codecs=vp9,opus` → `vp8,opus` → `h264,opus` →
`video/webm` → `video/mp4` (only if the browser itself records MP4), otherwise a clear
"unsupported browser" message. Never a hard-coded unsupported type and never an MP4 label on WebM
data. `videoBitsPerSecond` from the capture size (below), `audioBitsPerSecond` the browser's
default (Opus), 1 s timeslice. Chunks are released once the file is built.

### Resolution decision (Step 10)
The recording keeps the tab's own size (the text stays as sharp as on screen); captures larger than
**1920×1080** (HiDPI / 4K screens) are scaled down to 1080p with `applyConstraints`, and smaller tabs
are never scaled up. Found and fixed during the phase: asking `getDisplayMedia` for a maximum size
made Chrome **upscale** a 720p tab to 1080p (bigger file, 19.6 fps, 4 stalls); the size is now
limited only after the capture starts, and only downward. Measured (30 fps, with the recording
mode above):

| Window | Capture | Stalls / fallbacks | Delivered fps | Page fps | File |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1920×1080, blur on (before) | 1080p | 5 / 4 | 21.5 | 25.5 | 72 MB |
| 1920×1080, blur on, capture scaled to 720p | 720p | 6 / 5 | 22.5 | 25.7 | 47 MB |
| 1920×1080, blur on, 24 fps | 1080p | 5 / 4 | 20.0 | 26.6 | 67 MB |
| **1920×1080, recording mode** | 1080p | **0 / 0** | 27.4 | 84.8 | 94 MB |
| **1280×720, recording mode** | 720p | **0 / 0** | 27.4 | 99.3 | 64 MB |

A lower cap was rejected: it did not reduce stalls (the page's rendering, not the encoder, was the
limit) and would blur lesson text. Configurable for experiments (`aadhi_export_max_height`,
`EXPORT_TEST_MAX_HEIGHT`).

### Bitrate decision (Step 11)
Old: 15 Mbit/s requested, 19.3 Mbit/s actual (VBR overshoot), 167 MB for 69 s. Frames of lesson text
at 8 Mbit/s and 15 Mbit/s were compared side by side at 720p and are indistinguishable. **New: 8
Mbit/s up to 720p, 12 Mbit/s above** (the extra pixels of 1080p). Result: 64 MB (720p) and 94 MB
(1080p) for the same lesson, and upload + save 26–34 s instead of 57 s. Configurable
(`aadhi_export_bitrate`, `EXPORT_TEST_BITRATE`).

### Chunks, progress, cancellation, retry, persistence, history
- Recording data is flushed every second and held until the file is built, then released; an
  empty recording is reported ("came out empty") instead of uploading nothing. Upload stays the
  Phase 2 resumable 8 MB chunk upload with real byte progress.
- Progress steps: Preparing lesson (asset *n of m*), Choose tab, Recording (scene *n of m*),
  Finalizing (size and format), Uploading (real bytes), **Checking and saving the video** (the
  server's validation; no percentage because none is known). No step shows an invented percentage.
- Cancel and retry keep the Phase 2 behaviour (cancel at any step, retry the upload from memory or
  the whole export); an MP4 copy that failed can be retried on its own (`POST
  /api/exports/{id}/outputs/mp4`) without touching the WebM.
- Everything is stored on the server: after a refresh the history lists the export, its WebM, MP4
  status, subtitles and chapters; the ready panel keeps checking the MP4 copy (every 3 s, up to
  30 min) and adds its button when ready.

### MP4 output (Steps 18–20)
- Only when a real encoder exists: the server's ffmpeg must offer `libx264` **and** `aac`
  (checked once; otherwise the MP4 output is `unavailable` with the reason, and the WebM is the
  video). A browser that recorded MP4 itself is not converted again.
- Conversion runs in the background after the export is saved (`make_mp4`), one at a time
  (semaphore), as an argument list (no shell, no user text in arguments): `libx264 -preset
  veryfast -crf 22 -pix_fmt yuv420p -threads 2`, `aac 160k`, `+faststart`, subtitles as
  `mov_text` and chapters from an ffmetadata file (both optional). Timeout `max(300 s, 6 ×
  duration)`. Output goes to a `.part.mp4` temp file, is validated with ffprobe (H.264 video, AAC
  audio, duration within 2 s / 5 % of the WebM) and only then renamed into place; temp files are
  removed on every path.
- **WebM stays first-class**: it is saved and downloadable before the MP4 starts, and an MP4
  failure only marks the `mp4` output `failed` with a message ("The WebM video is complete").
- New table `export_outputs` (export, kind `webm|mp4|vtt|chapters|timeline`, status, file, size,
  type, asset id, error); new endpoints `GET /api/exports/{id}/outputs/{kind}` (signed link or
  owner, same access rules as the video) and `POST /api/exports/{id}/outputs/mp4` (retry);
  `/link` returns `output_urls`.

### Subtitles (Steps 21–23)
WebVTT built on the server from what the page actually showed: while recording, the page logs every
caption line of its own subtitle track (`#subtitle-track`, the narration text including `[SYNC]`
segments) with start and end on the recording clock, and sends it with `/complete`. No
speech-to-text. `[SYNC]`/`[PAUSE]` markers and real HTML tags are removed (text such as `x < y` is
kept and escaped), cues are sorted, never overlap, end at or before the video's end, and are split
into at most two lines of 42 characters (long lines share their time by length); a line without an
end gets its reading time (≤ 7 s). Downloadable as `.vtt`, embedded in the MP4 when it is made.

### Chapters (Steps 24–25)
From the logged scene starts: one chapter per scene title, "Introduction" at 00:00 for the intro
(unless a scene starts within the first second), strictly increasing times (a scene shorter than a
second is replaced by the next), nothing past the end of the video. Downloadable as a readable list
(`00:00 Introduction`, `00:12 MEASURING EXPORTS`, `00:19 LOAD DATA`, … — the format video sites
accept in a description) and embedded as MP4
chapters. Not required for a successful export. The old page-side "YouTube chapters" helper was
removed (the server's chapters replace it).

### Visual Review, AI cache, Asset Library
- The export plays the lesson through the same Phase 6 decisions (explicit / approved / changed
  visuals shown, removed stay absent, pending as before) and the same Phase 5 cache; the benchmark
  verifies the chosen clip and the removed picture in the recording and zero generation requests.
- Finished videos are registered as Phase 3 assets (source `export`, "Lesson video", with the
  export id and title): the WebM, and the MP4 when made. They are excluded from the visual router's
  candidates (like narration), so a lesson video is never offered as a scene visual.

### File validation and integrity
Unchanged Phase 2 validation on `/complete` (size, ffprobe streams and duration, remux of unseekable
WebM); the benchmark also checks the downloaded file byte-for-byte against the stored size, its
narration level, and that Aadhi's area is never still for 1 s in his scenes.

### Files changed
- New: `export_outputs.py`, `tests/test_export_outputs.py`, `tests/export_performance_browser_check.mjs`.
- Modified: `export.js`, `mascot.js`, `index.html`, `exports.py`, `models.py`, `server.py`,
  `assets.py`, `assets.js`, `visuals.py`, `tests/export.test.js`, `tests/mascot.test.js`.

### Tests
- `tests/test_export_outputs.py` (15): WebVTT from narration lines (timing, line splitting, `[SYNC]`
  / `[PAUSE]` removed), cues never overlapping and ending inside the video (short, long, missing
  ends, pauses, several scenes), special characters, markup and mathematics (`x < y`, `&`, quotes, `->`, `σ = F / A`),
  no narration (a valid empty file), chapters (first at 0, increasing, short scenes, past the end),
  escaped ffmpeg chapter metadata; a real conversion with embedded subtitles and chapters (H.264 +
  AAC + mov_text, duration kept) and a failed conversion leaving no files; through the API:
  subtitles, chapters and MP4 with the export, signed links and another user refused, a failed MP4
  keeping the WebM and retried, no encoder (MP4 `unavailable`, export complete), older clients
  without a timeline, finished videos as library assets but never scene visuals, the history's
  outputs in one query.
- `tests/export.test.js` (+6): configured frame rate and bitrates and the recorder's measurements
  (30 fps, no size constraint, 12 Mbit/s for 1080p, 8 Mbit/s for 720p); a capture larger than 1080p
  scaled down, a smaller one left alone; recording starting only once the lesson is confirmed
  playing; the timeline sent with the upload; an empty recording failing clearly; MP4, subtitles
  and chapters offered as they become ready (polling, retry). `tests/mascot.test.js` (+1): the
  playback measurements.
- `tests/export_performance_browser_check.mjs` (18 checks, silent, real Chrome, throwaway server with
  the stand-in providers, 1280×720 by default, `EXPORT_TEST_FPS` / `EXPORT_TEST_BITRATE` /
  `EXPORT_TEST_VIEWPORT` / `EXPORT_TEST_MAX_HEIGHT` for the experiments): recording starts, stops
  and the export finishes; the start card hidden and the lesson playing when the recorder starts
  (the page's own record at the recorder's `start` event, including the intro video playing);
  the glass blur off while recording and back afterwards; the clip chosen in Visual Review
  recorded; the removed visual absent; Aadhi playing; the fallback not left on; no AI generation;
  the downloaded file whole and non-empty; narration in it; Aadhi never still for 1 s in the
  recorded frames; subtitles from the narration, ordered and inside the video; chapters from 00:00
  in scene order; the MP4 H.264/AAC with subtitles and as long as the WebM; the ready panel
  offering the video, MP4, subtitles and chapters; the export surviving a reload; no console
  errors or failed requests. It prints and saves the run's measurements (`metrics.json`).

### Results
Full regression (server restarted with Phase 7; all browser tests silent):

| Suite | Result |
|---|---|
| Static checks (6 inline scripts, 5 JS modules, 11 Python modules compile, the new check parses) | pass |
| Backend unit (`unittest`; 110 earlier + 15 new) | **125/125** |
| Frontend unit (`node --test`; 78 earlier + 7 new) | **85/85** |
| Phase 1 mascot browser check | **27/27** |
| Phase 3 Asset Library browser check | **26/26** |
| Phase 4 visual router browser check | **24/24** |
| Phase 5 AI cache browser check | **11/11** |
| Phase 6 Visual Review browser check | **17/17** |
| Phase 2 export end-to-end | **30/30** |
| Export end-to-end with library assets | **35/35** |
| Phase 7 recording performance check (1280×720) | **18/18** |

The Phase 7 check was run once more after its start checks were tightened to read the page's own
record at the recorder's `start` event (card hidden, lesson and intro video playing): 18/18 again.

Phase 7 check at the final settings (30 fps, 8 / 12 Mbit/s, 1080p cap, recording mode):

| Window | Checks | Stalls / fallbacks | Delivered fps | Aadhi longest still | File (≈69 s) | Outputs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1280×720 | 18/18 | 0 / 0 | 28.3 | 0.00 s | 63.9 MB VP9/Opus | MP4 ready, 13 cues, 8 chapters |
| 1920×1080 | 18/18 | 0 / 0 | 27.9 | 0.00 s | 95.4 MB VP9/Opus | MP4 ready, 13 cues, 8 chapters |

Compared with the start of the phase (60 fps, 15 Mbit/s, 720p window): Aadhi stalls 3–4 → 0,
fallbacks 2–3 → 0, delivered 41 of 60 fps (uneven) → 28 of 30, file 167 MB → 64 MB, upload and
save 57 s → 26–46 s. The Phase 2 export runs no longer report a mascot fallback, and Aadhi's longest
still moment in their recordings is 0.00 s in every scene.

AI provider calls during Phase 7 testing: **none** (backend: fakes, `AI_GENERATION_ENABLED=0`;
browser: throwaway servers with the stand-in providers, generation requests counted and asserted
zero; the earlier browser checks stub or intercept both generators).

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- **1080p recordings stalled Aadhi** because the page's glass blur is recomputed over the moving
  studio video every frame; fixed with the recording mode (blur off only while recording).
- **Asking `getDisplayMedia` for a maximum size upscaled smaller tabs** (a 720p tab recorded at
  1080p: bigger file, fewer delivered frames, stalls); the size is now limited only after capture
  starts, and only downward.
- **The recorder started before the lesson**, so a recording could begin on the page before
  playback; playback now starts and is confirmed first.
- 60 fps was requested but not delivered (~40 fps, uneven); 30 fps adopted from the measurements.
- The subtitle cleaner first removed text such as `x < y` as if it were a tag, and a caption
  without an end time lasted until the next one; both fixed and covered by tests.
- The MP4 test expected an exact stream list, but embedded chapters add a data stream; it now
  checks the required streams.
- An assumption made early in the phase (that headless Chrome here renders in software) was wrong:
  `chrome://gpu` shows full GPU acceleration, which is why page fps was a trustworthy signal.
- The page's own "YouTube chapters" helper was dead code after the server's chapters; removed.

### Browser limitations
- Tab capture needs a user's "Share this tab" choice (auto-accepted only in tests) and a
  Chromium-based browser for tab audio; other browsers get the existing clear message.
- The delivered frame rate depends on the machine: this laptop (Intel UHD Graphics) delivers ~27–29
  of 30 fps. Very large or HiDPI windows are capped at 1080p.
- Headless Chrome does not cap `requestAnimationFrame` at 60 Hz, so page fps above 60 in the
  tables means spare capacity, not what the viewer sees.

### Known limitations
- The MP4 copy needs ffmpeg with libx264 and AAC on the server; without them only the WebM (plus
  subtitles and chapters as files) is offered.
- Subtitles follow the captions the page showed; narration without on-screen captions (browser
  speech with captions hidden) produces no cues.
- One MP4 conversion at a time; a server restart during a conversion leaves it `processing` until
  it is retried (automatic recovery is Phase 9).
- The glass blur is absent from recordings (the panels keep their colour and opacity).

### Deferred to Later Phases
- **Phase 8:** providers are untouched.
- **Phase 9:** orphaned export files, interrupted MP4 conversions and old outputs are not cleaned
  up automatically.
- **Phase 10:** untouched.

### Final status
**COMPLETE.** Recording measured and fixed from the measurements (30 fps, recording mode without the
glass blur, capture never upscaled, 8 / 12 Mbit/s), start and stop synchronized with confirmed
playback, MP4 copy (real encoder only, WebM kept), WebVTT subtitles and chapters from the lesson's
own timing, finished videos in the Asset Library; all Phase 7 tests pass at 720p and 1080p and
every Phase 1–6 suite passes in full (Phase 1 27/27). No AI provider was called during testing.
Nothing was committed, pushed or deployed.

---

## Phase 8 — Multi-Provider AI Media System

### Objective
Make Aadhi independent of any single AI image or video provider: one provider-independent AI media
layer that chooses a provider for each request, checks what it can do, falls back in a controlled way,
retries only what is worth retrying, validates what comes back, and keeps the Phase 5 cache, the Phase 3
library, the Phase 4 router and Phase 6 Visual Review authoritative. Infrastructure for later phases
(presenter, cinematic scenes, scene composer, visual director); none of those is implemented.

### Baseline (start of Phase 8)
Backend 125/125, frontend 85/85, Phase 1 27/27, Phase 3 26/26, Phase 4 24/24, Phase 5 11/11,
Phase 6 17/17, Phase 7 18/18, Phase 2 export 30/30, export with library assets 35/35.

### Audit (before Phase 8)
- **Images:** `POST /generate-ai-image` → `server._fetch_ai_image` → Pollinations only (HTTP GET, fixed
  800×1200, random seed); a failure fell back to a subject image. **Videos:** `POST /generate-ai-video` →
  `server._generate_ai_video` → whatever "Start AI Server" loaded (`ltx_pipeline`: Veo through the Gemini
  API, a local LTX pipeline, or the manual workflow). No fallback between providers, no retries, no
  capability model; provider names hard-coded in `server.py` and `visuals.py` (the router always planned
  images "via pollinations", even when another provider was configured).
- **Cache identity (Phase 5)** already contained provider, model and the settings each provider is sent
  (`ai_cache.PROVIDER_SETTINGS`); cache entries record provider and model; the asset keeps
  `details.generation` provenance. The router's cache step computed identities with the one current provider.
- **Errors:** provider failures became `HTTPException(500, str(e))` (unsanitized, unclassified). A Veo
  generation held the HTTP request open for up to 5 minutes.
- **Tests** faked providers by patching `server._generate_ai_video` / `server._fetch_ai_image`;
  `AI_FAKE_PROVIDER=1` gave browser tests a local ffmpeg stand-in.
- **Configured here:** no provider key (`GEMINI_API_KEY` empty), no GPU/torch; Pollinations needs no key.
- **Live check of the existing image provider (2026-09-29):** Pollinations' free image endpoint
  (`image.pollinations.ai/prompt/…`, the one the project has always used) now answers **HTTP 402 Payment
  Required** with an x402 payment request ("Pollinations legacy image", 0.01 USDC per image on the Base
  network), or HTTP 500. Automatic AI images therefore no longer work for free on any Aadhi installation
  that relies on it — exactly the single-provider dependency this phase removes.
- **Provider documentation checked (official sources):** Google's model list now shows Veo
  `veo-3.1-generate-preview` / `veo-3.1-lite-generate-preview` and no longer `veo-2.0-generate-001` (the
  project's configured model); **Imagen is shut down** in the Gemini API (replaced by Gemini image models
  `gemini-2.5-flash-image`, `gemini-3.1-flash-image`, …); the google-genai SDK installed (2.25.0) documents
  `models.generate_videos` → `operations.get` polling → `files.download(file=video)`, and
  `models.generate_content(..., config=GenerateContentConfig(response_modalities=["IMAGE"], image_config=ImageConfig(aspect_ratio=...)))`.
  The existing Veo code downloaded with `generated_video.video.name`, a field the installed SDK's `Video`
  type does not have — it would have failed after a paid generation (fixed in the Veo adapter).

### Architecture
```
Lesson ─► Visual Router (Phase 4: matching unchanged; never names a provider)
              │ "an AI video / image is needed"
              ▼
        AI MEDIA LAYER  (ai_media.py)
              │  MediaRequest {media type, prompt, [provider, allow_fallback, model, aspect, duration], purpose, scene}
              ├──► Provider Registry (ai_providers.py): preference → configured order → capabilities →
              │        configuration → recent failures  ⇒ ordered candidates, each usable or not, with why
              ├──► AI Media Cache (Phase 5, ai_cache.py): identity per candidate {media, provider, model,
              │        prompt, parameters}; the preferred provider's result, a fallback's only when used
              ├──► generate: one per identity at a time (cache lock) ─ per-provider concurrency limit ─
              │        bounded retries (transient only) ─ next provider when the failure allows it
              │        ┌──────────┬──────────────┬─────────┬─────────┬─────────┐
              │        Pollinations Gemini image    Veo       LTX       manual     (fake, fake-alt in tests)
              ├──► validate the media (hard checks reject; soft checks become warnings)
              ├──► Asset Library (Phase 3): the file becomes a normal asset, provenance in details.generation
              ├──► cache entry under the identity of the provider that REALLY made it
              └──► generation run (ai_generation_runs): choice, attempts, job id, timing, result
              ▼
        Visual Review (Phase 6: decisions authoritative; shows provider, model, backup, warnings)
              ▼
        Export (Phase 2/7: unchanged; plays the same assets)
```

### Provider abstraction (`ai_providers.py`)
- **`MediaRequest`** (Aadhi's terms only): media type, prompt, optional explicit `provider`,
  `allow_fallback`, `model`, `aspect_ratio`, `duration_seconds`, `force_regenerate`, purpose, project,
  scene, slot. No endpoint, key or provider parameter name appears in it.
- **`GenerationResult`**: status (`queued | running | completed | failed | cancelled | unsupported`),
  provider, model, media type, output path, provider job id, metadata (seed, model version, filtered
  count — never secrets), error, timing.
- **`Capabilities`** (only meaningful fields): media types; execution `sync | job | local | manual`;
  models it may be asked for; aspect ratios; video durations it can be asked for (`None` = fixed
  length); inputs (`text` today — image-to-video later); negative prompt; audio; prompt length; `paid`.
- **`AIProvider`** interface: `capabilities()`, `configuration()` (from configuration only, never a
  network call), `settings_for(request)` (what it is sent = what the cache identity contains),
  `check(request)` (raises `UNSUPPORTED` with the reason), `generate(request, settings, dest, progress)`.
  `SyncProvider` runs one blocking call in a worker thread under a timeout; `JobProvider` submits a job,
  polls it (bounded by the timeout, cancellable — a cancelled request asks the provider to cancel), then
  fetches the result; a job that runs out of time is never resubmitted (it may still be billed).
- **Errors** (`ProviderError`, categories): `configuration`, `auth`, `unsupported`, `rejected`,
  `rate_limited` (with `retry_after`), `unavailable`, `timeout`, `invalid_output`, `cancelled`. HTTP
  statuses, SDK API errors (their `code`), network errors and adapter bugs are classified in one place.
  Messages are sanitized (keys, bearer tokens, `key=`/`token=`/signature parameters, every configured
  `*_API_KEY`/`*_TOKEN`/`*_SECRET`/`*_PASSWORD` value) before they are logged or shown.

### Providers (adapters)
| Provider | Media | Execution | Configured when | Status here |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `pollinations` | image | sync HTTP (allow-listed host) | always (no key) | configured, but **its free endpoint now requires payment** (live call, see Results) |
| `gemini-image` | image | sync SDK `generate_content` (IMAGE) | `GEMINI_IMAGE_ENABLED=1` + `GEMINI_API_KEY` (billed, opt-in) | disabled / not configured — mocked tests only |
| `veo` | video | job: `generate_videos` → `operations.get` → `files.download` | `GEMINI_API_KEY` and (AI server started with Gemini, or `VEO_ENABLED=1`) | not configured — mocked tests only |
| `ltx` | video | local GPU pipeline | an LTX profile loaded by "Start AI Server" | not configured (no GPU) |
| `manual` | video | the user places the file | AI server profile "No AI Generation" | available (the default profile) |
| `fake`, `fake-alt` | both | job (videos) / sync (images), ffmpeg | `AI_FAKE_PROVIDER=1` only (then no real provider is registered) | tests |

### Provider registry, configuration and selection
- One registry (`ProviderRegistry`) registers the adapters, reports their state (`available`,
  `not_configured`, `disabled`, `temporarily_unavailable`) with a reason, and makes the choice. Nothing
  else in the code picks a provider.
- **Preference:** videos — the provider "Start AI Server" chose (the existing mechanism); images —
  `AI_IMAGE_PROVIDER`, else the first of `AI_IMAGE_PROVIDERS`. **Order after it:** `AI_VIDEO_PROVIDERS`
  (default `veo,ltx`), `AI_IMAGE_PROVIDERS` (default `pollinations,gemini-image`). **Request level:**
  `provider` = an explicit choice, never silently replaced; `allow_fallback: true` allows others (an
  automatic choice falls back by default). `AI_PROVIDER_FALLBACK=0` switches fallback off. The manual
  workflow is never a fallback and, when chosen, excludes automatic generation.
- **Per candidate:** registered? makes this media type? enabled and configured? supports the model,
  shape, duration and prompt length? failed recently? → usable or not, with the reason. Deterministic and
  explained (runs, plans' debug trace and the provider panel show it).
- **Recent failures** (rate limit, unavailable, timeout) move a provider behind the others for
  `retry_after` or `AI_PROVIDER_COOLDOWN` (60 s) — it is still tried when nothing else can make the
  request (a provider that just failed is never excluded; the Phase 5 tests caught the first version,
  which excluded it). A refused prompt says nothing about availability.
- **Paid providers are opt-in:** Gemini image needs `GEMINI_IMAGE_ENABLED=1`; Veo is used only when the
  AI server was started with Gemini or `VEO_ENABLED=1`. No quality or "best provider" scores exist:
  order and preference come from configuration and the request only.
- **Configuration** is environment only (`.env.example` lists every variable); keys never leave the
  server; `GET /api/ai-media/providers` returns states, reasons, capabilities and order, never a secret.

### Fallback and retry
| Failure | Retried on the same provider | Next provider |
|---|---|---|
| not configured / disabled / unsupported | — (not tried) | yes |
| `rate_limited` | after its `Retry-After` if ≤ `AI_PROVIDER_MAX_RETRY_WAIT` (20 s) | yes |
| `unavailable` (5xx, network) | yes, bounded (`AI_PROVIDER_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, 2 tries, backoff) | yes |
| `timeout` (answer) | yes, bounded | yes |
| `timeout` (provider job ran out of time) | **no** (may still finish and be billed) | yes |
| `auth` | no | yes |
| `invalid_output` | no | yes |
| `rejected` (prompt/request refused) | no | **no** — the user rephrases (safety decisions are respected) |
| `cancelled` | no | no |
Every attempt is recorded (provider, try, category, time). When every provider fails the answer is one
normalized error (429 with `Retry-After` / 422 / 504 / 502 / 503 depending on the last failure) naming
each provider's reason; no asset, cache entry or file is left behind. The image endpoint's subject-image
fallback (existing behaviour) still applies after provider failures only.

### Cache interaction (Phase 5 extended, not duplicated)
- The identity scheme is unchanged (`v` 1): default requests have exactly the Phase 5 identities, so
  everything cached before Phase 8 is still reused (tested for Veo, LTX, Pollinations). A requested
  model, shape or duration changes the provider's settings and therefore the identity.
- Lookup order: the preferred provider's identity; a fallback provider's identity only when that
  fallback would be tried first (preferred not usable) or after the preferred one failed; with AI
  generation off, any candidate's earlier result (nothing new can be made). A healthy preferred provider
  is never replaced by a backup's older result.
- **A result is cached under the identity of the provider that actually made it** (provider B's video is
  never recorded as provider A's). If provider A fails and B already made the same request, B's asset is
  reused — no duplicate generation. One generation per identity at a time (the Phase 5 lock, taken per
  attempted provider). `force_regenerate` skips lookups, uses the same provider policy (with fallback),
  makes a new asset and keeps the old one and its lesson references.

### Asset Library interaction
Every generation is registered through the library exactly as before (videos adopted in place, images
stored once per content). `details.generation` keeps the Phase 5 fields (hash, provider, model, media
type, parameters, seed) plus `requested_provider`, `fallback_from`, `attempts`, `generation_ms`,
`job_id`, `run_id`, `warnings`, `purpose`. The library's own validation (decodable, right kind) still runs.

### Output validation
Hard checks (the output is rejected, the next provider may be tried): a file exists, ≥ 256 bytes, within
`AI_MAX_IMAGE_BYTES` / `AI_MAX_VIDEO_BYTES`, decodable (`media.inspect_media`, ffprobe), the requested
kind, at least 32 px each side, videos ≥ 0.5 s. Soft checks (kept as warnings on the asset, returned by
the API and shown in Visual Review for a person to judge): one flat colour (blank) in every sampled frame,
a shape more than 8 % off the expected one, a duration more than 50 % off a requested one, no sound
track from a provider that makes audio. Artistic quality and educational relevance are left to Visual
Review.

### Background generations, runs and observability
- `POST /generate-ai-video` with `wait: false` answers at once when nothing needs a provider (cache hit,
  AI off, manual workflow, errors) and otherwise returns `202 {status, run_id}`; the generation continues
  on the server and `GET /api/ai-media/runs/{id}` reports it (with `result` = the usual generator answer
  when done). The page uses this for videos, so a slow provider no longer holds one HTTP request open for
  minutes; `wait` defaults to `true` (older callers unchanged).
- `ai_generation_runs` (new table; nothing else in the schema changed): status, requested / actual
  provider, model, fallback, identity hash, provider job id, attempts, cache hit, asset, sanitized error
  and HTTP status, the selection and every attempt (no prompt), heartbeat. A queued/running run whose
  heartbeat stopped and that no request runs is reported `interrupted` (nothing is resumed — Phase 9).
  `POST /api/ai-media/runs/{id}/cancel` cancels a background generation (and the provider job where the
  provider supports it). `GET /api/ai-media/runs` lists the user's recent generations.
- Structured log lines `[AI MEDIA] {json}` (`select`, `generate`, `retry`, `fallback`, `provider_failed`,
  `completed`, `cache_hit`, `failed`, `queued`): media type, providers, model, hash prefix, attempt,
  category, time, run — never a prompt or secret (`AI_MEDIA_LOG=0` silences them). `/api/ai-cache/stats`
  adds provider calls per provider and counters for attempts, retries, fallbacks, failures and rejected outputs.

### Security and cost control
Keys from the environment only; statuses and errors sanitized; provider output is fetched only over https
from allow-listed hosts that resolve to public addresses (redirects checked the same way), size-capped and
time-bounded; SDK results are size-checked; downloaded media is validated before it becomes an asset.
Bounded retries and polling, per-provider concurrency limits (`<NAME>_MAX_CONCURRENT`, a queue with a
time limit), cache reuse, explicit regeneration only, paid providers opt-in, provider enable/disable.

### Visual Router and Visual Review
- The router asks the media layer which provider would make a planned AI visual and whose cached
  results count; `visuals.py` names no provider. Plans carry `provider`, `model`, `fallback_from` (and
  `quality_warnings` for generated assets); a planned visual that no provider can make says why.
- Visual Review decisions stay first; Keep / Change / Remove / New AI version work as before; "New AI
  version" follows the provider policy (fallback included) and keeps the old asset; opening the review
  never generates; fingerprints are unchanged. The detail shows "AI provider: fake-alt · model · backup
  for fake" and "Please check this visual: …" for flagged outputs.

### Frontend
- `visuals.js`: `AiMediaApi({background: true})` generates videos in the background and follows the run
  (polling every 2 s, bounded, tolerant of brief server hiccups; immediate answers taken as they are);
  status texts add "(backup AI provider)"; `provenanceText`, `ProvidersApi`, `stateLabel`; stored plans
  keep `model`, `fallback_from`, `quality_warnings`.
- `index.html`: background video generation; an "AI providers" panel under the AI Visuals setting
  (states, reasons, paid flag, order, recent generations; loaded when opened, no provider contacted).
- `review.js`: provider line and quality note.

### API
| Endpoint | Change |
|---|---|
| `POST /generate-ai-video` | optional `provider`, `allow_fallback`, `model`, `aspect_ratio`, `duration_seconds`, `wait`, `project_id`, `scene_index`, `slot`; answers add `provider`, `model`, `requested_provider`, `fallback_from`, `run_id`, `generation_ms`, `warnings`; `202 {run_id}` with `wait: false`; normalized errors |
| `POST /generate-ai-image` | the same optional fields (no `wait`); subject-image fallback after provider failures only |
| `GET /api/ai-media/providers` (new) | provider states, reasons, capabilities, preference, order, fallback switch |
| `GET /api/ai-media/runs`, `GET /api/ai-media/runs/{id}`, `POST /api/ai-media/runs/{id}/cancel` (new) | the user's generations: status, provenance, attempts, result |
| `POST /api/visuals/plan`, `/api/visuals/review` | plans may carry `model`, `fallback_from`, `quality_warnings` |
| `GET /api/ai-cache/stats` | adds `provider_calls_by_provider` and `media` counters |
Unchanged: `/start-ai-server`, `/get-image` (legacy, outside the layer, as documented in Phase 5).

### Adding a provider
1. Write an adapter in `ai_providers.py`: subclass `SyncProvider` (one call) or `JobProvider` (submit /
   poll / fetch / cancel); set `name`, `label`, `env_prefix`, `model_env` if it takes a model.
2. Implement `capabilities()` truthfully (media, execution, models, shapes, durations, inputs, paid).
3. Implement `configuration()` from environment variables only (key present, `*_ENABLED`, opt-in if billed).
4. Map its answers and errors to `GenerationResult` / `ProviderError` (use `error_for_status` /
   `error_from_exception`; refusals → `REJECTED`); write bytes with `write_bytes` or fetch URLs with
   `safe_download` and an allow-list; never log raw responses.
5. Add its settings to `ai_cache.PROVIDER_SETTINGS` (exactly what it sends; `shape_parameters` /
   `duration_parameters` for requested shapes/lengths) and register it in `ProviderRegistry.__init__`;
   add its name to the order variables' documentation.
6. Mock tests (`tests/test_ai_providers.py`): request/response normalization, success, every failure
   category, malformed answers, timeouts, polling, metadata — with the network blocked.
7. Integration: a media-layer test (`tests/test_ai_media.py`) — the identity differs from other
   providers', provenance names it after a fallback, the router plans it, Visual Review shows it.
8. Check `GET /api/ai-media/providers` shows its state and no secret.

### Files changed
- New: `ai_providers.py`, `ai_media.py`, `tests/test_ai_providers.py`, `tests/test_ai_media.py`,
  `tests/ai_providers_browser_check.mjs`.
- Modified: `server.py` (generator endpoints through the layer; provider code moved into adapters; new
  endpoints), `visuals.py`, `ai_cache.py` (settings of the new providers), `models.py`
  (`AIGenerationRun`), `visuals.js`, `review.js`, `index.html`, `.env.example`, `tests/visuals.test.js`,
  and the mocking seam of `tests/test_ai_cache.py`, `tests/test_visual_review.py`, `tests/test_visuals.py`.

### Tests
- `tests/test_ai_providers.py` (33, network blocked — any outside socket connection fails the test):
  error categories for HTTP statuses, SDK errors, timeouts, network errors and adapter bugs;
  `Retry-After` in seconds and dates; secrets masked (keys, bearer tokens, signed-URL parameters,
  configured values); safe downloads (https only, allow-listed hosts, private/loopback/metadata
  addresses refused, redirects checked, size cap, content type). Per adapter — **Pollinations**: the URL
  it builds, success and metadata, 429/503/401/400/timeout/network/wrong content type, shapes and
  unsupported requests; **Gemini image**: opt-in and key, the SDK call (model, IMAGE modality, aspect
  ratio), success, safety refusal, blocked prompt, no image, 429/401/404/503/timeout, configurable model;
  **Veo**: configuration states, submit/poll/download with the job id and progress, requested shape and
  duration, bounded polling that cancels and is not resubmitted, refused/failed/filtered/malformed jobs,
  429/403 at submission; **LTX**: needs a loaded pipeline, fixed length, failures not retried;
  **manual**: no regeneration. Registry: preference and order, the manual workflow never falling back,
  explicit choices not replaced (and allowed fallback), fallback switch, capabilities deciding, recent
  failures moving a provider back without excluding it, concurrency limit, a status with no secret and
  no network call, stand-ins only in test mode, settings for every adapter. The stand-in's job behaviour.
- `tests/test_ai_media.py` (23, through the API, stand-ins only, outside network blocked): **cache
  matrix** — Phase 5 identities unchanged; same provider/model/request hit; different provider, model,
  shape, duration miss (and each reused for its own request); three concurrent identical requests → one
  generation; force regenerate with the provider policy keeping every version; A fails → B makes it,
  provenance and cache entry name B; B's earlier result reused when A fails (no duplicate); a healthy A
  not replaced by B's result; AI off serving any candidate's result. **Fallback matrix** — A disabled or
  unsupported → B without trying A; timeouts retried twice then B; a job that ran out of time not
  resubmitted (cancelled) → B; rate limits honouring `Retry-After`, then B, and 429 + `Retry-After` when
  all are limited; a refused request neither retried nor sent to B (no asset, no file); all failing →
  502 naming both, nothing left behind, the run recorded; unusable output rejected → B; blank output
  flagged; explicit provider choices (no silent substitute, allowed fallback, unknown 400, unsupported
  422, invalid values 422). **Runs** — background generation followed to its result (owner only),
  immediate cache answer, cancellation (provider job cancelled), interrupted run reported. **Safety** —
  provider status without secrets; errors and logs without keys or prompts. **Router and review** —
  plans naming provider/model/backup, "no provider can be used" plans, cached plans with provenance and
  quality warnings; Visual Review keep/choose with new versions made by the backup, old version kept.
- Existing backend suites: only the **mocking seam** changed in `test_ai_cache.py`,
  `test_visual_review.py` and `test_visuals.py` — they now replace `server.ai_media.call_provider` (the
  single provider call) instead of the removed private `server._generate_ai_video` /
  `server._fetch_ai_image`, and set a dummy `GEMINI_API_KEY` (never sent) because Veo now counts as set up
  only with a key. No assertion changed; one test body swaps its image fake through the new seam.
- `tests/visuals.test.js` (+5): a background generation followed to the same answer (including a
  transient server error), failed/unknown runs and the waiting limit, immediate answers (cache hit,
  manual workflow, older server), provenance/state texts and stored plan fields, the providers client.
  The existing request-body test is unchanged (background mode is opt-in; the page opts in).
- `tests/ai_providers_browser_check.mjs` (12 checks, silent, real Chrome, throwaway server with only the
  stand-ins, the preferred one failing for videos and returning a blank picture): the providers panel
  (states, order, no secret, no provider called), the plan naming the provider, "Generate AI video" as a
  background run followed through status polls, the preferred provider failing (retried once) and the
  backup making it with "(backup AI provider)" shown, the library asset's provenance, a second lesson

  afterwards (temporarily unavailable, recent generation listed), no real provider registered, no
