### Results
Full regression (server restarted with Phase 8; all browser tests silent):

| Suite | Result |
|---|---|
| Static checks (6 inline scripts, 5 JS modules, 13 Python modules compile, browser checks parse) | pass |
| Backend unit (`unittest`; 125 earlier + 33 provider + 23 media layer) | **181/181** |
| Frontend unit (`node --test`; 85 earlier + 5 new) | **90/90** |
| Phase 1 mascot browser check | **27/27** |
| Phase 3 Asset Library browser check | **26/26** |
| Phase 4 visual router browser check | **24/24** |
| Phase 5 AI cache browser check | **11/11** |
| Phase 6 Visual Review browser check | **17/17** |
| Phase 8 AI providers browser check | **12/12** |
| Phase 2 export end-to-end | **30/30** |
| Export end-to-end with library assets | **35/35** |
| Phase 7 recording performance check (1280×720) | **18/18** (0 Aadhi stalls, 28.6 fps delivered) |

The backend suite was run once more after the last change (HTTP 402 handling): 181/181.

**Live validation.** One manual call through the Pollinations adapter (the only real provider usable
here: no keys, no GPU), isolated from the project database: the provider answered HTTP 500, and a raw
request to the same endpoint answered **HTTP 402 Payment Required** (x402, "Pollinations legacy image",
0.01 USDC per image). The adapter reached the real API and normalized the failure (`unavailable` / now
`auth` for 402); no image was produced. No other real provider was called. Everything else — image and
video generation, cache hits and misses, fallback, retries, timeouts, rate limits, invalid and blank
output, background runs, cancellation, provenance, the Asset Library, the router, Visual Review, and
export afterwards — was exercised with the local stand-in providers.

AI provider calls during automated testing: **none** (backend: stand-ins or mocked HTTP/SDK with outside
network connections failing the test; browser: throwaway servers where only the stand-ins exist).

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- The Veo adapter's download: the old code used `generated_video.video.name`, which the installed SDK
  does not have; now `client.files.download(file=video)` (the SDK's documented call).
- The router planned AI images "via pollinations" whatever the server used; the media layer decides now.
- **Visual Review notes never displayed (Phase 6 bug):** the detail was filled with a plain DOM `append`,
  which turned the notes list into the text "[object HTMLParagraphElement]" (and a missing part into
  "null"); the "scene changed", error and new quality notes now render. Found by the Phase 8 browser check.
- First version of the recent-failure rule excluded a provider for 60 s even when it was the only one
  (every image failed for a minute after one error); now it only moves the provider back.
- Sanitizing missed `token=` values outside URL queries; covered now.
- HTTP 402 (a provider asking for payment) was first treated as a refused request (no fallback); it is
  now `auth`: not retried, the next provider is tried, and the provider is moved back for
  `AI_PROVIDER_AUTH_COOLDOWN` (600 s) so working providers go first and the panel shows why.

### Known limitations
- **Pollinations now requires payment** (HTTP 402, observed live): until a paid or authenticated
  Pollinations access is set up (an adapter for its current API can be added with the guide above once
  its documentation and a key are at hand — not written here, the API was not verified), automatic AI
  images need `GEMINI_IMAGE_ENABLED=1` with a `GEMINI_API_KEY`, and the layer falls back to it; without
  it, image requests fail cleanly (and the subject-image fallback fails the same way).
- No real provider produced media during this phase (no keys here, no GPU; Pollinations refused): Veo and
  Gemini image are implemented against the installed SDK and the official documentation and tested with
  mocked clients only; the whole layer was exercised end to end with the local stand-ins. The project's Veo model (`veo-2.0-generate-001`) is no longer in Google's model list:
  set `VEO_MODEL` to a model the key can use (e.g. a Veo 3.1 code) after checking access.
- Background runs live in the server process: after a restart they are reported interrupted, not
  resumed (Phase 9). The runs table is not cleaned up (Phase 9).
- Images are generated while the request waits (they finish in seconds); only videos use background runs.
- Recent-failure memory and concurrency limits are per process.
- Blank detection is a heuristic (one flat colour); artistic quality is judged in Visual Review.
- `/get-image` (legacy, unauthenticated) still calls Pollinations directly, outside the layer.

### Deferred to Later Phases
- **Phase 9:** resuming or re-attaching interrupted generations, cleaning up runs and unused generated
  assets, persistent provider health.
- **Phase 10–20:** untouched. The interface leaves room for them: `inputs` (image/reference inputs for
  image-to-video and character consistency), `audio` and new media types (presenter, voice), request
  purpose and scene fields (scene composer, visual director), job providers with polling and
  cancellation (long cinematic renders), and provenance on every asset (consistency checks).

### Final status
**COMPLETE (with the provider limitations above).** The provider interface, the registry and its
selection, capabilities, normalized requests/results/errors, configuration, preference, controlled fallback,
bounded retries, rate-limit handling, job polling, background runs, output validation, provenance and the
provider status UI are in place; the Phase 5 cache is extended (not duplicated), generated media stays in
the Phase 3 library, the router names no provider, Visual Review decisions stay authoritative. Every
Phase 1–8 suite passes in full. Real media could not be generated with a real provider here (no keys;
Pollinations now requires payment) — stated, not hidden. Nothing was committed, pushed or deployed.

---

## Phase 9 — Recovery, Resumability & Fault-Tolerant Generation

### Objective
When something is interrupted — server restart or crash, browser refresh or closure, network loss,
provider timeout or failure — Aadhi keeps what was already done and continues from the right point:
never a duplicate (possibly paid) AI generation because local state was lost, never a lost result.

### Baseline (start of Phase 9)
Backend 181/181, frontend 90/90, Phase 1 27/27, Phase 3 26/26, Phase 4 24/24, Phase 5 11/11, Phase 6 17/17,
Phase 8 12/12, Phase 2 export 30/30, export with library assets 35/35, Phase 7 18/18.

### Audit (before Phase 9, from the code)
- `ai_generation_runs` (Phase 8) recorded one request, but **not the request itself** (prompt, options) —
  nothing could be resumed; attempts only as JSON in `detail`; the provider job id written through a
  progress callback without any ownership check; background work only in an in-memory task table; state
  changes as plain ORM writes (two workers could both finish a run); after a restart a run was only
  *reported* interrupted.
- **Billing flaw found:** a network error while *polling* a submitted provider job was classified as a
  retryable failure, and the retry **submitted a new provider job** (a second paid generation).
- **Another:** the job adapter cancelled the provider's job whenever its task was cancelled — including
  when the server was just shutting down, so a restart would have thrown away paid work.
- Exports: chunked upload resume already survived a server restart (the `.part` file and the offset);
  a crash while finishing (`/complete`) left the export `PROCESSING` for 24 h (the request, incl. the
  timeline for subtitles/chapters, was not kept); an interrupted MP4 conversion stayed `processing`.
- The page's AI video generation was tied to the page (a refresh showed "Generate" again although the
  server was still working); export preparation generated scene by scene from the page (closing it
  stopped the remaining scenes).

### Architecture
```
 Generation request (page, lesson batch, export preparation)
        │ cache hit / AI off / manual workflow → answered at once (no run)
        ▼
 ai_generation_runs  ── identical active request? → attach to it (no second run)
   request (JSON), request_hash, scene, lease_owner + lease_expires_at, status (state machine)
        │ worker owns it (lease, heartbeat renews it)
        ▼
 ai_generation_attempts (one per provider call, history kept)
   starting ──► submitted (provider job id + handle saved AT ONCE) ──► downloading ──► registering ──► completed
        │                                   │                                              │
        │                     server crash / kill / restart                                 │
        ▼                                   ▼                                              ▼
 ┌──────────────────────── Recovery manager (server start, then every AI_RECOVERY_INTERVAL) ───────────────┐
 │ due runs: active, lease expired (or queued), backoff over — claimed atomically, AI_JOB_CONCURRENCY at a time │
 │   asset already registered ──► finalize (cache entry, run completed)                                      │
 │   output on disk          ──► validate + register (idempotent) ──► finalize                               │
 │   provider job saved      ──► poll the SAME job ──► done: fetch ─► register ─► finalize                   │
 │                                  job lost / cancelled by provider: free → try again · paid → NEEDS ATTENTION │
 │                                  job failed: next provider (Phase 8 fallback) · temporary trouble: back off │
 │   provider call without a saved answer: free → try again · paid → NEEDS ATTENTION (no duplicate)           │
 │   cancel requested        ──► cancel the provider's job where supported ──► cancelled                     │
 │   no attempt yet (queued) ──► Visual Review still wants it? ─► cache first ─► generate                    │
 └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        ▼
 Library asset (Phase 3) ─► cache entry (Phase 5) ─► lesson plan of the scene updated ─► Visual Review / export
```
Modules: `ai_runs.py` (state machine, guarded transitions, leases, attempt history), `ai_media.py`
(execution and recovery of runs), `ai_recovery.py` (the manager), `ai_providers.py` (recoverable job
providers), `exports.py` (`recover_exports`), `visuals.py` (`store_scene_plans`, `still_wanted`).

### State machine
| Run state | Meaning | Set by | Terminal | Recovery | New provider request allowed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `queued` | waiting for a worker (batch, deferred after trouble, user retry) | request / batch / worker (backoff) / user | no | the manager starts it | yes (after the cache and Visual Review checks) |
| `running` | a worker owns it (lease) | worker | no | after the lease expires | only for a new attempt the worker decides |
| `recovering` | the manager took over an interrupted run | manager | no | continues the open attempt | only when safe (see classification) |
| `cancel_requested` | the user asked to stop | user | no | the owner or the manager finishes the cancel | no |
| `completed` | usable media is a library asset | owner | yes | — | no |
| `failed` | nothing usable could be made | owner | yes | — | no |
| `cancelled` | stopped on request, or no longer wanted (Visual Review) | owner / user (queued) / manager | yes | never restarts by itself | no |
| `needs_attention` | continuing could duplicate a paid generation, the provider's state is unknown, or it was interrupted too often | owner / manager | until a person acts | — | only after the user chooses **Retry** (`allow_duplicate`) |
Allowed transitions are listed in `ai_runs.TRANSITIONS`; any other is refused (an assertion in tests).
Attempt states: `starting`, `submitted`, `downloading`, `registering`, `completed`, `failed`, `lost`
(the provider no longer has the job, or nothing was billed), `ambiguous` (a paid request may have been
accepted but cannot be found), `cancelled`.

### Durable data (database changes)
- `ai_generation_runs` gains `request` (the provider-independent request as JSON), `request_hash`
  (the logical request: user, what, options, lesson, scene, slot, forced), `project_id`, `scene_index`,
  `slot`, `batch_id`, `lease_owner`, `lease_expires_at`, `recovery_count`, `cancel_requested_at`,
  `not_before`, `last_checked_at`, `allow_duplicate`; indexes `(status, lease_expires_at)`,
  `(scope_key, request_hash)`, `(project_id, scene_index)`, `(batch_id)`.
- New table `ai_generation_attempts`: run, number, provider, model, generation hash, state, provider
  job id and **job handle** (JSON, no secrets), output path, asset, error category/message, times
  recovered, owner, detail (identity, fallback, retries, provider's cancel answer, how it was
  recovered), timestamps.
- **Migration:** the project only ever ran `create_all` (new tables, never new columns). A small
  additive step `database.ensure_schema` adds missing columns (`ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN`) and named
  indexes at startup; tested on a Phase 8 table with data, and applied to this installation's
  `projects.db` (13 columns added, all 74 lessons kept; a copy was taken first).

### Requests, attempts, idempotency
- **Request vs attempt:** a run is the logical request; every provider call is an attempt row (never
  overwritten). **Resume** = continue the same attempt's provider job; **retry** = a new attempt after a
  failure (only when safe); **regenerate** = a new run with `force_regenerate` (a new media version).
- **Identical requests** (same `request_hash`) while one is active attach to it — the page after a
  refresh, a second click, the export preparation — so the work is done once (the waiting request
  reports the media as reused, as in Phase 5).
- **Provider idempotency keys:** none of the integrated providers documents one (Veo, Gemini image,
  Pollinations, LTX), so none is sent — none is invented. Safety comes from saving the job handle the
  moment the provider accepted it, and from treating an unconfirmed paid submission as needs attention.
- **Submission safety:** attempt `starting` is written *before* the provider call; the job id and handle
  are written *immediately* after acceptance (before any polling). A crash in between leaves an
  unknown submission: for a paid provider → `needs_attention` ("No duplicate generation was started
  automatically"); for a free one → tried again.

### Leases, heartbeats, ownership
A worker owns a run through a lease (`AI_JOB_LEASE_SECONDS`, 60) renewed by its heartbeat every
`AI_JOB_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS` (15): a few missed heartbeats, not one delay, make a run stale. Claiming is
one conditional UPDATE (of two workers exactly one succeeds); every later write checks the owner — a
worker that lost its lease (it stalled and recovery took over) stops at its next write (`LeaseLost`)
and cannot overwrite the new owner. A stopping server expires its own leases at once, so the next start
continues its runs without waiting.

### Recovery manager
Started with the server (the app serves requests meanwhile), then every `AI_RECOVERY_INTERVAL` (15 s)
or at once when work is queued. One indexed query finds due runs; each is claimed atomically; at most
`AI_JOB_CONCURRENCY` (2) run at a time, and the Phase 8 per-provider limits still apply. A run
interrupted `AI_RECOVERY_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (3) times stops as needs attention. Temporary provider trouble
during recovery re-queues the run with growing waits (30 s, 60 s, 120 s … up to 15 min, bounded by the
same maximum) instead of retrying everything at once — no retry storm after a restart. The
classification table is in the architecture diagram above; nothing is regenerated without a decision
made from the run's saved state.

### Provider recovery (Phase 8 interface extended)
`JobProvider` gains `recoverable`, `job_handle(job)`, `restore_job(handle)`, `resume(handle, …)` (polls
the same job, then fetches — never submits), `cancel_supported` and `cancel_handle(handle)`; a new error
category `lost` (the provider no longer knows the job). **Veo:** the handle is the operation name;
`restore_job` rebuilds the operation for `operations.get`; a 404 → `lost`; cancelling is declared
unsupported (not verified). **Stand-ins:** jobs are files in `AI_FAKE_STATE_DIR` (they survive the
server), with modes for slow, failed-on-provider (`jobfail`), vanished (`vanish`), delayed
(`<NAME>_JOB_SECONDS`) and billed (`<NAME>_PAID`). **Pollinations, Gemini image, LTX** answer in one
call: nothing to reattach to — an interrupted call of a paid one (Gemini image) needs attention, of a
free one is repeated. Slow-provider safety (Phase 8) is kept: a job that ran out of time is never
resubmitted, and recovery prefers polling the existing job over creating another.

### Cache, assets, finalization
- **Cache first:** before any new attempt (queued run, recovered run without a saved job) the Phase 5
  cache is consulted; a result made meanwhile by another request answers the run (`cache_hit`).
- **Locks:** the run that is recovered adopts the generation lock of its identity (its lease proves it
  owns that generation), so identical requests keep waiting for it rather than generating; abandoned
  lock rows are swept.
- **Finalization is idempotent:** validate (the same checks as Phase 8 — recovered output is never
  trusted), register (a video is adopted in place: the same file is the same asset; an image is kept in a
  private work folder until the library has it by content, then removed), save the asset on the attempt,
  record the cache entry once (`record_once`), complete the run (guarded). Called again after a crash at
  any step it finds what was done: no second asset, cache entry, reference or history row.
- No false completion: a run is completed only after the media passed validation and is a library asset.

### Lessons: batches, plans, Visual Review
- `POST /api/ai-media/lessons/{project_id}/generate` queues every AI visual a saved lesson still needs
  (its visual plan first: Visual Review decisions, library, cache); the manager's workers make them one
  by one on the server. Each finished run updates that scene's stored plan at once (`store_scene_plans`,
  as the review does) with its asset reference. After a restart, finished scenes stay, the interrupted
  one is resumed, the rest continue; asking again queues nothing ("already available").
- Export preparation (AI videos in "Auto: images + videos") uses the batch: closing the page no longer
  stops the remaining scenes, and an export retried later finds them done.
- Plans are reused, never recomputed needlessly: the router's rules keep valid stored plans (Phase 4).
- **Visual Review stays authoritative:** before anything is generated without the user (recovery,
  batch), `still_wanted` checks the scene: removed → not generated; another visual chosen → not
  generated (unless the run is a New AI Version the user asked for); the scene's request changed → not
  generated. Such runs end `cancelled` with the reason; decisions are never undone.

### Browser
- The browser is not the source of truth: generations run on the server. The page sends which scene a
  generation is for; on load it fetches the lesson's active runs and, for a scene still being generated,
  shows "Still generating on the server…" and follows the run (states in words: queued, generating,
  recovering after a restart — no invented percentages).
- Network loss: following a run tolerates failed polls (it keeps waiting); nothing is restarted.
- Closing the browser: the server finishes; on return the lesson's plan already holds the result.
- The AI providers panel lists generations in progress and needing attention with the explanation and
  **Retry / Dismiss / Cancel**, and recent ones (with "recovered after an interruption").

### Exports
- Upload resume across a server restart: already correct (unchanged).
- Finishing: `/complete` keeps its request next to the upload (`tmp/<id>.complete.json`). At startup,
  exports left `PROCESSING` are finished from what exists — the file already moved into place, or the
  upload still waiting — without uploading or recording again; if neither exists, the export fails with
  a clear message. MP4 copies left `pending`/`processing` are made again. Idempotent; the kept request
  is removed when done (and by the hourly housekeeping).

### Cancellation
Queued → cancelled at once (nothing sent). Running → `cancel_requested`; the owning worker (this
process at once, another process via its heartbeat, or the manager if the owner died) cancels the
provider's job where the provider supports it and records the provider's answer (`cancelled`,
`failed`, `unsupported` — "it may still finish and bill on its side", `not_submitted`); cancelled runs
never restart by themselves.

### Failure handling and error categories
Every outcome is chosen deliberately: resume (job saved), retry (free provider, or transient before
submission), fallback (job failed on the provider, unsupported, invalid output — Phase 8 policy),
back off (temporary trouble during recovery), cancel (requested or no longer wanted), fail (refused,
nothing left), needs attention (`ambiguous_submission`, `job_lost`, `provider_unknown`, `interrupted`
too often). Categories stored on runs/attempts: the Phase 8 ones plus `lost`, `ambiguous_submission`,
`job_lost`, `interrupted`, `not_wanted`, `provider_unknown`.

### Observability and cleanup
Structured `[AI MEDIA]` lines: `recovery_started`, `recovery_claim` / `queue_claim` (previous state,
attempt state, job), `recovering`, `resuming_job`, `job_saved`, `recovered` (how), `attached`,
`deferred`, `needs_attention`, `lease_lost`, `not_wanted`, `cancelled`, `cleanup` — run ids as
correlation ids, never prompts or secrets. Each run's view answers why it recovered / retried / stopped,
which job was recovered and which asset was produced (`history`, `explanation`). Finished runs
(completed, failed, cancelled) older than `AI_RUN_RETENTION_DAYS` (90) are removed with their attempts
(assets keep their provenance in their own details); active and needs-attention runs are never removed.

### Security
Runs, their history, cancel and resolve are scoped to their owner (another user gets 404); the
recovery workers act on the owner's behalf only; recovered output goes through the same validation and
library registration; logs and views stay sanitized.

### Configuration
`AI_RECOVERY_ENABLED` (1), `AI_RECOVERY_INTERVAL` (15 s), `AI_JOB_LEASE_SECONDS` (60),
`AI_JOB_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS` (15), `AI_JOB_CONCURRENCY` (2), `AI_RECOVERY_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (3),
`AI_RUN_RETENTION_DAYS` (90) — in `.env.example`. Test-only: `AI_TEST_CRASH_AT` (stand-ins only: the
process exits at `before_job_saved`, `after_job_saved`, `after_download`, `after_register`,
`after_cache`), `AI_FAKE_STATE_DIR`, `<NAME>_JOB_SECONDS`, `<NAME>_PAID`.

### API
| Endpoint | Change |
|---|---|
| `POST /generate-ai-video`, `/generate-ai-image` | durable runs; identical active requests attach; `project_id` / `scene_index` / `slot` tie a generation to its scene |
| `GET /api/ai-media/runs` | filters `project_id`, `active`, `batch_id` |
| `GET /api/ai-media/runs/{id}` | adds `state_label`, `recovery_count`, `history` (attempts), `explanation`, `actions`, `prompt`, scene |
| `POST /api/ai-media/runs/{id}/cancel` | queued: cancelled at once; running: persisted cancel request (recoverable) |
| `POST /api/ai-media/runs/{id}/resolve` (new) | `retry` / `dismiss` a run that needs attention |
| `POST /api/ai-media/lessons/{project_id}/generate` (new) | lesson batch |
| `GET /api/ai-media/providers` | adds `generations` (active / recovering / needing attention) |

### Files changed
- New: `ai_runs.py`, `ai_recovery.py`, `tests/test_ai_recovery.py`, `tests/ai_recovery_browser_check.mjs`.
- Modified: `ai_media.py` (durable execution and recovery), `ai_providers.py` (recoverable jobs, `lost`,
  stand-in job store), `ai_cache.py` (`record_once`, `adopt_lock`, `sweep_locks`), `models.py`,
  `database.py` (`ensure_schema`), `server.py`, `visuals.py`, `exports.py`, `visuals.js`, `index.html`,
  `.env.example`, `tests/backend_env.py` (`AI_RECOVERY_ENABLED=0`: recovery tests run the manager
  themselves), `tests/visuals.test.js`.

### Tests
- `tests/test_ai_recovery.py` (23; a crash = the worker stopped at a crash point with nothing tidied up,
  its lease left to expire, then a *new* media service and manager continue from the database alone;
  stand-ins only, outside network blocked): guarded state changes and exclusive leases (two claims → one
  winner; a stalled worker can neither renew, write nor finish; completed and failed never both); the
  schema step on a Phase 8 table with data (columns added, row kept, indexes, second run adds nothing);
  **restart while the provider job runs** → the same job resumed, no second job, a normal asset (640×360,
  4 s), the explanation; **after the download** → the file registered without fetching again; **during /
  after registration and after the cache entry** → one asset, one cache entry, finalizing again finds
  nothing to do; images likewise (work file removed); **ambiguous paid submission** → needs attention,
  no resubmission, private to its owner, then Retry → generated once with the history kept; **ambiguous
  free submission** → tried again; **a job the provider lost** → paid: needs attention (`job_lost`),
  free: tried again; **a job that failed on the provider** → the backup makes it, the first never asked
  again; **a hiccup while following a job** → the same job followed again (one submission); **cache
  first** (made meanwhile elsewhere → cache hit, no job); **an identical request attaches** to the
  interrupted run; **two managers** take a run once; **cancel while the worker is gone** → the
  provider's job cancelled, never restarted; **cancel while queued** → nothing sent; **interrupted too
  often** → needs attention; **temporary trouble during recovery** → deferred with backoff, not retried
  at once; **lesson batch across a restart** → the finished scene untouched, the interrupted one resumed,
  the rest made (two new jobs in all), every scene's plan stored, asking again queues nothing; **Visual
  Review** → removed / changed scenes not generated (reasons recorded), the untouched one made, a New AI
  Version still made; housekeeping (old finished runs and their attempts removed, active and
  needs-attention kept, abandoned locks swept); privacy; **export finishing after a restart** (file
  already moved / still uploaded → completed with subtitles, chapters and a pending MP4; nothing left →
  failed; a second pass finishes nothing twice).
- Existing suites unchanged in their assertions; the backend test environment turns the background
  recovery loop off (`AI_RECOVERY_ENABLED=0`) because the recovery tests drive the manager themselves.
- `tests/visuals.test.js` (+2): a generation carrying its scene (body), `recovering` followed rather than
  treated as an error, the runs / cancel / resolve / batch calls; a run needing attention ends the wait
  with its explanation; run states in words.
- `tests/ai_recovery_browser_check.mjs` (14 checks, silent, real Chrome, **real server processes killed
  (`taskkill /F`) or crashing (`os._exit` at a crash point) and started again** on the same database and
  provider-job store; every provider job is a file, so duplicates are counted): the page's generation
  saved with its provider job; after a page refresh the page finds and follows it; the server killed
  mid-job and restarted → the same job recovered (no second job), the page reconnects and plays the
  video, the asset is normal (size, duration, provenance), Visual Review shows it; a crash after the
  provider finished → registered, no new job; a lesson batch with the server killed during the second
  scene → finished scene untouched, interrupted one resumed, three jobs in all, every scene in the saved
  lesson; a job failing on the provider while the server is down → the backup makes it; a paid provider
  crashing before its answer was saved → needs attention, no duplicate, the panel explains it, Retry
  makes it once; no console errors outside the moments the server was down.

### Results
Full regression (server restarted with Phase 9, which migrated its database; all browser tests silent):

| Suite | Result |
|---|---|
| Static checks (6 inline scripts, 5 JS modules, 15 Python modules compile, browser checks parse) | pass |
| Backend unit (`unittest`; 181 earlier + 23 new) | **204/204** |
| Frontend unit (`node --test`; 90 earlier + 2 new) | **92/92** |
| Phase 1 mascot browser check | **27/27** |
| Phase 3 Asset Library browser check | **26/26** |
| Phase 4 visual router browser check | **24/24** |
| Phase 5 AI cache browser check | **11/11** (after the fix below; 10/11 in the first pass) |
| Phase 6 Visual Review browser check | **17/17** |
| Phase 8 AI providers browser check | **12/12** |
| Phase 9 recovery check (real server processes killed / crashed and restarted) | **14/14** |
| Phase 2 export end-to-end | **30/30** |
| Export end-to-end with library assets | **35/35** |
| Phase 7 recording performance check (1280×720) | **18/18** (0 Aadhi stalls, 28.5 fps delivered) |

The first regression pass caught one real conflict: the new "update the lesson when a generation
finishes" step also ran for **New AI Version** requests and re-pointed the saved lesson to the new
version, removing the lesson's reference to the old one — against the Phase 5 guarantee that a forced
regeneration leaves the earlier version and its references exactly as they were (Phase 5 check "the
earlier version is kept … and its lesson reference untouched"). The step now fills only a scene slot that
has no visual yet and never runs for a forced version; afterwards the backend suite (204/204), the
Phase 5 (11/11), Phase 6 (17/17) and Phase 9 (14/14) checks were run again and pass.

**Recovery simulations actually performed** (tests/ai_recovery_browser_check.mjs, real processes):
1. AI video started in the page → page refreshed (reattached) → server killed mid-job → restarted: the
   same provider job recovered, no second job, the page reconnected and played the video, the asset was
   registered, Visual Review showed it.
2. Server crashed (process exit) after the provider finished, before registration → restarted: the
   result registered, no new job.
3. Lesson batch of three scenes, server killed during the second → restarted: the finished scene
   untouched, the interrupted one resumed (same job), the last made; three jobs in all; the saved lesson
   holds every scene's video.
4. (Browser refresh — part of 1.)
5. A provider job failed on the provider's side while the server was down → the backup provider made it,
   the failed provider was never asked again.
6. Paid provider, server crashed before its answer was saved → needs attention, no duplicate job; the
   providers panel explained it; Retry generated it once.
In-process crash tests (tests/test_ai_recovery.py) add every other crash point (after registration,
after the cache entry, images), lost and failed jobs, two recovery workers, stale workers, cancellation
before and after a restart, backoff, Visual Review, the schema step and export finishing.

**Provider testing:** real providers — none (no keys here; Pollinations requires payment, Phase 8);
mocked — the Veo recovery path (operation restored by name) is covered only by the Phase 8 mocked
client tests of polling/fetching; stand-in providers — everything above.

AI provider calls during testing: **none**.

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- A network error while following a submitted provider job resubmitted the job (Phase 8): now the same
  job is followed again (`resume`), never a second submission.
- The job adapter cancelled the provider's job on any task cancellation, including a server shutdown:
  now only a user's cancel cancels a provider job; a stopping server hands its runs to recovery.
- The state machine's guard caught two wrong transitions while the tests were written (a queued run
  cannot go straight to needs attention; a run being cancelled cannot be re-queued); fixed.
- A job that failed on the provider's side after a restart was first deferred like a temporary problem;
  it now goes to the next provider (the Phase 8 fallback), and only temporary trouble is deferred.
- A recovered image left its private work file behind; removed now.
- The first version of the lesson update after a finished generation also replaced a visual the lesson
  already used — including after a **New AI Version**, dropping the lesson's reference to the earlier
  version (caught by the Phase 5 browser check). It now only fills a slot that has no visual yet and
  never acts for a forced version; the user applies a new version (page or Visual Review) as before.

### Known limitations
- **No real provider recovery was exercised:** no provider keys here (and Pollinations requires payment,
  Phase 8). Veo recovery (restore by operation name, 404 → lost) is implemented against the installed
  SDK and tested only with mocked clients; everything else was exercised with the stand-ins, including
  real server processes killed and restarted.
- A recording that is still being uploaded lives in the page's memory: a browser **refresh during the
  upload** loses it (the server keeps the uploaded part; only the same page can resume). Keeping the
  recording in browser storage was not implemented.
- After a crash, recovery waits for the lease to expire (up to `AI_JOB_LEASE_SECONDS`) before taking a
  run over (a graceful stop hands runs over at once).
- A waiting (non-background) image request whose server restarts gets a connection error; its run is
  recovered and the next identical request is answered from the cache.
- Recent-failure memory and per-provider concurrency limits are per process (as in Phase 8).
- Veo and the other single-call providers cannot be cancelled on the provider's side (declared, not faked).

### Deferred to Later Phases
- **Phase 10:** Manim renders are not durable runs yet; the run/attempt/lease model and the manager are
  generic enough to take them (a new job kind), not implemented.
- **Phases 11–20:** untouched; presenter, cinematic and composition jobs can use the same persistent
  job → state → checkpoint (attempt) → recover → finalize pattern.

### Final status
**COMPLETE (recovery verified with the stand-in providers; no real provider available here).**
Durable runs with their request, an explicit state machine with guarded transitions, worker leases and
heartbeats, attempt history with the provider job saved at submission, the recovery manager (startup and
periodic, concurrency- and backoff-limited), provider recovery (resume the same job, never resubmit),
billing safety (needs attention instead of a possible duplicate paid generation), idempotent
finalization (no duplicate asset, cache entry or reference), attaching identical requests, cache-first
recovery, lesson batches that continue across restarts, Visual Review preserved, recoverable
cancellation, export finishing and MP4 recovery, page reattachment after a refresh, and the providers
panel's generations — all tested; every Phase 1–9 suite passes in full. Nothing was committed, pushed or
deployed.

---

## Phase 10 — Secure Manim Sandbox

### 1. Purpose
Scenes carry Manim code written by an AI (or pasted by a user). Before Phase 10, `/render` ran it with
`manim -qm` **inside the server's environment**: the repository as working directory, a copy of the
server's environment (API keys, JWT secret, database URL), full network, the user's files, no memory or
process limits, a 60 s timeout that did not work (it caught the wrong exception), and a cache that trusted
any file named after the code. Phase 10 treats that code as untrusted: it runs only in an isolated
sandbox, as a durable Phase 9 job, and its video becomes a normal library asset. If no secure runtime
exists, rendering answers **"Secure Manim execution is unavailable in this deployment."**; there is no
fallback to unsafe execution.

Baseline at the start: backend 204/204, frontend 92/92, Phase 1 27/27, Phase 3 26/26, Phase 4 24/24,
Phase 5 11/11, Phase 6 17/17, Phase 8 12/12, Phase 9 14/14, Phase 2 export 30/30, export with library
assets 35/35, Phase 7 18/18.

### 2. Threat model
The code may be malicious: a prompt injection in lesson material, a compromised or careless model, or a
user attacking the server or other users. It may try to:
- **read** secrets (`.env`, `projects.db`, source code, other users' media, SSH keys, the user profile);
- **write** outside its folder (plant code in the repository or in site-packages, fill the disk);
- **reach the network** (exfiltrate, call the app's own API on localhost, cloud metadata at
  169.254.169.254, private networks);
- **start processes** (shells, fork bombs) or escape Python (eval/exec, ctypes, importlib tricks,
  `__subclasses__` walks);
- **exhaust resources** (memory, CPU, infinite loops, huge output, endless frames, 4K/120 fps);
- **outlive its job** (orphan processes after a timeout, a cancel or a server crash).

Out of scope: kernel exploits and hypervisor escapes. The OS boundary is trusted, as documented below.

### 3. Architecture
```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Aadhi backend (FastAPI)                   │
│  page /render · lesson plan (Visual Router)│
│  POST /render {code, profile, lesson slot} │
└────────────────────┬─────────────────────┘
                     │ static checks (manim_security): size, scenes, AST allowlist → 422 early
                     │ secure runtime available? no → 503 "unavailable" (no fallback)
                     │ cache: same code + Manim version + profile rendered before → that asset
                     ▼
        Persistent job — Phase 9 engine (manim_jobs.ManimService)
        ai_generation_runs (kind "manim") · attempts · lease + heartbeat · recovery manager
        attempt: starting → rendering → registering → completed
                     │ one sandbox per attempt; MANIM_MAX_CONCURRENT / MANIM_MAX_PER_USER
                     ▼
   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │ SECURE SANDBOX (manim_sandbox)                  │
   │ Windows: AppContainer + Job Object              │
   │ (Docker: --network none --read-only --user …)   │
   │  · low-integrity, non-admin token; no user SIDs │
   │  · no network (internet, LAN, loopback, IMDS)   │
   │  · files: its own workspace (per-job capability)│
   │    + read/execute of the Python runtime only    │
   │  · empty environment (no secrets)               │
   │  · memory, CPU rate + CPU time, 1 process,      │
   │    wall timeout, workspace/output size          │
   │  · kill-on-close: dies with its server          │
   │  runner.py (trusted bootstrap: profile, frame   │
   │  counter, audit hook) → Manim → scene.mp4       │
   └───────────────────────┬───────────────────────┘
                           ▼
        Output validation (manim_jobs.validate): a real video (ffprobe), the profile's size,
        within duration and size limits; no link followed; copied out, workspace deleted
                           ▼
        Asset Library (adopted in place, idempotent) + AI cache entry (once)
                           ▼
        Lesson plan (scene slot) · Visual Review (decisions first) · playback
                           ▼
        Export (reuses the asset; no second render)
```

Modules (nothing of the sandbox is in `server.py`):
| Module | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `manim_security.py` | Security validation and configuration: render profiles and hard maximums, static checks (AST allowlist), code normalization, fingerprint |
| `manim_sandbox.py` | The sandbox: runtime detection, Windows AppContainer runtime, Docker runtime, workspaces, health check, orphan sweep, failure categories and messages |
| `manim_runner.py` | The trusted bootstrap that runs *inside* the sandbox (copied into each workspace) |
| `manim_jobs.py` | Job integration: Phase 9 runs/attempts/leases/recovery, cache, output validation, asset registration, cancel, concurrency |

### 4. Sandbox mechanism
**Tested runtime: Windows AppContainer + Job Object** (`WindowsAppContainerRuntime`). This is the
development machine's only option: Windows 11, no Docker, no WSL. It uses real OS isolation (the same
mechanism that sandboxes Edge/Chrome renderer and UWP processes), not a plain subprocess:
- An AppContainer profile `AadhiEduEngine.ManimSandbox` gives the process a **low-integrity AppContainer
  token**. Every access check then also requires a grant to the AppContainer (or one of its capabilities).
  The user's own files, the repository and other users' media grant nothing to it, so they are denied
  even though the process runs under the same Windows account.
- Each job gets a **per-job capability SID** (`DeriveCapabilitySidsFromName("aadhiManimJob<id>")`). Only
  that job's workspace is granted to it (Modify), so one job cannot see another's.
- The process starts **suspended**, is placed in a **Job Object**, and only then resumes. The Job Object
  sets: active process limit (1), job/process memory limit, CPU time limit, a hard CPU rate cap, UI
  restrictions (no desktop, clipboard, global atoms, system settings), die-on-unhandled-exception and
  **kill-on-job-close**. When the server process dies, for any reason, its job handle closes and Windows
  kills every process in the job.
- The **environment block is built from scratch**: SYSTEMROOT, WINDIR, PATH (Python + System32), TEMP/TMP
  and USERPROFILE/HOME/APPDATA/LOCALAPPDATA pointing into the workspace, and thread counts of 1. No
  variable of the server's is inherited, so no API key, JWT secret or database URL.
- The command is `python -I -S -B runner.py job.json`: isolated mode, no site hooks, no bytecode writes.

**Docker runtime** (`DockerRuntime`, selected by `MANIM_SANDBOX_RUNTIME=docker` or `auto` where Windows
isn't available). It is implemented and its command line is unit-tested, **but it was not run: there is
no Docker here.** Flags: `--network none --read-only --tmpfs /tmp:rw,noexec,nosuid --user 10001:10001
--cap-drop ALL --security-opt no-new-privileges --pids-limit --memory/--memory-swap --cpus`, with only the
job workspace mounted and no environment passed. The image is defined in `sandbox/Dockerfile.manim`
(manim 0.21.0, no pip, non-root), also untested.

**Selection** (`ManimSandbox.runtime()`): `MANIM_SANDBOX_ENABLED=0` → unavailable;
`MANIM_SANDBOX_RUNTIME` = `auto` | `windows-appcontainer` | `docker`, and any other value is refused.
Each runtime checks itself (APIs present, profile creatable, Docker daemon and image present). With none
available, the reason is logged and the API answers 503 "Secure Manim execution is unavailable in this
deployment." The rest of the application keeps working.

**Defence in depth** (not the boundary; each is tested *bypassed* to prove the OS refuses on its own):
1. **Static checks** (`manim_security.check_source`): source size, a scene count, syntax, an **import
   allowlist** (manim, math, numpy, random, itertools, … — no os/sys/subprocess/socket/ctypes/importlib/
   winreg/pathlib), forbidden calls (eval, exec, compile, `__import__`, open, globals, …), forbidden
   attributes (system, popen, numpy load/save/tofile/memmap, …), dunder access. They give an early,
   friendly 422 (`unsafe_code`) without starting anything.
2. **Audit hook** in the runner (`sys.addaudithook`, which cannot be removed): refuses process creation,
   sockets, ctypes/dlopen, registry and webbrowser events, and any Python file open or folder listing
   outside the workspace and the Python runtime.
3. **Runner limits**: the profile's resolution, fps and background are forced after the scene module
   is imported. Every frame written is counted against the frame limit (still waits count their repeated
   frames). A change of resolution or fps during the render is stopped before the frame is written.

### 5. Filesystem isolation
- Workspace per job, under `MANIM_SANDBOX_DIR` (default `%LOCALAPPDATA%\AadhiEduEngine\manim-sandbox`):
  `jobs/job-<id>/{source,output,media,temp,home,logs}` plus a marker file. Only this folder is writable,
  and only by this job's capability.
- Read/execute only: the Python installation (`sys.base_prefix`) and the virtualenv (for manim's
  packages), granted once to the AppContainer SID (`(OI)(CI)(RX)`). Windows' own system folders stay
  readable to every AppContainer (DLLs, fonts), as for any sandboxed app.
- Denied, verified: `.env`, `projects.db`, `server.py`, listing the repository, the user profile, a
  canary file, other jobs' folders, `../` traversal, absolute paths, writing into site-packages or the
  repository, creating links, and reading through a junction planted in the workspace.
- The output is accepted only as `output/scene.mp4`, a regular file inside the workspace (no link, no
  junction, containment checked). It is copied to `static_videos/manim_<fp16>_<attempt8>.mp4` and the
  workspace is deleted: source, temp files, logs and partial output are never kept.
- Error text returned to the page has workspace and Python paths redacted.

### 6. Network isolation
The AppContainer has no network capability (no internetClient, no privateNetworkClientServer, no
loopback exemption). Verified refused: HTTP and HTTPS to the internet (WSAEACCES 10013, a policy refusal,
not a routing failure), DNS resolution of outside names, 127.0.0.1 (a canary listener received nothing;
the app's port 9942), ::1, 10.0.0.1, 192.168.1.1, 169.254.169.254 (cloud metadata), and UDP. The sandbox
can open a listening socket on its own isolated loopback. Nothing outside the container can reach it,
and it cannot reach the host's loopback. Docker runs with `--network none`.

### 7. Process isolation
- Active process limit 1: every child process is refused by the Job Object (`subprocess`, `os.system`,
  `os.startfile`, a Python child). The test proves no child ever ran by checking a marker file.
- Non-admin, low-integrity AppContainer token; UI restrictions; no access to other processes.
- Timeout, cancel and server death kill the **whole job** (TerminateJobObject, then kill-on-close). With a
  child explicitly allowed (a test-only limit of 3), a runaway parent and its child both die at the
  timeout. Their PIDs are checked afterwards.
- Verified with real server processes: a crash inside the server during a render (exit 97), and the
  server process killed from outside (`taskkill /F` on the server alone, not its tree). **No sandboxed
  process survived either time.**
- The cancel flag, a lost lease and server shutdown all stop the sandbox **before** the worker gives up:
  a render never outlives the worker that owns it.

### 8. Resource limits
Render profiles (`manim_security.PROFILES`). Each value can be lowered or raised through the environment
only up to the hard maximum (`HARD_MAX`: 1920×1080, 60 fps, 300 s of video, 900 s timeout, 4096 MB, 500 MB
output, 64 processes, 200 KB source, 5 scenes):

| Profile | Resolution | FPS | Max video | Timeout | Memory |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `preview` | 854×480 | 15 | 45 s | 90 s | 1024 MB |
| `standard` (default, what the page uses) | 1280×720 | 30 | 60 s | 180 s | 1536 MB |
| `high_quality` | 1920×1080 | 30 | 60 s | 300 s | 2048 MB |

Common to all profiles, configurable:
- CPU: a hard cap of 50 % of the machine.
- CPU time: at most the timeout, as a backstop.
- Processes: 1.
- Output: 150 MB.
- Workspace: 800 MB, checked every second while running.
- Source: 100 KB.
- Scenes: 3.
- Frames: duration × fps.

Exceeding a limit stops the job with a category: `memory_limit`, `timeout`, `cpu_limit`,
`output_limit`, `frame_limit`, `resolution_limit`, `fps_limit`, `source_limit`, `scene_limit`. Measured
per render: seconds, CPU seconds, peak memory, processes started/left.

### 9. Output validation
`ManimService.validate`: ffprobe must decode a video stream (`inspect_media`, verified); width × height
must be exactly the profile's; duration must be within the profile's maximum; size within the output
limit. Only then is it copied out (`.part` + rename) and registered. A text file named `.mp4` is
rejected as `invalid_output`, a 320×240 video as `resolution_limit`; neither becomes an asset.

### 10. Phase 9 integration
No separate framework: a render is an `ai_generation_runs` row with the new column `kind = "manim"`,
and its attempts go in `ai_generation_attempts` (a new attempt state, `rendering`):
- **Lease, heartbeat, and a transition guarded by owner.** The shared heartbeat (`ai_runs.heartbeat`)
  renews the lease. If the lease is lost, the flag "lease" stops the sandbox and the worker writes nothing
  more. A cancel_requested run flags "user".
- The **recovery manager dispatches by kind** (`RecoveryManager.register("manim", manim_service)`,
  `executor_for(run)`). Old runs without a kind are AI media.
- **Checkpoints**, and what recovery does after a crash:

  | Where the crash happened | What recovery does | Recorded as |
  |---|---|---|
  | before the sandbox started | renders again | `rendered_again` |
  | while rendering | renders again from the start; the dead sandbox cannot be resumed and its workspace is removed | `rendered_again` |
  | after the render (output placed, attempt `registering`) | registers that output, no new render | `registered_output` |
  | after registration (asset recorded on the attempt) | finalizes | `finalized_asset` |

  Nothing is rendered twice once finished, and no asset is duplicated.
- Interrupted `AI_RECOVERY_MAX_ATTEMPTS` times → `needs_attention` (no endless loop).
- Two recovery managers: the atomic claim lets exactly one render the run.
- Identical requests from the same user while a render runs (the preview and the export, two tabs)
  attach to that run: one render, one asset.
- Cancel (`POST /api/ai-media/runs/{id}/cancel`, routed by kind): a queued run is cancelled at once; a
  running one has its sandbox stopped (whole job) and the attempt and run end as `cancelled`.
- Background mode: `/render` with `wait:false` answers 202 with the run id, followed with the existing
  runs API.
- Test-only crash points `MANIM_TEST_CRASH_AT` = `manim_rendering` | `manim_during_render` |
  `manim_after_render` | `manim_after_register`, active only with `AI_FAKE_PROVIDER=1`.

### 11. Asset integration
The validated video is adopted in place by the Phase 3 library (`source = "manim"`). Adoption is
idempotent: the same file is the same asset, so a registration repeated after a crash finds it.
`details.generation` records hash, fingerprint, profile, Manim version, runtime, run and attempt,
render seconds, CPU seconds, peak memory and frames. It never records a secret or the code. The `/render`
answer keeps its old shape (`status, video_url, asset_id, cached`) plus `cache_hit, generated, provider,
model, run_id`.

### 12. Cache integration
Identity = the sha256 of the normalized code (common LLM mistakes fixed as before, line endings
normalized), plus the Manim version, the scene, and the profile's width, height, fps and background.
It is stored through the Phase 5 AI cache: provider `manim`, model `manim 0.21.0`, `record_once` per
user. Same code + version + profile → the same asset, no render. Another profile, another Manim version
or changed code → a new render. `force` makes a new version. **Old pipeline videos**
(`static_videos/<Scene>_<sha16>.mp4`, 720p30 = the standard profile) are adopted as the cached result,
so existing lessons don't re-render.

### 13. Visual Router integration
`ManimRule` looks up an earlier render of the scene's code (standard profile), without rendering. If
found, the plan carries `asset_id`, `url` and `cache_hit`, and the page plays it without calling `/render`;
otherwise the plan says it will be rendered in the secure sandbox. The page's `/render` calls now pass
`project_id`, `scene_index` and `slot`, so a render finished after the page closed still lands in the
lesson's plan (Phase 9's `_lesson_after_generation`, same rules: never replaces a visual the lesson
already shows).

### 14. Visual Review integration
Review decisions stay authoritative. Before recovery (or a queued job) renders, `still_wanted` checks
the lesson. A **removed** visual is not rendered, and neither is a slot where **another visual was
chosen**. A **scene whose code changed** since the request is not rendered either. These runs end as
`cancelled / not_wanted` without starting a sandbox (quality-gate Test 10: the removed visual's queued
render, picked up by a fresh recovery manager as after a restart, is not rendered). Visual Review lists
the rendered animation as the scene's `MANIM` visual with its asset.

### 15. Deployment requirements
- **Windows host** (as tested): Windows 10/11 with AppContainer support. The Python installation and
  virtualenv must be on a local NTFS disk (they get an RX grant for the sandbox profile once). Use a short
  `MANIM_SANDBOX_DIR` (Windows paths are limited to 260 characters), and ffmpeg on the server. The server
  account needs no admin rights.
- **Linux host**: Docker (or a compatible runtime) that the server may call, the image built from
  `sandbox/Dockerfile.manim`, and `MANIM_SANDBOX_RUNTIME=docker`. **Untested here.** Test the runtime and
  run `tests/test_manim_sandbox.py` there before relying on it.
- **Current Hugging Face Space** (Dockerfile, user 1000): no Docker daemon and not Windows, so Manim
  rendering reports "Secure Manim execution is unavailable in this deployment." and everything else
  works. Rendering there needs a separate render host running this code with a secure runtime. Nothing was
  deployed in this phase.
- Keep API keys in `.env` (denied to the sandbox), **not** in Windows user environment variables (see
  limitations).
- Removing the sandbox from a Windows machine: `icacls "<Python install>" /remove *<SID>` and
  `icacls "<repo>\.venv" /remove *<SID>` (the SID is in `MANIM_SANDBOX_DIR\runtime-acl-*.done`), then
  delete the profile (`DeleteAppContainerProfile("AadhiEduEngine.ManimSandbox")`).

### 16. Security limitations
- **Registry reads (Windows).** Windows lets every AppContainer read some registry keys, including
  `HKCU\Environment` (the user's persistent environment variables) and the lists of installed software.
  Writes and the autostart keys are refused. A deny entry for the sandbox's SID was tried and **is not
  enforced** by Windows for this read. A "less privileged AppContainer" cannot start without the
  `registryRead` capability, which is exactly what grants that read. So the OS boundary does not stop
  this read. The static checks (no `winreg`/`ctypes` imports) and the audit hook do, and both are tested.
  → Don't keep secrets in Windows user environment variables on a render host, and prefer the Docker
  runtime on servers.
- System files that Windows makes readable to all AppContainers (Windows folder, fonts) remain readable.
  They contain nothing of the application.
- The Docker runtime and `sandbox/Dockerfile.manim` are **untested** (no Docker here).
- The CPU *time* limit is a backstop. With the 50 % rate cap, the wall-clock timeout ends a busy loop
  first (category `timeout`).
- Concurrency counters (global/per user) are per server process. The queue caps are counted in the
  database.
- The static checks may refuse unusual but harmless code (e.g. `open`, dunder attributes, numpy file
  I/O). Refused code is reported as `unsafe_code`, not run.
- LaTeX is not installed, so `Tex`/`MathTex` scenes fail (as before Phase 10 on this machine). The Gemini
  auto-heal (only with a key and AI generation on; its rewrite goes through the same checks and sandbox)
  is told to use `Text`.
- The first render with a new sandbox folder on a new machine is slower (the one-time runtime grant,
  about 15 s).

### 17. Testing
- **`tests/test_manim_sandbox.py`** (31 tests, the real AppContainer; skipped without a secure runtime):
  - **OS isolation probes** run as the sandboxed program *with no Python defences*:
    - files: `.env`, the database, source, the repo listing, the user profile, a canary file,
      traversal, absolute paths, a planted junction, links, the output link, writes outside;
    - network: the probes listed in §6;
    - processes: subprocess, os.system, startfile, a Python child (marker file proves none ran),
      whole-tree kill with PIDs checked;
    - resources: memory (MemoryError below the limit), infinite loop killed at the timeout, CPU
      rate cap measured, workspace size;
    - identity: a secret test variable absent, only the minimal variables present, AppContainer token
      with low integrity (S-1-16-4096), registry writes/autostart denied, the health check.
  - **Runner tests with real Manim**:
    - 7 legitimate educational scenes: text, a Text formula, a graph, shapes, 117 objects,
      MovingCameraScene, a diagram;
    - hook refusals (file, network, process, ctypes) and OS categories without the hook;
    - memory limit (numpy 3.2 GB), frame limit, resolution/fps raised at import (the profile wins) and
      during the render (stopped), timeout.
  - **Static checks**: 14 escape attempts, limits, profiles capped, fingerprint.
  - **No unsafe fallback**: disabled / unknown runtimes; the Docker command locked down.
- **`tests/test_manim_jobs.py`** (20 tests, Phase 9 integration with a stand-in sandbox, plus one real
  render):
  - render → asset → cache; profile/force; old pipeline videos reused; rejections and "unavailable";
    output validation;
  - crash before / during / after the render and after registration; repeated interruptions →
    needs_attention;
  - lost lease stops the render; two managers render once; cancel stops the render; identical
    requests share a render; concurrency and queue limits;
  - Visual Review (removed, code changed); the lesson plan and the router reuse the render; orphan
    workspace sweep;
  - the API (contract, error category header, privacy, 202, 503, the status endpoint); the schema step.
- **`tests/manim_sandbox_browser_check.mjs`** (17 checks, real server processes, real sandbox, real
  Chrome, silent):
  - the scene rendered and playing at 1280×720; a library asset recording runtime and memory; a durable
    run tied to the scene;
  - Visual Review shows it; the export completes and reuses the render;
  - forbidden code gets a friendly, escaped error;
  - server crash mid-render and an outside kill: no sandboxed process left, the render done again once;
    no workspace left.
- **Quality gate**: Test 1 → browser check §1–3; Tests 2–6 → OS isolation probes and runner tests;
  Test 7 → browser check §5; Test 8 → two managers; Test 9 → crash after the render; Test 10 → Visual
  Review test.

### 18. How to add or modify a safe render profile
1. Add or change an entry in `manim_security.PROFILES`: `width`, `height`, `fps`, `timeout`,
   `memory_mb`, `max_duration`. Stay within `HARD_MAX` (`limits()` caps every value anyway).
2. The fingerprint includes width, height, fps and background, so a changed profile never reuses
   another's renders. Bump `MANIM_VERSION` only together with the installed manim (and the Docker image
   tag).
3. The runner forces the profile and the output validator checks it: no other change is needed.
4. Add the profile to the tests (`StaticCheckTest.test_profiles_are_capped`; a render in `RunnerTest`
   when the resolution is new) and to the table above.
5. Never raise a limit to make a particular render pass: shorten the scene instead.

### Manim Sandbox Security Model (summary)
| Concern | Boundary (OS) | Defence in depth (Python) | Verified by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Files | AppContainer: only its workspace (per-job capability) and RX on the Python runtime | static checks (no open/os/pathlib), audit hook (open/listdir outside) | OS probes with no hook; runner tests |
| Network | AppContainer without network capabilities | no socket import; audit hook (connect/bind/getaddrinfo) | OS probes (10013 / refused / timeout); runner tests |
| Processes | Job Object: 1 process; kill-on-close; tree kill | no subprocess/os import; audit hook | OS probes, PIDs checked, server crash/kill checks |
| Privileges | low-integrity AppContainer token, no UI | — | token probe |
| Secrets | environment built from scratch; `.env` unreadable | — | secret-variable probe, `.env` probe |
| CPU | Job CPU rate hard cap + CPU time limit + wall timeout | — | CPU cap probe, infinite loop |
| Memory | Job memory limit | memory errors classified | 800 MB probe, numpy 3.2 GB |
| Output | workspace size watched; output size, type, resolution, duration validated | frame counter, profile forced | output-limit probe, validation tests |
| Registry | writes/autostart denied; **some reads allowed by Windows** | no winreg/ctypes imports; audit hook | registry probes; documented limitation |

### Database
One additive column: `ai_generation_runs.kind VARCHAR(20)` (NULL = AI media, `"manim"`), added by the
existing `database.ensure_schema` step. Nothing else changed; all lessons, runs, assets and Visual
Review decisions are kept.
- Tested on a copy of the real `projects.db`: column added, no-op the second time, rows kept, 78 lessons.
- Before the server restarted with it, a backup was made with SQLite's online backup and verified
  (integrity ok, 78 lessons, 115 assets): `scratchpad/projects.db.before-phase10`. It is not deleted
  automatically.

### Configuration (`.env.example`)
| Setting | Controls |
|---|---|
| `MANIM_SANDBOX_ENABLED` | switches Manim rendering off entirely |
| `MANIM_SANDBOX_RUNTIME` | which secure runtime to use |
| `MANIM_SANDBOX_DIR` | where job workspaces live |
| `MANIM_SANDBOX_IMAGE` | the Docker runtime's image |
| `MANIM_SANDBOX_PROFILE` | the Windows AppContainer profile name |
| `MANIM_TIMEOUT`, `MANIM_MEMORY_LIMIT_MB`, `MANIM_CPU_LIMIT_PERCENT`, `MANIM_MAX_PROCESSES`, `MANIM_MAX_OUTPUT_MB`, `MANIM_MAX_WORKSPACE_MB`, `MANIM_MAX_DURATION`, `MANIM_MAX_FPS`, `MANIM_MAX_RESOLUTION`, `MANIM_MAX_SOURCE_KB`, `MANIM_MAX_SCENES` | resource limits |
| `MANIM_MAX_CONCURRENT`, `MANIM_MAX_PER_USER`, `MANIM_MAX_QUEUED_PER_USER`, `MANIM_MAX_QUEUED` | concurrency and queue caps |
| `MANIM_AUTO_HEAL_ATTEMPTS` | how many times Gemini may rewrite code that crashed |

The defaults are the secure ones.

### API
- `POST /render` — `{code, profile?, project_id?, scene_index?, slot?, force?, wait?}`.
  - Success: `{status, video_url, asset_id, cached, cache_hit, generated, provider, model, run_id}`.
  - Errors: `{status:"error", detail, category}` with header `X-Error-Category`: 422 refused or failed,
    413 too large, 429 busy, 499 cancelled, 502 invalid output, 503 unavailable, 504 timeout.
  - `wait:false` → 202 `{run_id}`.
- `GET /api/manim/sandbox` (login required) — availability, runtime and isolation, health check
  (`check=true` runs it again: a minimal isolated process that must be refused the app's files and the
  network and see manim 0.21.0), profiles and limits, concurrency, counters. It returns no host path and
  no reason text.
- The runs API shows Manim runs with `kind`, `profile` and state label "Rendering". It never returns the
  code, and cancel is routed to the right executor.

### Files changed
- **New:** `manim_security.py`, `manim_sandbox.py`, `manim_runner.py`, `manim_jobs.py`,
  `sandbox/Dockerfile.manim`, `tests/test_manim_sandbox.py`, `tests/test_manim_jobs.py`,
  `tests/manim_sandbox_browser_check.mjs`.
- **Changed:**
  - `server.py`: the unsafe `/render` replaced; `/api/manim/sandbox`; wiring, startup sweep, shutdown,
    cancel routing, run view.
  - `visuals.py`: `ManimRule` reuse, `still_wanted` for renders, `use_manim_renders`.
  - `ai_runs.py`: `rendering` attempt state, shared heartbeat.
  - `ai_recovery.py`: executors by kind, workspace sweep.
  - `models.py`: the `kind` column.
  - `index.html`: lesson slot on renders, escaped friendly errors.
  - `.env.example`, `tests/backend_env.py`: test sandbox folder.

### Performance
Median of 3 renders of the same scene (standard 1280×720 30 fps, 5 s), with the same runner:
- sandboxed: **5.62 s**;
- direct, in a plain process: **5.11 s**;
- overhead: **+0.5 s, about 10 %** (AppContainer, Job Object, workspace ACL).
- Peak memory: 369–376 MB.
- The first sandboxed render on a machine includes the one-time runtime grant (21.8 s once).
- A health check takes 0.1–0.35 s.

### Results
The full silent regression ran on the final code; the backend was rerun after the Phase 4 test-helper
update below.

| Suite | Result |
|---|---|
| Backend (unittest) | **255/255** (204 before + 31 sandbox security + 20 job integration) |
| Frontend (node --test) | 92/92 |
| Phase 1 mascot | 27/27 |
| Phase 2 export | 30/30 |
| Export with library assets | 35/35 |
| Phase 3 assets | 26/26 |
| Phase 4 visual router | 24/24 |
| Phase 5 AI cache | 11/11 |
| Phase 6 Visual Review | 17/17 |
| Phase 7 recording performance (720p) | 18/18 (29 fps effective, 0 stalls) |
| Phase 8 providers | 12/12 |
| Phase 9 recovery | 14/14 |
| **Phase 10 browser + crash check** | **17/17** |
| Static checks | all inline scripts, browser checks parse, Python modules compile |

- **Phase 4 test helper updated:** it patched the removed `server._render_manim` to prove planning never
  renders. It now patches the new entry points (`manim_service.render`, `manim_sandbox.execute`), with
  the same intent.
- **Silence:** every browser check stays silent (no audio output, speech stubbed, silent narration).

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- The old `/render` executed untrusted code in the server's environment (secrets, network, files, no
  limits). It was replaced.
- Its timeout never worked (it caught `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` around an asyncio wait).
- A still `wait()` wrote one frame many times in one call, so the frame limit counted calls. It now
  counts frames.
- Code could raise the resolution/fps *during* the render (a 3840-wide, 120 fps video was produced in
  the first test run). It is now stopped at the first frame written.
- Denied sockets were reported as a file error. The runner now classifies OS refusals (network,
  process quota).
- A waiting request whose render lost its lease or was cancelled received a raw task cancellation. It now
  gets a proper error.
- The sandbox thread could outlive a cancelled worker. The worker now stops the sandbox and waits for it.
- Windows' 260-character path limit broke renders in deep folders. The runner now uses short output
  folders.
- The registry read limitation was found by a probe. A deny entry and LPAC were tried (neither works, see
  §16); it is documented and closed at the Python layers.
- The feasibility probe's AppContainer profile and its permissions were removed from the Python install
  and the virtualenv.

### Deferred to later phases
Phases 11–20 were not started.
- For the AI Presenter / Visual Director (Phases 11+), a renderer can be added as another job kind
  through `RecoveryManager.register` with the same run/attempt/lease model. The render profiles are the
  extension point for styling.

### Final status
**Complete** on the tested runtime (Windows AppContainer + Job Object).
- **Verified:** AI-generated Manim code never runs in the server. Real OS isolation was checked for files,
  network, processes, secrets and resources, and the Phase 9 durable job, asset, cache, router and
  review integration works. Crash and kill recovery was checked with real server processes, and no
  unsafe fallback exists.
- **Not verified:** the Docker runtime (no Docker here), and the Windows registry read limitation
  (§16) is closed only at the Python layers.
- **Deployment:** the current Hugging Face deployment reports Manim as unavailable until a render host
  with a secure runtime exists.
- Nothing was committed or deployed. The database backup is kept.
