## Phase 11 — Source Document Formatting Assistant

### Objective
Make the lesson's input reliable before a lesson is generated. The assistant analyses an uploaded source
document (PDF, DOCX, TXT or pasted text) and shows:
- what it contains and how it is structured;
- what can be used directly, and what is poorly structured, unclear or missing;
- how to reorganise it.

It prepares an **Aadhi-ready structure** that the user can correct and then hand to the **existing** lesson
generation. It never invents educational content, and it always shows where each item comes from.

### Baseline (start of Phase 11)
Backend 255/255, frontend 92/92, Phase 1 27/27, Phase 2 export 30/30, export with library assets 35/35,
Phase 3 26/26, Phase 4 24/24, Phase 5 11/11, Phase 6 17/17, Phase 7 18/18, Phase 8 12/12, Phase 9 14/14,
Phase 10 17/17.

### Audit (before Phase 11, from the code)
- **Upload:** in the page. It accepted PDF, DOCX and JSON (a ready script); **TXT was not accepted**.
- **Extraction:** the page used **pdf.js** (text joined per page as `--- Page N ---`) and **mammoth**
  (`extractRawText`). Both flattened everything: no headings, lists, tables or code survived. The server
  had no document parser.
- **Lesson Director:** `getSystemPrompt()` in `index.html` plus `POST /generate-script`, which streams from
  Gemini or OpenAI with the page's model choice. The screenplay schema (`scenes`, `concept_map`, …) feeds
  the Visual Router, Visual Review, playback and export.
- **Text-model access:** no shared layer. `/generate-script` calls the providers directly with `.env` keys;
  `ai_providers.py` covers images and video only. There were no Gemini or OpenAI keys on this machine.
- **Bug found in the existing generation:** the page's `/generate-script` reader declared its own
  `finalJsonData` inside the streaming block, so the parsed lesson never reached the code after it, and
  **every AI-generated lesson (PDF/DOCX → Gemini/OpenAI) failed** with "Generated output is not a valid
  non-empty array of scenes". The same bug is in the committed original (`e413bf0`). Only JSON script
  uploads, which skip that block, produced lessons. No earlier test generated a lesson through
  `/generate-script`. It is fixed (one line; see "Issues found").

### Architecture
```
 Upload (start screen)  ── "Generate Video" (unchanged: raw text → Lesson Director)
        │
        └─ "Analyze & Prepare Document"  (or paste text in the panel)
              ▼
 EXTRACTION in the page (sources.js; the page's existing pdf.js and mammoth, no new dependency)
   PDF  : text items → lines → headings (font size), paragraphs, lists, captions, formulas, code
          (monospace, indentation rebuilt), page numbers; running headers/footers dropped
   DOCX : mammoth HTML with Word's Title / Heading / Caption / Code styles → blocks, tables, images (alt only)
   TXT / pasted : markdown, underlined and numbered headings, lists, ``` code, formulas, page markers
        │  blocks {type, text, level, page, rows, alt, lang} + SHA-256 of the file
        ▼
 POST /api/source-documents  (source_documents.py)          ── validated, bounded, normalized (ids b1…bn,
        │                                                       heading path, paragraph numbers, language)
        ▼                                                    ── a version per owner + file name; identical
 source_documents (table)                                       content = the same document (reused)
        │
 POST /api/source-documents/{id}/analyze
        ├─ STRUCTURAL ANALYSIS (source_analysis.py, rule-based, no AI, instant)
        │     outline → sections / subtopics → content found → quality findings → readiness → recommendations
        │     → Aadhi-ready structure
        └─ AI ANALYSIS, when a model is available (the same Gemini / OpenAI keys as lesson generation)
              Phase 9 durable run, kind "document_analysis" (lease, heartbeat, recovery manager)
              structure-aware chunks → one model call each (document as untrusted data, JSON schema,
              one bounded repair) → keep only TRACEABLE items (quote found in the cited blocks)
              → checkpoint per chunk → one global pass (outline + findings, not the full text again)
              → merged, every AI item labelled
        ▼
 document_analyses (table): result (as found)  +  edits (the user's corrections, kept apart)
        │
 Document Assistant panel: overview · objectives · content structure · issues · recommendations
                           · Aadhi-ready structure (rename / reorder / leave out / edit / add) · Save
        │
 "Use for Lesson Generation" → GET …/lesson-input (refused if stale or running)
        ▼
 EXISTING Lesson Director (getSystemPrompt + /generate-script, unchanged) ← the Aadhi-ready text instead
        ▼                                                                    of the raw extraction
 Screenplay (same schema) → Visual Router → Visual Review → playback → export   (unchanged)
 The saved lesson records source_document {document_id, analysis_id}.
```

### Data model (additive)
Two new tables, created by `create_all`; no existing table changed.
- **`source_documents`**
  - id, owner, file name (display only), source type (pdf/docx/txt/text);
  - `content_sha256`: of the normalized blocks, the identity analyses use;
  - `file_sha256`: from the page;
  - extractor version, `version` (1, 2, … per owner and file name), pages, language, block and character
    counts, blocks (JSON).
  - A document version is never changed.
- **`document_analyses`**
  - id, document, owner, `content_sha256` of the version analysed, `analysis_version`;
  - `config_key`: analysis version + mode + provider + model;
  - mode (structural/ai), provider, model, status (running/completed/failed);
  - `run_id`: the Phase 9 run;
  - `chunk_results`: AI checkpoints;
  - `result`: the analysis as produced;
  - `edits`: the user's corrections;
  - timestamps.
- **The saved lesson** (`/save-history`) may carry `source_document: {document_id, analysis_id}`, validated
  as 32-hex ids. Direct lessons are unchanged.

A backup of `projects.db` was made and verified before the server started with the new tables:
`projects.db.before-phase11`, integrity ok, 82 lessons, 124 assets. After the start all 82 lessons and 124
assets are still there.

### Analysis
**Outline.**
- The title is a Word Title style, a `Title:` line or a unique top heading. Otherwise it is
  **"not provided"**, and an issue says so.
- Sections come from the top heading level and subtopics from the next level. Deeper headings stay inside
  their subtopic, and text before the first sub-heading becomes an untitled opening subtopic.
- Headings with special roles are recognised in English, Tamil and Hindi: objectives, prerequisites,
  summary, questions/exercises, references. They feed the objectives, prerequisites, summary and
  assessment instead of becoming teaching sections.

**Content found** (provenance `source`, each with its reference):
- **definitions:** "X is defined as / refers to / is a …", or a subtopic's first sentence defining its title;
- **explanations:** every text block is kept as written;
- **examples:** "Example / For example / e.g. …", or a subtopic titled Examples;
- **formulas:** kept verbatim, with variables and chemical components. Variables are checked against the
  text before the formula and in the rest of its subtopic ("where F is …", "force (F)"). Superscripts are
  exponents (v² → v), and only reactions (→) have chemical components;
- **code:** verbatim with indentation, language guessed, explained or not (prose before or after it), and
  the expected output when the source gives one;
- **visual references:** images (alt text), captions (Figure/Table/Chart N), tables (rows), each with a
  potential use;
- **important points, learning objectives, prerequisites, summary, source questions.**

**Rule-based findings** (provenance `analysis`, never called facts). Each has a type, severity, *why it
matters*, *source reference* and *suggestion*. The types:
- no title; no headings; very long paragraph; overloaded section; empty section;
- formula symbols not explained; code without explanation; reference to a missing figure or table;
- repeated content (shingle similarity); a term used before its definition; an example before its concept;
- no objectives, no examples, no summary;
- **text that reads like an instruction to an AI** (it stays document text; see Security).

Each finding maps to one quality class: `needs_reorganization`, `incomplete`, `missing_context`,
`ambiguous` or `partially_structured`.

**Readiness** comes from the **open** findings:
- The verdict is the most serious class among open warnings: well_structured, partially_structured,
  needs_reorganization, missing_context, ambiguous or incomplete.
- `ready_for_generation` means no open warning.
- Resolving an issue recomputes it.

**Recommendations** are restructuring actions derived from the findings (add headings, split a section,
define before use, explain formulas or code, add figures, remove duplicates, add objectives, examples or a
summary, move the summary to the end). AI recommendations are added when a model is used. Each can be
accepted or rejected.

**Aadhi-ready structure.**
- It holds: title, subject, audience, difficulty ("requires your input" until known), language,
  prerequisites, learning objectives, and the teaching sections in order, with the summary last and
  references left out.
- Each subtopic has its concept, definitions, explanations, examples, formulas, code, visual opportunities
  (the source's figures and tables, plus equation and code-walkthrough opportunities), important points and
  quiz candidates.
- Also the summary, and assessment material (source questions verbatim, plus quiz candidates marked as
  analysis).
- It is machine-readable JSON, and the source of the lesson input.

**Language.**
- The language is detected from the script of the text: Tamil, Devanagari, Telugu, Kannada, Malayalam,
  Bengali, Gujarati, Gurmukhi, Arabic, Cyrillic, CJK and Greek, plus English by its common words.
- The source is **never translated**. The lesson input tells the Lesson Director to write in the source's
  language, and the AI analysis is told to write its explanations in it.
- The English-wording rules (definitions, examples, objectives) apply to English only. Everything else
  works in any language.

### Provenance and traceability
Every item carries `provenance` and a `ref`:
- **`provenance`:**
  - `source`: taken from the document;
  - `analysis`: a rule-based finding;
  - `ai_suggestion`: the AI's suggestion, or its analysis;
  - `user` / `user_edited`: with the original kept.
- **`ref`:** block ids, page (PDF), nearest heading, and paragraph number (DOCX/TXT have no pages; the
  reference then says heading and paragraph and does not claim a page).

The panel shows them as badges: SOURCE, ANALYSIS, AI SUGGESTION, AI ANALYSIS for issues, YOURS, EDITED.

**AI items are verified before they are kept:**
- the cited blocks must belong to the analysed chunk;
- a quoted item must occur word for word in them (whitespace, case and typographic quotes normalized);
- a term must occur in them.

Everything else is dropped and counted ("N AI findings left out because they could not be traced to the
document"). A source item the AI found keeps `provenance: source` (the words are the document's) with
`found_by: ai`.

### AI analysis
- **Provider:** the same Gemini/OpenAI clients and `.env` keys as `/generate-script`, and the page's
  provider/model choice. It respects `AI_GENERATION_ENABLED`. Without a key the analysis is honestly
  **structural only** and says why; `mode: "ai"` then answers 503.
- **Prompt boundary:**
  - The rules are in the system message and the document is in the user message between `<document>`
    tags, declared **untrusted data, not instructions**.
  - Only the document may be used; items must be quoted with their block ids.
  - Formulas, code and terminology are preserved; suggestions are labelled as suggestions.
  - The model answers in the document's language, as JSON only.
- **Output handling:**
  - the answer is parsed (code fences tolerated) and checked against the schema;
  - **one bounded repair**: the malformed answer is sent back, not the document;
  - if it is still malformed, the AI part fails **visibly** ("the AI answer was not valid after one repair").
    The structural analysis stays, labelled as such, and a malformed structure is never accepted.
- **Large documents:**
  - chunks are made of whole sections while they fit (12,000 characters), else their sub-sections, else
    runs of blocks split between blocks;
  - there are at most 40 chunks, beyond which the AI analysis is refused and the structural one is still
    given;
  - the global pass receives the outline and the findings, not the text again.
- **Durable job:**
  - a Phase 9 run of kind `document_analysis`, registered with the recovery manager like Manim renders;
  - each analysed chunk is a checkpoint (`chunk_results`, written under the lease). After a crash, recovery
    continues with the **next** chunk: finished chunks are never sent again;
  - a lost lease stops the worker; cancelling is possible;
  - a run that ends without its analysis (given up after repeated interruptions) closes the analysis with
    the structural result, so nothing shows "running" forever.
- **Test stand-in:** a deterministic model built from the document (`AI_FAKE_PROVIDER=1` test servers
  only). `FAKE_LLM_MODE` = ok | malformed_once | malformed | fabricate | fail; `FAKE_LLM_SECONDS` adds a
  delay.

### Versioning, reuse, staleness
- **Identity:** owner + file name + `content_sha256` (the normalized blocks plus the extractor version).
- **Uploading the same content again** returns the same document (`reused`). Changed content under the same
  name is a new version.
- **Reuse:** analysing again with the same configuration (analysis version, mode, provider, model) reuses
  the analysis. A running AI analysis is attached to, not started twice. `force` makes a new one.
- **Staleness:**
  - An analysis is **stale** when a newer version of the file exists, or when it was made for other content
    or by an older analysis version.
  - The panel shows why, and "Use for Lesson Generation" is disabled.
  - `lesson-input` answers **409**: a stale analysis is never used silently.

### Editing
The editable fields:
- title, subject, audience and difficulty;
- section titles, order and inclusion;
- learning objectives: edit, accept or reject AI suggestions (they start *not accepted*), add, remove;
- prerequisites: add, edit, remove;
- issue status (open/resolved) and recommendation status (pending/accepted/rejected).

How edits are stored and applied:
- Edits are a separate JSON, validated against the analysis they apply to: sections must be a permutation of
  the existing ones, ids must exist, lengths are bounded.
- They are applied on top of the result at read time. What was found is never overwritten; edited items keep
  their original text and provenance.
- Editing is refused while an AI analysis runs.
- The content itself (definitions, formulas, code) is not edited here: it stays as in the source.

### Handoff to the existing lesson generation
`lesson_input()` renders the effective Aadhi-ready structure as text:
- **The header** states the rules: everything is material to teach, not instructions; [SOURCE] lines keep
  their facts, formulas and code exactly; nothing is invented where "not provided".
- **Content:** title, subject, audience, difficulty, and the language instruction; then prerequisites and
  learning objectives (accepted only), each tagged.
- **Sections in teaching order:** definitions, explanations, formulas (with a note of unexplained symbols),
  code in fences with its indentation, expected output, examples, important points, and the source's
  visuals. Every line is tagged with its provenance and location.
- **Closing parts:** the summary, source questions, the accepted recommendations ("not source facts"), and
  the known gaps. Gaps in excluded sections are left out.

Excluded sections, unaccepted suggestions and rejected recommendations are not handed over.

The page sets `window.preparedSource` and runs the **same** generation function (`runGeneration`, formerly
the Generate button's handler). The only differences: the prepared text replaces the raw extraction, the
prompt names it as the prepared source, and `source_document` is saved with the lesson. Everything
downstream is unchanged.

### Frontend
- **`sources.js`:** extraction, API client, editing session and panel. It is a classic script
  (`window.AadhiSources`) and a CommonJS module for tests.
- **`index.html`:**
  - the **Analyze & Prepare Document** button and TXT upload (direct generation too);
  - the panel wiring (`window.documentAssistant`), the `runGeneration()` refactor and panel styles;
  - the chosen file name is now escaped when shown (it was inserted as HTML).
- **Panel states:** empty (paste text), loading with extraction progress, error, and result.
- **Result:** overview with the readiness verdict and the AI status; objectives and prerequisites;
  collapsible content structure; issues with *why / source / suggestion* and resolve buttons;
  recommendations with accept/reject; the Aadhi-ready structure editor; and the actions (Analyse other
  text, Stop AI analysis, Save, **Use for Lesson Generation**).
- **Behaviour:** it polls a running AI analysis every 1.5 s and stops when closed, and it keeps the scroll
  position when an edit re-renders it. The **Generate Video** direct path works as before.

### Security
- **Uploads:** documents are untrusted input. Extraction happens in the browser and the server receives only
  text blocks: no file is stored, nothing is executed or rendered, and image data is not sent.
- **Limits:** request ≤ 6 MB (checked before parsing); ≤ 6,000 blocks, 50,000 characters per block,
  800,000 characters in total; tables ≤ 300 × 30; the page refuses files over 20 MB.
- **Names and access:** the file name is display-only (path separators removed). Access is owner-only, with
  the same 404 for other users' documents and analyses.
- **Prompt injection:** the document is data in the AI prompt (see above). Instruction-like text is flagged
  as an issue and handed over as material to teach, under a header that says so.
- **No secrets:** model errors are reported with key/token parameters masked.

### Observability
`[DOCUMENT]` JSON log lines (ids, counts, durations, provider/model, never document text):
- `document_stored`, `document_reused`;
- `document_analysis_started`, `document_analysis_completed`, `document_analysis_failed`;
- `document_analysis_reused`, `document_analysis_saved`, `document_analysis_stale`;
- `document_chunk_analysed`, `document_analysis_resumed`, `document_analysis_lease_lost`.

AI analysis runs appear in the existing runs API with `kind: "document_analysis"`.

### Configuration
No new settings. It uses the existing `GEMINI_API_KEY` / `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `AI_GENERATION_ENABLED`, the
Phase 9 `AI_JOB_*` / `AI_RECOVERY_*` settings and `AI_MEDIA_LOG`. For tests: `AI_FAKE_PROVIDER`,
`FAKE_LLM_MODE`, `FAKE_LLM_SECONDS`.

### API
| Endpoint | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `POST /api/source-documents` | submit extracted blocks; returns the document version and `reused` |
| `GET /api/source-documents/{id}` | the document and its analyses (with stale flags) |
| `POST /api/source-documents/{id}/analyze` | `{mode: auto\|structural\|ai, provider, model, force}` → the analysis (reused, completed, or running) |
| `GET /api/source-analyses/{id}` | the analysis with edits applied, readiness, stale flag and reason, AI status |
| `PUT /api/source-analyses/{id}/edits` | save corrections (validated) |
| `GET /api/source-analyses/{id}/lesson-input` | the prepared source for the Lesson Director (409 when stale or running) |
| `POST /api/source-analyses/{id}/cancel` | stop a running AI analysis |

### Files changed
- **New:**
  - `source_analysis.py`: the analysis, pure functions;
  - `source_documents.py`: service, AI job, API;
  - `sources.js`: page extraction, API, editing, panel;
  - tests: `tests/test_source_documents.py`, `tests/sources.test.js`, `tests/source_documents_browser_check.mjs`;
  - fixtures: `tests/fixtures/sources/{A_well_structured,B_poorly_structured,C_technical,D_tamil}.txt`,
    `make_docx.py`.
- **Changed:**
  - `models.py`: two tables;
  - `server.py`: router, recovery registration, `/sources.js`, shutdown, `source_document` on
    `/save-history`;
  - `index.html`: button, TXT, panel, `runGeneration`, styles, the generation fix, the escaped file name.

### Tests
- **`tests/test_source_documents.py`** (32 tests):
  - block validation, bounds, normalization, content hash;
  - fixtures A–D through the page's own extractor (Node);
  - missing title, nested sections, references; figures, tables, captions; concept before definition and
    example first; instruction-like text; quiz provenance; structure-aware chunking;
  - traceability of AI items (fabricated quote and foreign block dropped), schema errors and fenced JSON,
    merge labels;
  - edits with provenance and invalid edits; readiness following resolved issues;
  - submit, reuse, versions and staleness (409); access control (404/401); request limits; AI analysis
    through the API (a durable run); AI unavailable (structural, 503 for `ai`);
  - malformed output repaired once, else refused; fabricated items dropped; provider failure;
  - **recovery from the last checkpoint** (crash after chunk 1: the new server sends only the remaining
    chunks);
  - edits persisted and handoff; the lesson saved with its source; edits and lesson input refused while
    running.
- **`tests/sources.test.js`** (12 tests):
  - extraction: PDF items (headings by size, joined lines, bullets, formulas, captions, monospace code with
    rebuilt indentation, page numbers, running headers dropped); mammoth HTML (title, headings, nested
    lists, tables, images without data, captions, code, formulas, entities); TXT (all heading forms,
    fenced and indented code, pages); Tamil kept exactly;
  - file kinds, `extractFile` with pdf.js/mammoth/text and hashing;
  - the edit session and its payload; labels; the API client;
  - the panel: pasted text, polling a running AI analysis, save-then-use; errors and stale.
- **`tests/source_documents_browser_check.mjs`** (18 checks; real Chrome, a throwaway server, silent):
  - A as a Chrome-printed PDF; B as TXT with the stand-in AI; C as a real Word document (edited, saved,
    used for generation); D in Tamil; pasted text; stale;
  - `/generate-script` is answered by a fixed screenplay (no AI provider). The lesson then plays through the
    existing router, shows in Visual Review, and is exported (COMPLETED).

### Results
Full silent regression on the final code. The backend was rerun alone after a test-isolation fix in two new
Phase 11 tests: they had passed alone and failed in the first full run.

| Suite | Result |
|---|---|
| Backend (unittest) | **287/287** (255 before + 32 Phase 11) |
| Frontend (node --test) | **104/104** (92 before + 12 Phase 11) |
| Phase 1 mascot | 27/27 |
| Phase 2 export | 30/30 |
| Export with library assets | 35/35 |
| Phase 3 assets | 26/26 |
| Phase 4 visual router | 24/24 |
| Phase 5 AI cache | 11/11 |
| Phase 6 Visual Review | 17/17 |
| Phase 7 recording performance (720p) | 18/18 (27.7 fps effective, 0 stalls) |
| Phase 8 providers | 12/12 |
| Phase 9 recovery | 14/14 |
| Phase 10 Manim sandbox | 17/17 |
| **Phase 11 browser check** | **18/18** |
| Static checks | 6 inline scripts and all JS files parse; browser checks parse; Python modules compile |

The two new tests that failed in the first full run:
- **The API AI-analysis test** used the shared test client without holding its event loop, so the
  background analysis was abandoned after the request. It now keeps one client for the whole test.
- **The recovery test** found the run orphaned by that failure. Service tests now start without leftover
  active runs.

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- **Existing lesson generation was broken:** a shadowed `finalJsonData` in the page's `/generate-script`
  reader, in the original code as well. Every AI-generated lesson failed after the model answered. It was
  found by the Phase 11 browser check, the first test to generate a lesson through that path, and fixed by
  assigning to the outer variable.
- **The analyzer's first run:**
  - "where F is the force…" was taken as a definition of "where F";
  - "Chlorophyll is a green pigment" was missed;
  - objectives mentioning a term were flagged as "used before its definition";
  - superscripts produced "v2";
  - "KE" was treated as a chemical formula;
  - a variable defined earlier in the document counted as undefined.

  All fixed and covered by tests.
- **Too lenient on poorly structured text:** a 1,600-character document with no headings was not flagged.
  The thresholds were lowered (1,200 characters or 5 paragraphs; long paragraph over 1,000 characters).
- **Tagging:** AI-suggested subject, audience and difficulty were tagged "accepted" although nobody accepted
  them. Only accepted objectives and prerequisites say ACCEPTED.
- **Leak:** the "known gaps" list leaked the title of a section the user had left out.
- **Page:**
  - PDF code lost its indentation; it is rebuilt from positions;
  - the body text size was taken from a median that picked the title's size in short documents;
  - bullets are removed from list items;
  - an edit re-render lost the scroll position;
  - there was no way back to pasted text after a result.

### Known limitations
- **Extraction limits:**
  - PDF structure is inferred from font sizes and positions: multi-column layouts and scanned PDFs (no text
    layer) are not handled, and there is no OCR.
  - Word equations (OMML) are dropped by mammoth (the text around them stays).
  - Word lists become paragraphs unless mammoth recognises the numbering.
- **Rules:** the English-wording rules (definitions, examples, objectives, important points) apply to
  English text. Other languages get structure, formulas, code, figures, duplicates and the AI analysis.
- **References:** DOCX/TXT references have no page numbers; they give the heading and paragraph number.
- **Not tested against a real model:** no Gemini or OpenAI key is configured here. The AI analysis ran only
  with the deterministic stand-in; the provider calls use the same clients as `/generate-script`.
- **Ignored edits:** while an AI analysis runs, edits are refused. After it finishes, AI objectives are added
  unaccepted, so earlier edits are not needed.
- **Recommendations are advice:** accepting one hands it to the Lesson Director as guidance; the assistant
  does not rewrite the source. Only "summary last" is applied to the structure itself.

### Deferred to later phases
- Phases 12–20 were not started.
- For them, the Aadhi-ready structure (with its visual opportunities, formulas and code, each with
  provenance) is the input foundation. The saved lesson's `source_document` links a lesson to its prepared
  source.

### Final status
**Complete.**
- **Verified end to end:** PDF, DOCX, TXT and pasted text are extracted with their structure, analysed with
  traceable, provenance-labelled results, corrected by the user, and handed to the existing Lesson Director.
  The generated lesson then plays, shows in Visual Review and exports.
- **Not verified:** the AI analysis ran only with the deterministic stand-in, because no Gemini or OpenAI key
  is configured here.
- **Found and fixed:** a bug in the existing generation that made every AI-generated lesson fail.
- **Nothing committed or deployed.** The database backup is kept.

---

## Phase 12 — AI Presenter / AI Teacher

### Objective
A reusable presenter / teacher for lessons: it appears where it helps teaching, speaks the lesson's own narration,
uses expressions and gestures, stands where the content stays readable, and stays the same presenter across scenes.

The presenter supports the lesson; it never becomes the lesson. It is an additional scene element beside the
Visual Router's educational visual, and the architecture is provider-independent, cacheable, recoverable,
reviewable and exportable. It is the foundation Phase 13 builds on; no cinematic composition is implemented here.

### Baseline (start of Phase 12)
Backend 287/287, frontend 104/104, Phase 1 27/27, Phase 2 30/30, export with library assets 35/35, Phase 3 26/26,
Phase 4 24/24, Phase 5 11/11, Phase 6 17/17, Phase 7 18/18, Phase 8 12/12, Phase 9 14/14, Phase 10 17/17,
Phase 11 18/18.

### Audit (before Phase 12, from the code)
- **Mascot:**
  - `MascotController` (`mascot.js`) plays clip-based states: idle, talking, explaining, thinking, question, success.
  - The screenplay places the mascot with `aadhi_position` (left, right, center, a bottom-left or bottom-right
    popup, or hidden).
  - `applyAadhiLayout()` arranges the board and the side zone around it.
  - The side zone always holds content: a side visual, or the lesson's Skill Tree.
- **Narration:** split at `[PAUSE:n]`; each segment is one `/generate-audio` TTS file played back to back, with
  silences between. The Default engine uses browser speech in preview (a recording cannot capture it) and server
  audio when exporting.
- **Providers:** images and videos only. Veo makes video from text or a picture and **cannot speak a supplied
  narration**. No provider can make an audio-driven talking presenter, and no API keys are configured here.

### Architecture
```
 Screenplay scene (unchanged schema; presenter_plan is optional)
        │
        ├──► Visual Router (visuals.py)            educational visual (visual_plan)          unchanged
        │
        └──► Presenter Director (presenters.plan_scene)        POST /api/presenters/plan
               settings: presenter · Show (As the lesson says | Automatic | Always | Off) · position · style
               role of the scene → shown? where? behaviour / expression / gesture, and why
               Visual Review decision first → safe composition (never over board, side zone, subtitles)
               → presenter_plan {enabled, presenter_id, type, position, placement, layout, box, behavior,
                                 expression, gesture, *_shown + fallbacks, reason, review_status, media?}
        │
 Playback (index.html renderSlide) ── presenter plan → applyAadhiLayout(layout)  (the board makes room)
        │   Aadhi (mascot) ........ MascotController, as before
        │   Aadhi Teacher (SVG) ... presenter.js: expressions, gestures, blinking, breathing;
        │                            mouth ← speech timeline envelope, in time with the narration audio
        │   AI presenter clip ..... muted <video>, kept in time with the narration segments
        │
 Speech timeline (POST /api/presenters/speech): the same [PAUSE] segments and the same /generate-audio files
        the page plays → timing + loudness envelope (25/s) + audio identity                 (one audio pipeline)
        │
 AI presenter clips (explicit, in Visual Review): POST /api/presenters/generate
        → normalized presenter request (MediaRequest media_type "presenter")
        → AI media layer (ai_media.py): registry capability check → AI cache → Phase 9 run/attempt
          (provider job id kept, recovery, cancel, needs_attention) → validation → library asset (ai-presenter)
        → the scene's presenter_plan.media (and the saved lesson, via the completion hook)
        │
 Visual Review (review.js): a "presenter" item per scene → POST /api/presenters/review
        (keep · choose a clip/picture · move · remove · reset · generate / New Version); never generates on open
        │
 Export: the same saved plan; clips and speech timelines preloaded; a missing AI clip is a warning, never generated
```

### Presenter model (`presenters.py`)
- **Types:**
  - **mascot:** Aadhi, played by the existing MascotController.
  - **illustrated:** Aadhi Teacher, drawn by the page.
  - **ai_avatar:** a generated clip.
  - **custom:** a user's presenter with an optional reference picture.
- **Profiles:**
  - Each has a stable id, name, type, role, version, appearance (description, palette, clothing, hair), voice
    ("lesson narration"), defaults (position, behaviour, expression, gesture) and capabilities.
  - The built-ins are `aadhi`, `aadhi-teacher` and `ai-teacher`, defined in code. Custom profiles live in the new
    `presenter_profiles` table: owner, name, appearance, `reference_asset_id` (one of the owner's pictures),
    defaults, version.
  - Identity is never a prompt string: the `appearance_hash` is taken over id, version, appearance and reference.
- **Vocabularies** (normalized; provider-specific names stay in the adapters):

  | Kind | Values |
  |---|---|
  | behaviours | idle, talking, explaining, thinking, listening, welcoming, concluding |
  | expressions | neutral, friendly, engaged, thinking, surprised, happy, encouraging, serious |
  | gestures | none, open_hand, point, counting, explaining, emphasis, thinking, welcome |
  | positions | left, center, right, hidden |
  | placements | side, pip, foreground, background |

- **Fallbacks:** a presenter that cannot show an expression or gesture shows the nearest one it can (for example
  surprised → engaged, counting → explaining). This is recorded (`expression_shown`, `fallbacks`, "surprised
  shown as engaged") and never fails.

### Presenter Director (rule-based, like the Visual Router)
| Scene role (detected) | Presenter |
|---|---|
| intro (first / title scene) | shown, side, welcoming, happy, gesture welcome |
| visual (a side visual) | shown, side, explaining, engaged, open hand; points when the narration says "look at / this diagram" |
| explanation | shown, side, explaining, engaged; counting when the narration counts ("three stages") |
| math (MathJax / equation) | shown **small** (picture-in-picture in its own zone) |
| text_heavy | shown small |
| quiz | shown, side, listening, encouraging, point |
| summary | shown, side, concluding, encouraging |
| code, technical (Manim / simulation), video | **hidden**: the visual needs the canvas |

- **Show presenter setting:**
  - "As the lesson says" (the default) follows the screenplay's `aadhi_position`. With the Aadhi mascot this is
    exactly the old behaviour, so existing lessons are unchanged.
  - "Automatic" applies the table above.
  - "Always" makes hidden scenes small instead.
  - "Off" hides the presenter.
- **Other inputs:** the teaching style (friendly, energetic, calm, formal) adjusts expressions, and Advanced
  settings can fix the expression or gesture.
- **Review first:** Visual Review decisions (removed, moved, a chosen clip) are applied before anything else.

### Safe composition
- Normalized boxes on the 16:9 stage match the page's own layouts:
  - side zones: left 0.02–0.28 and right 0.72–0.98, height 0.14–0.86;
  - center: 0.28–0.53;
  - picture-in-picture: a small box low in the presenter's own zone (0.18 × 0.34, above the subtitles).
- The content-safe area is the board (per layout), the side zone (a side visual or the Skill Tree) and the
  subtitle band.
- A conflicting picture-in-picture moves to the side; any other conflict hides the presenter, with the reason
  recorded.
- Drawn and AI presenters never use the popup layouts: they have no free corner, which the first browser run
  showed (the corner presenter covered the Skill Tree).
- The browser check measures the real on-screen rectangles in every scene.

### Speech and lip sync
- **One audio pipeline:** the presenter speaks the lesson's own narration. The speech timeline is built from the
  same TTS segment files the page plays, with their pauses, plus a loudness envelope (25 values a second, from
  ffmpeg) and `audio_sha256` (the segment files' hashes plus their pauses).
- **Server audio in presenter scenes:** scenes with a drawn or AI presenter use server audio in preview as well
  (as export already did), so the presenter follows exactly the audio that is heard and recorded.
- **What each presenter really does:**
  - **Aadhi Teacher:** the mouth opens with the voice's loudness in the playing segment. This is **not** phoneme
    lip sync and is never called that. With browser speech (no audio file) it falls back to a plain talking
    animation.
  - **AI clip:** muted (no second voice), re-synced at each segment to `segment.start + audio.currentTime`,
    playbackRate matched, and paused on hold.
  - **Lip sync:** claimed only when the provider's capability says so (the stand-in's mouth follows the audio's
    loudness).

### Provider integration and capability model (`ai_providers.py`, reused)
- **Media type:** `presenter` (the file is a video). `MediaRequest.presenter` holds the normalized request: profile
  snapshot, appearance hash, reference assets, behaviour, expression, gesture, background, and the speech (audio
  hash, duration, fps, envelope, segment timing).
- **Capability flags** (only what is real; nothing is claimed by default): `lip_sync`, `audio_input`,
  `expressions`, `gestures`, `transparent_background`, `reference_image`, `consistent_identity`, `cancellation`.
- **Discovery:**
  - The registry order for presenters is `AI_PRESENTER_PROVIDERS` (empty by default), and
    `AI_PRESENTER_PROVIDER` puts one first.
  - Capability checks happen before any generation. An explicit choice is honoured, and fallback happens only if
    allowed.
  - Without a provider the answer is **503 "AI presenter generation isn't configured yet … continue with the Aadhi
    mascot, the illustrated Aadhi Teacher, or no presenter."** Nothing is faked.
- **Honest listing:** `/api/presenters` lists the presenter providers with their capabilities, and the providers
  that cannot present (Veo) with the reason.
- **Test stand-in:** `fake-presenter` (tests only, `AI_FAKE_PROVIDER=1`) is a job provider whose jobs survive a
  restart and can be cancelled or made to fail. It draws a teacher with ffmpeg: its mouth opens during the
  envelope's loud parts, and it uses the profile's colours and gesture poses. It declares exactly that: lip sync
  by loudness, five expressions and five gestures, no transparency, no reference image.

### Caching, assets, recovery, cancellation (reused)
- **Cache identity** (Phase 5): the presenter id and profile version, the appearance hash, the speech audio hash,
  behaviour, the expression and gesture **as shown**, background, plus provider, model and parameters.
  Position is excluded: it is layout. The same request is a cache hit, and "New version" is `force_regenerate`:
  a new clip, with the earlier one kept.
- **Asset:** the clip is adopted in place as a library asset (source `ai-presenter`). `details.presenter` records
  presenter id, version, behaviour, expression, gesture, speech seconds, scene and lip sync, and
  `details.generation` records provider, model, identity, run and job. It holds no speech data and no secret.
- **In use:** the lesson reference field `presenter_plan.media` means a used clip cannot be deleted.
- **Recovery** (Phase 9): the presenter request is saved with the run (`presenter` in the request fields), so a
  restart resumes the **same provider job** with no second submission. An ambiguous paid submission becomes
  "needs attention".
- **Recovery respects review:** `presenter_still_wanted` skips scenes whose presenter was removed in Visual Review.
  A finished clip lands in the saved lesson's `presenter_plan.media` (unless removed, already chosen, or a New
  Version the user applies).
- **Cancellation:** the existing runs cancel, with provider-side cancel where supported.

### Visual Review integration (Phase 6, extended)
- **Where it lives:** a `presenter` item per scene, in the same panel, filters and statuses, stored in the same
  `scene.visual_review` map under the key `presenter`.
- **Actions:** Keep, Move (left, right, center, small corner), Choose a clip or picture from the Asset Library,
  Remove presenter, Restore automatic, and for AI presenters "Generate presenter clip" or "Generate new presenter
  version". Opening the review never generates.
- **Facts shown:** presenter and type, where, doing, speech ("mouth follows the narration's loudness (not phoneme
  lip sync)", "Lip-synced to the narration by <provider>", "Talking animation (not lip-synced)"), why, and the
  fallbacks.
- **Endpoint:** `POST /api/presenters/review` records the decision, re-plans the scene and saves it. The decision
  is authoritative for the Director, for recovery and for export.

### Playback and export
- `renderSlide` asks the stage for the scene's effective plan. The legacy path (Aadhi placed by the lesson)
  returns none and nothing changes.
- A presenter plan sets the board layout and hides Aadhi's clip for drawn or AI presenters.
- The presenter layer is fixed in the presenter's box and cleared on every scene change (no stale presenter).
- **Export** uses the same saved plans:
  - export preparation preloads each presenter scene's speech timeline and clip;
  - a missing AI clip gives the warning "no presenter clip yet (generate it in Visual Review)", and is never
    generated automatically (it may be billed);
  - the setting "If an AI presenter clip is missing: use Aadhi Teacher instead" is off by default. Without it, no
    presenter is substituted.

### Frontend
- **Start screen:** Presenter (unavailable ones disabled, with the reason), Show presenter, Position, Teaching
  style, and a preview card.
  - The preview is the live Aadhi Teacher with the chosen expression and gesture, or Aadhi's poster.
  - For AI presenters it says the clip is made only when asked, in Visual Review, and may be billed.
- **Advanced:** presenter provider (Automatic plus the presenter providers, or "not presenter-capable: Google Veo"),
  expression, gesture, background, and the missing-clip fallback. Settings are kept per browser.

### Configuration
| Setting | Controls |
|---|---|
| `AI_PRESENTER_PROVIDERS` | the presenter provider order (none by default) |
| `AI_PRESENTER_PROVIDER` | the preferred presenter provider |

For test servers: `FAKE_TTS=1` (a speech-like tone instead of a TTS service, only with `AI_FAKE_PROVIDER=1`) and
`FAKE_PRESENTER_JOB_SECONDS`.

### Database (additive)
- The new table `presenter_profiles` is created by `create_all`. No existing table changed.
- The optional `scene.presenter_plan` and `scene.visual_review.presenter` live in the lesson JSON.
- A verified backup was made before the server started with the table: `projects.db.before-phase12`, integrity ok,
  86 lessons, 133 assets, all present after the start.

### API
| Endpoint | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `GET /api/presenters` | profiles (built-in and own), what each can do here, presenter providers and non-capable providers, vocabularies |
| `POST /api/presenters/profiles` | create a custom presenter |
| `DELETE /api/presenters/profiles/{id}` | delete a custom presenter |
| `POST /api/presenters/plan` | the Director's plans (validated settings; never generates) |
| `POST /api/presenters/speech` | the speech timeline of a narration |
| `POST /api/presenters/generate` | a presenter clip: wait, or 202 with a run to follow |
| `POST /api/presenters/review` | a presenter review decision |

### Files changed
- **New:**
  - `presenters.py`: model, Director, composition, speech, requests, API;
  - `presenter.js`: settings, stage, the illustrated teacher, API, review facts;
  - tests: `tests/test_presenters.py`, `tests/presenter.test.js`, `tests/presenter_browser_check.mjs`.
- **Changed:**
  - `ai_providers.py`: the presenter media type, capability flags, the presenter request, `FakePresenterProvider`,
    presenter order;
  - `ai_cache.py`: stand-in settings, and a presenter asset is a video;
  - `ai_media.py`: presenter output paths, validation, registration, the unavailable message, the request hash;
  - `ai_runs.py`: the request field;
  - `assets.py`: the `ai-presenter` source, and the presenter clip as a lesson reference;
  - `models.py`: `PresenterProfile`;
  - `server.py`: router, completion hook, still-wanted check, `/presenter.js`, run view shape, test TTS stand-in;
  - `review.js`: the presenter item, decisions, generation, detail;
  - `index.html`: settings block, styles, `renderSlide` and narration hooks, presenter planning, export
    preparation, the review adapter.

### Tests
- **`tests/test_presenters.py`** (25 tests):
  - vocabulary fallbacks and stable profiles;
  - the Director: roles, modes, styles and overrides, left position, mascot capabilities, review first, safe
    composition, scenes without a presenter;
  - speech segments matching the page, and the timeline (pauses, envelope, audio identity);
  - capabilities reported honestly (stand-in versus the real registry: none available, Veo not capable);
  - generation, cache hit, New Version and provenance; cache identity (speech, expression, profile version count;
    position does not); unsupported, unknown and explicit providers with no silent substitute;
  - recovery resuming the same job; cancellation with provider cancel, and duplicates attaching;
  - the clip landing in the lesson, in use and undeletable, and not wanted after a removal;
  - the API: list, plan validation, honest refusals, speech, review and access, custom profiles; lessons without
    presenters unchanged.
- **`tests/presenter.test.js`** (12 tests):
  - settings and the legacy path; plan merge and boxes; every expression and gesture; envelope levels;
  - the stage: the audio-driven mouth, the browser-speech fallback, the AI clip in sync, a missing clip with
    nothing faked, the allowed fallback, hidden and mascot;
  - the settings panel; honest review facts; the Visual Review presenter flow (generate, New Version, move,
    remove, reset); the API client.
- **`tests/presenter_browser_check.mjs`** (20 checks; real Chrome, a throwaway server, the stand-in presenter and

  - default lessons unchanged, and the start-screen choice;
  - "no clip, nothing shown"; the Director's plan for A–F; review listing without generation; generation, New

  - A–F geometry with no overlap; small for maths, hidden for code; the clip in sync;
  - export (the presenter in presenter scenes only, and the recorded frame's presenter colours);
  - Aadhi Teacher: placement, expressions and gestures, the envelope-driven mouth;
  - a server kill during generation, recovered from the same job, landing in the saved lesson.

After the re-runs, the Aadhi preview's crop was moved by a few pixels (CSS only; nothing tests it) and the page's
scripts were checked again.

### Real provider validation
**None.**
- No real presenter provider was available. None of the configured providers can make a presenter speak a supplied
  narration: Veo makes video from text or pictures. No Gemini or OpenAI key is configured here.
- No real presenter request was made; the presenter pipeline ran only with the local stand-in.
- The Aadhi Teacher is not a provider (it is drawn by the page). It was validated for real in Chrome.

### Visual quality validation
Screenshots inspected (`qa/presenter-out`) for scenes A–F with the AI presenter clip (stand-in) and with the Aadhi
Teacher:
- **A (intro):** the teacher at the right, welcoming.
- **B (diagram):** points at the board; mouth open mid-word.
- **C (three stages):** counting.
- **D (equation):** small, low in its zone, pointing; the equation fully readable.
- **E (quiz):** encouraging, pointing.
- **F (code):** hidden; the board full width.

No overlap with the board, the Skill Tree or side visual, or the subtitles, measured in every scene. The recorded
export frame shows the same composition.

The illustrated teacher's first render showed defects, fixed before acceptance:
- a floating head (neck gap);
- arms detached from the shoulders;
- the "thinking" hand going outward instead of to the chin;
- hands clipped at the frame edge (view box widened);
- a long neck (head lowered).

The stand-in AI figure is deliberately crude (it only proves the pipeline).

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- The picture-in-picture layout first reused the mascot's popup layout, which has no free corner. The presenter
  covered the Skill Tree and reached the subtitles. Picture-in-picture now sits in the presenter's own side zone,
  and the side zone always counts as content.
- Presenter run results were shaped as images in the runs view; they are shaped as videos now.
- Stand-in presenter settings did not map expressions and gestures; mapping and fallbacks now happen in the adapter
  (so the cache identity uses what is really shown).
- Presenter settings with unknown values were accepted; they answer 422 now.
- **Regressions found by the full regression, fixed:**
  - The presenter stand-in appeared in the Phase 8 image/video provider status (the providers panel, and the
    order and preferred maps): 3 Phase 8 backend tests failed and the providers check was 11/12. The status keeps
    its Phase 8 shape again, and presenter providers are in their own `presenter` section.
  - The page loaded presenter profiles at start-up even without a login. The 401 opened the login box over the
    page (for example Play Demo in the Phase 1 check). Profiles now load only once signed in (after login too),
    and the Phase 1 check stubs `/api/presenters` like the other APIs it stubs.
  - The one Phase 8 test that lists the test-mode stand-ins now includes the presenter stand-in. Its purpose, that
    no real provider is registered in test mode, is unchanged, and the video order is still asserted as before.
  - The presenter settings made the start screen 1119 px tall. It is centred in a fixed full-window box, so on an
    800 px window its top (title, Assets, My Videos) sat 130 px above the window and could not be scrolled to, and
    it shifted when the settings loaded. The Phase 3 check and both export checks timed out clicking those buttons.
    The start screen now keeps inside the window (`max-height`, scrolls inside), so nothing is cut off and nothing
    moves; this also fixes the slight overflow the screen already had before Phase 12.
  - The Aadhi preview cropped the middle of a 1280×720 poster (mostly empty wall); it now crops to Aadhi.
- The illustrated teacher's drawing defects listed above.

### Known limitations
- **No real AI presenter provider here.** Adding one (a paid avatar API, or a heavy local model) needs a new
  dependency and was not added without approval. The adapter interface, capability model, cache, recovery and
  review are ready for it.
- **Aadhi Teacher's mouth** follows loudness, not phonemes, so it is not lip sync. It is honestly labelled.
- **Picture-in-picture** makes the presenter small but does not widen the board: the page's layout presets are
  kept. A dedicated composition belongs to Phase 13.
- **Browser speech:** a scene with a drawn or AI presenter uses server TTS in preview too (needed to follow the
  audio). With the Default engine, the preview voice is then the server's Edge voice rather than the browser's.
- **Custom presenters** are a foundation only (a profile with a reference picture): no provider here accepts a
  reference picture, so they show as unavailable.

### Deferred to later phases
Phase 13 and beyond were not started (cinematic camera, composition engine, advanced backgrounds, transitions,
styling, quality engine, editor). The presenter plan (normalized box, layout, role, behaviour) and the capability
model are the extension points.

### Final status
**Complete.**
- **Verified end to end:** the Presenter Director, safe composition, the speech timeline from the lesson's own
  narration, the illustrated Aadhi Teacher, Visual Review presenter decisions, playback and export from the same
  saved plan. AI presenter clips were verified with the stand-in: explicit generation, cache hit, New Version,
  restart recovery of the same job, and cancellation.
- **Not verified:** a real AI presenter. No presenter-capable provider exists here, so the page says honestly that
  AI presenter generation isn't configured.
- **Unchanged by default:** existing lessons still play with the Aadhi mascot, placed by the lesson.
- **Nothing committed or deployed.** The database backup `projects.db.before-phase12` is kept.

---

## Phase 13 — Cinematic Scene Generation

### Objective
Compose the elements earlier phases produce (the router's educational visual, the Phase 12 presenter, the scene's
text, a background) into deliberately designed educational scenes: where each element goes, how large, when it
appears, how the camera frames it, and how scenes change. The composition is data (a plan per scene), rendered by
the page, and recorded by the export, so the preview and the video show the same scene. Educational content keeps
priority: the composer never selects visuals, never changes text, formulas or code, and never lets anything
important be covered, cropped or pushed into the subtitle band. Phase 14 (the reasoning composer) was not started.

### Baseline (start of Phase 13)
Backend 312/312, frontend 116/116, Phase 1 27/27, Phase 2 30/30, export with library assets 35/35, Phase 3 26/26,
Phase 4 24/24, Phase 5 11/11, Phase 6 17/17, Phase 7 18/18, Phase 8 12/12, Phase 9 14/14, Phase 10 17/17,
Phase 11 18/18, Phase 12 20/20.

### Audit (before Phase 13, from the code)
- **One renderer already.** The export records the page itself (tab capture, Phases 2 and 7), so whatever the page
  draws is what the video shows. Scenes live in `slides[]`; each carries `html`, `side_panel`, `narration` with
  `[SYNC]` / `[PAUSE]`, `aadhi_position`, and the saved `visual_plan` (Phase 4) and `presenter_plan` (Phase 12).
- **Layout** was four CSS variables (board left and width, side zone left and width) set per `aadhi_position`;
  beyond Phase 12's presenter boxes there was no safe-area model.
- **Existing "cinematic" pieces:** View Transitions between scenes (a fixed crossfade), a 20 s 1.05× zoom on the
  board, narration-synced text reveals (`[SYNC]`), MathJax formulas and Prism code. No reduced-motion handling.
- **Aadhi's clips are full-frame studio videos** (he stands at about x 0.68–0.97 in the right clip; the popup
  clip is also full-frame). He cannot be moved, scaled or put in front of another background.
- **AI images** already go through the Phase 5/8/9 media layer (cache, providers, runs, recovery); the cache
  identity is media type, provider, model, prompt and parameters.

### Architecture
```
 Screenplay scene (optional "composition" field) ─┬─ Visual Router (visuals.py) ─────► visual_plan      (unchanged)
                                                  ├─ Presenter Director (presenters.py) ► presenter_plan (unchanged)
                                                  └─ Cinematic composer (cinematic.py)  ► cinematic_plan
      template: Visual Review choice → screenplay choice → fixed table by the scene's role (no reasoning)
      layers on the normalized 16:9 frame: background · visual · board · presenter · labels · title · subtitles
      safe areas: title band, subtitle band (y ≥ 0.85), content area, presenter zone; nothing important overlaps
      camera: a move only as far as every important layer stays whole and clear of the title and the subtitles
      timeline (seconds, or a [SYNC] reveal) · the lesson's transition · fingerprint + plan hash
                                   │   POST /api/cinematic/plan (never generates)
 Page (cinematic.js, CinematicStage) ── renders the plan with the page's own elements:
      board (#presentation-board), side panel (the router's visual), presenter layer (presenter.js) placed in
      the plan's boxes; background, title overlay and label chips added; one camera transform per layer (all
      from the same framing); entrances, labels and emphasis on the scene clock / narration beats
                                   │
 Playback = preview = export (the recorder captures this page) ── Visual Review: a "composition" item per scene
      (scene inspector: drawn to scale, facts, element states, keep / change / back to automatic)
 Optional AI background ── POST /api/cinematic/background → the AI image layer (cache, providers, runs, recovery)
```

### The composition plan (`scene.cinematic_plan`, cinematic.py)
- **Fields:** `version`, `template` (+ label and the reason it was chosen), `shot`, `style` (the lesson's),
  `background`, `layers`, `camera`, `transition`, `duration`, `timeline`, `safe_areas`, `presenter` (who, shown,
  side), `notes`, `warnings`, `review_status` / `review_stale`, `fingerprint`, `plan_hash`.
- **Layers** each have an id, type, role, normalized box (`x, y, w, h` in 0..1 of the frame), order (`z`),
  visibility, opacity, scale, start / end (seconds), enter / exit animation, whether it is important, and whether
  the camera moves it. They point to content, never copy it: the visual layer to `visual_plan`, the presenter
  layer to `presenter_plan`, text layers to the scene's own `title` / `subtitle` / `html`, labels to the
  screenplay's `composition.labels` by index.
- **Order:** background 0 · visual 20 · board 30 · presenter 40 · labels 50 · title 60 · subtitles 90 (reserved).
- **Validation** (`validate_plan`): boxes on the frame, known types and animations, sane timing and opacity,
  important layers not overlapping each other or the subtitle band, known camera / shot / transition, and camera
  framings that keep important layers whole. The composer runs it as a safety net on every plan.
- **Explicit composition** (optional screenplay field, added to the generation prompt): `template`,
  `presenter_position` (left / right / hidden), `visual_position`, `camera`, `shot`, `focus`, `transition`,
  `background`, up to 6 `labels` (`text`, `at`: seconds or `{"sync": n}`) and `emphasis` (`target`, `at`).
  Unknown values are dropped with a warning; nothing fails.
- **Classic** (the default scene style) produces no plan: existing lessons render exactly as before.

### Templates (data)
| Template | Presenter | Content | Default camera |
|---|---|---|---|
| presenter_intro | large, beside | hero title, subtitle, short text | slow zoom in towards the title |
| presenter_explanation | side | text board | slow zoom in |
| presenter_plus_visual | side | visual beside the text, labels under the visual | slow zoom in towards the visual |
| diagram_focus | small (picture-in-picture) | large visual, compact text, labels | focus on the visual |
| formula_focus | small, secondary | formula board (MathJax, enlarged), labels | focus on the formula (measured once typeset) |
| code_focus | hidden | code board (Prism, enlarged) | still |
| visual_focus | hidden | the main visual (AI video 16:9, or full canvas for simulations) | still unless asked |
| quiz | side | question, answers, countdown | still |
| summary | side | takeaways (with the visual when there is one) | slow zoom out |

- **Automatic choice** (only when neither Visual Review nor the screenplay names one) uses the Phase 12 scene
  role: intro → presenter_intro, explanation → presenter_explanation, visual → presenter_plus_visual, math →
  formula_focus, code → code_focus, quiz → quiz, summary → summary, technical / video → visual_focus. An intro with
  a visual becomes presenter_plus_visual, so a chosen visual is never dropped by the automatic choice.
- **Mirrored** for a presenter on the left. **Aadhi (the mascot)** keeps his studio clip's own place (content
  beside him), is never made small (the scene then shows the visual without him), and keeps the studio background.

### Safe areas and positions
- Normalized frame, 16:9. Title band y 0.05–0.15 (the title lines up with the content column under it); content
  y 0.19–0.81; subtitle band y ≥ 0.85 (the page's `#subtitle-track`); presenter zone x 0.70–0.97.
- Boxes are computed from the template (never free pixels), then checked: no important layer overlaps another or
  the subtitle band; the presenter's face (the top 42 % of its box) is what must stay clear.

### Camera
- **Moves:** static, slow_zoom_in, slow_zoom_out, pan_left / right / up / down, focus. **Shots:** wide, medium,
  close, presenter, visual, split, full_canvas (they set the zoom cap: 1.03–1.10; never above 1.12).
- **Safety:** a framing is allowed only if every important layer, as the camera shows it, stays whole, at least
  2 % from the frame's edge, and clear of the title overlay and the subtitle band (checked along the whole path).
  The composer takes the largest safe zoom nearest the target; when none exists the move becomes static with the
  reason ("no room to move without cropping content"). Typical moves are 1.05–1.07×.
- The title and subtitles are overlays the camera never moves (like broadcast graphics). The page applies one
  transform per layer (`translate(vw, vh) scale`, origin top-left), all computed from the same framing, so the
  layers move as one picture. Formula focus re-aims at the formula's measured place once MathJax has typeset it,
  under the same rules.
- **Still camera when:** Aadhi's studio clip is on screen (it cannot move with the camera), the lesson style's
  motion is "none", or the viewer prefers reduced motion (preview only; the export follows the plan).

### Timing and animation
- The scene's duration is estimated from the narration (2.6 words/s plus its pauses; at least 4 s).
- Entrances: title fade / slide, visual scale-in, presenter slide-in from its side, board fade; labels slide in at
  their time or at their `[SYNC]` reveal (the estimate is only a late safety net); emphasis is a short gold pulse
  on the formula, visual or a label. With motion "none" or reduced motion, entrances are plain fades.
- The stage keeps a scene clock that holds with the narration (animations pause, pending events wait) and
  resumes with it; every narration beat Aadhi hears (`[SYNC]`, hold, resume) drives the timeline too.
- Board text that does not fit its box is made smaller in small steps, never below 80 %; if it still does not
  fit, that is reported.

### Transitions
- One kind per lesson (the style), unless a scene's composition asks for another: **fade** (a short dip),
  **crossfade**, **slide** (3 % with a fade), **cut** (no transition at all). 0.5–0.6 s, on the page's existing
  View Transitions; the narration starts as before (transitions never delay it). Slide becomes fade without motion.

### Backgrounds
- **gradient** (the default, the page's deep purple / indigo with a soft vignette), **solid**, **studio** (Aadhi's
  clip), a **picture or clip from the Asset Library** (by `asset_id`, under a scrim so text stays readable), or an
  **AI background** (below). A missing or inaccessible asset falls back to the gradient, with a note and an export
  warning; another user's asset is never shown.
- **Aadhi's lessons keep the studio** (he is filmed in it); other backgrounds show with the Aadhi Teacher, an AI
  presenter or no presenter. When a background covers the studio, Aadhi's clip is paused (`mascot.suspend`), so
  no hidden video is decoded, and recording cannot restart it.

### AI background (optional, explicit)
- `POST /api/cinematic/background` → `background_request()` → the existing AI image layer: provider choice,
  explicit providers never silently replaced (fallback only when allowed), the AI cache, durable runs, recovery,
  and the Asset Library. The prompt is the style's words plus the user's wish, always calm and empty ("no text, no
  people"); the cache identity is that prompt + provider + model + parameters (16:9), so the same style is reused
  and another style makes another background; "Generate a new background" is `force_regenerate`.
- Generated only when the user clicks it (choosing "AI background" makes nothing). A run finished in the
  background (or after a restart) lands in the saved lesson's scenes that wait for it.

### Presenter and visual integration
- The presenter plan (Phase 12) still decides who presents, whether they appear, their behaviour, expression,
  gesture and speech. The composition decides the box, size, visibility in this template, order and entrance, and
  hands the box to `presenterStage` (`presenterPlanFor`).
- The visual plan (Phase 4) still decides what the visual is. The composition places the page's side panel (or
  the board, for a main visual) in the visual box; a scene whose visual was removed in Visual Review has no visual
  layer. In a cinematic scene the visual appears at once (the classic view first shows the Skill Tree for 3 s).
  Diagrams are shown whole (`object-fit: contain`), never cropped.

### Visual Review (Phase 6, extended)
- A **composition** item per scene, stored in `scene.visual_review.composition` (`status`, `overrides`,
  `fingerprint`, `reviewed_at`), applied first by the composer.
- **Scene inspector:** the composition drawn to scale (layers, the subtitle band, where the camera ends), the facts
  (template, presenter, camera, transition, background, duration, layers and their times), each element's state
  (✓ ready, ● generating, ⚠ needs attention: AI background generating, a missing presenter clip, a visual not
  generated or unavailable, a background that fell back), notes, and **Keep**, **Change** (template, presenter
  side or hidden, visual side, camera, transition, background → Apply), **Back to automatic**, and **Preview this
  scene** (plays it in the lesson, the same renderer the export records). Opening it generates nothing.
- **Fingerprint:** the scene's content, its visual and presenter, its explicit composition and the lesson style.
  When it changes (the presenter moved, the visual changed, the style changed) an approval becomes pending and is
  flagged; an explicit change is kept and flagged. `POST /api/cinematic/review`.

### Start screen
Scene style (Classic / Cinematic) and Transitions; in Cinematic also Background (Automatic, Aadhi's studio, Clean
gradient, Solid colour, Picture / Clip from Asset Library, AI background) and Camera motion (Subtle / None), with a
"Choose from Asset Library" or "Generate AI background" button where it applies and honest notes (what Aadhi's
studio means for backgrounds and the camera). Kept per browser, like the presenter settings.

### Configuration and API
| Endpoint | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `GET /api/cinematic` | the vocabulary: templates, camera moves, shots, transitions, backgrounds, styles, animations, text roles, safe areas |
| `POST /api/cinematic/plan` | the composition of every scene (Classic: none); never generates. 422 bad settings, 413 over 200 scenes, 404 another user's lesson |
| `POST /api/cinematic/review` | keep / change / reset a scene's composition (saved with the lesson). 422 unknown action or override, 404 another user's lesson or scene |
| `POST /api/cinematic/background` | an AI background (cache, runs, recovery); 403 when AI generation is off |
| `GET /cinematic.js` | the page module |

No new setting or database change: plans and decisions live in the lesson JSON; the background asset is recorded
as a lesson reference (`scenes[i].cinematic_plan.background`), so a used background cannot be deleted.

### Files changed
- **New:** `cinematic.py` (composer, validation, camera safety, timeline, background, review, API), `cinematic.js`
  (settings, stage, camera maths, inspector words, settings panel), `tests/test_cinematic.py`,
  `tests/cinematic.test.js`, `tests/cinematic_browser_check.mjs`, `tests/fixtures/cinematic_diagrams.py`.
- **Changed:** `server.py` (router, `/cinematic.js`, the background completion hook), `assets.py` (background
  reference), `mascot.js` (`suspend`), `review.js` (composition item and scene inspector), `index.html` (module,
  styles, settings block, scene hooks, transitions, planning, export preparation, review adapter, the optional
  `composition` field in the generation prompt), `tests/mascot.test.js` (+1).

|---|---|
| Backend (unittest) | **337/337** (312 before + 25 Phase 13) |
| Frontend (node --test) | **133/133** (116 before + 16 Phase 13 + 1 mascot) |
| Phase 1 mascot | 27/27 |
| Phase 2 export | 30/30 |
| Export with library assets | 35/35 (re-run; see below) |

In the full run the library-asset export was 34/35: its check "frames of every scene differ" compares JPEG file
sizes, and two different scenes (the title and the quiz, checked by eye and byte for byte) happened to compress to
exactly 19,532 bytes. The recording was correct. Run again unchanged: 35/35. The test was not changed.

- **Measured:** the cinematic export delivered 1,707 frames in 58.5 s (29.2 of 30 fps). A cinematic scene with a
  camera move ran at 61–63 page fps with the glass blur and 76–79 without it (three samples each, headless Chrome;
  the blur was then removed in composed scenes).
- The export recorded all seven templates; a recorded frame of scene B matched the preview (mean difference 10.8
  of 255 on a 32×18 grid, the diagram in the same place).

### Real provider validation
**None.** No real image or presenter provider is available here (Pollinations answers 402, no Gemini or OpenAI
key). The AI background was generated only by the local stand-in (a flat colour with a white box: it proves the
pipeline, not the look); no real request was made, nothing real was downloaded or inspected. Gradients, solid
colours and Asset Library pictures need no provider and were inspected for real.

### Visual quality validation
Scenes rendered in real Chrome (the page the export records) with the Aadhi Teacher, the gradient background and the
lesson's own diagrams (two plain diagrams drawn for the check), screenshots inspected at 1280×720 and 1920×1080:
- **A (intro):** hero title and subtitle, a short text card, the teacher large beside it; slow push-in.
- **B (presenter + visual):** the diagram whole on the left, the text beside it, the teacher on the right, the label
  under the diagram, the title aligned with the content column.
- **C (diagram focus):** the leaf diagram large, compact text, the teacher small in the corner, the camera focused
  on the diagram (1.065×, everything still whole).
- **D (formula):** F = ma typeset by MathJax and enlarged (about 2.5× the body text), the explanation under it, the
  labels F = Force, m = Mass, a = Acceleration below, the teacher secondary; focus towards the formula.
- **E (code):** the code whole, highlighted and enlarged on a dark panel, no presenter, a still camera.
- **F (quiz):** question, answers and countdown in the board, the teacher encouraging beside it.
- **G (visual only):** the clip large in a 16:9 frame, no presenter, a gentle focus.
- **Aadhi's lessons:** the studio background with the title on a dark card, content beside Aadhi, a still camera.

Measured in every scene: title, board, visual, presenter and labels never overlap each other or the subtitles
(720p and 1080p); text fits without shrinking. **Preview versus export:** a recorded frame of scene B compared with
the preview screenshot: the same layout (mean difference 10.8 of 255 on a 32×18 grid; the diagram in the same
place). The stand-in AI background (a flat colour with a white box) was inspected only as proof of the pipeline.

The first renders were not accepted; the defects found by looking are in "Issues found and fixed".

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- **Found by looking at the renders:**
  - The board ignored its box height (the page sets `height: fit-content` inline), so the quiz board ran into
    the subtitle band and scene B looked unbalanced. Boards now fill their box; the intro card still hugs its text.
  - The classic view shows the Skill Tree for 3 s before a scene's visual, so composed scenes showed the Skill
    Tree where the diagram belonged. In a cinematic scene the visual now appears at once.
  - With the side panel's floating animation off, its base style (95 %, 10 px down) returned and misaligned the
    visual card. Removed in cinematic scenes.
  - Side images used `object-fit: cover`, which cut a diagram's label off. Diagrams are now shown whole.
  - The formula (39 px) and the code (16 px) were not dominant. Now MathJax at 250 % and code at 1.9vw.
  - Board text stayed small at 1080p. It now scales with the frame.
  - Gold label chips were unreadable on Aadhi's white studio wall. They get a dark backing there.
  - After a push-in, content came within 18 px of the frame's edge. The camera keeps 2 % now.
- **Found by measuring:** the glass blur, recomputed every frame of a camera move, cost about a fifth of the page's
  frame rate (62 → 78 fps with it removed). Composed scenes drop it; Classic keeps its look.
- **Found by the tests:**
  - Switching the background kind kept the previous picture's asset, so a library picture could have posed as the
    AI background. Any switch now clears it.
  - The text fit could shrink to 76 % despite an 80 % floor. The floor is now enforced, and an overflow is reported.
  - Aadhi stands further in on his left clip than a mirrored right clip, so the board touched him. His margins are
    now per clip.
  - A full-canvas simulation was flagged as reaching the subtitle band. It is the canvas; the check skips it.
  - In the scene inspector, the "Camera" fact shared a class with the camera outline and was drawn out of place.
- **Test harness:**
  - The stand-in's images come back in one call (no provider job), so the recovery test cuts the server off after
    the download. Recovery then finished from the downloaded file with no second provider call.
  - Two browser-check steps were fixed for timing: reduced motion read the scene the lesson start showed, and the
    1080p pass compared across a presenter move made on purpose. Neither was a product problem.

### Known limitations
- **Aadhi (the mascot)** is filmed in full-frame studio clips: in his lessons the background stays the studio, the
  camera stays still while he is on screen, and he cannot be made small (a picture-in-picture template shows the
  visual without him). Full composition needs the Aadhi Teacher, an AI presenter or no presenter.
- **Timing is estimated** from the narration's words (a speaking pace); `[SYNC]`-anchored labels and emphasis follow
  the real narration, the camera's duration follows the estimate.
- **Label and emphasis text** come only from the screenplay's `composition` field; the composer invents none.
- **Background clips** from the library work but decode alongside the scene (a cost measured only for pictures).
- **The inspector** changes whole-scene choices (template, sides, camera, transition, background); it is not an
  editor (no keyframes, no timeline editing: Phase 19).
- **No real AI background** was validated (no provider here).

### Deferred to later phases
Phase 14 (the intelligent composer that reasons about focus and timing), Phase 17 (the full styling system), Phase
19 (the editor) and later were not started. The plan's layers, safe areas, camera safety and fingerprints are the
extension points.

### Final status
**Complete.**
- **Verified end to end:** composed scenes (templates, safe areas, camera, timeline, transitions, backgrounds, labels)
  in preview, playback and export from the same saved plans; the scene inspector in Visual Review with stale
  approvals; the presenter (Phase 12) and the visual (Phase 4) placed, not replaced; the AI background through the
  AI media layer, cache and recovery (stand-in provider).
- **Not verified:** a real AI background or presenter (no provider here).
- **Unchanged by default:** the Classic scene style renders every lesson exactly as before.
- **Nothing committed or deployed.** No database change (plans live in the lesson JSON).
