## Phase 14 — Intelligent Scene Composer

### Objective
Decide automatically how each scene's existing building blocks are arranged: what the scene teaches, which element
deserves the most attention, whether the presenter helps and how large, where the visual goes and how large, how the
text is sized, what the camera does, how much motion is right, what is emphasised and when. The output is a Phase 13
composition plan, rendered by the Phase 13 page (and therefore the same in the preview and the export). Deterministic
by default; a text model may help with ambiguous scenes, constrained to enum values and checked by the rules. Phase 15
(the AI Visual Director: how a concept is taught visually) was not started.

### Baseline (start of Phase 14)
Backend 337/337, frontend 133/133, Phase 1 27/27, Phase 2 30/30, export with library assets 35/35, Phase 3 26/26,
Phase 4 24/24, Phase 5 11/11, Phase 6 17/17, Phase 7 18/18, Phase 8 12/12, Phase 9 14/14, Phase 10 17/17,
Phase 11 18/18, Phase 12 20/20, Phase 13 24/24.

### Audit (before Phase 14, from the code)
- Phase 13 chose a template from a fixed table by the Phase 12 scene role; the placements and the visual/text split were
  fixed per template, so it could not express a smaller presenter, a larger visual, a motion level or information
  density.
- Scene facts already structured (no model needed): the scene type (title, content, example, key-takeaway, summary,
  recap, quiz_checkpoint, chapter_card, simulation, ai_video, p5), the board markup (definitions, formula blocks,
  `pre/code`, tables, lists, `takeaway-list`), the router's `visual_plan` (source, media, renderer, `asset_id`), the Asset
  Library's width and height, the Phase 12 presenter plan and capabilities. Phase 11's formula detector is reused.
- Text models: only Phase 11's `call_model` (Gemini / OpenAI, JSON mode, low temperature), `model_available`,
  `parse_json` and its one-repair pattern. No keys on this machine.

### Architecture
```
 scene ─► scene intent (scene_intent.py): purpose · content · density · media (visual kind, aspect; presenter type,
          transparency, clip) · priority order · weights · ambiguity · reading time · short reasons · hash
       ─► candidate decisions (composer.py): 1–3 from the rules for the purpose; in AI-assisted mode, for an ambiguous
          scene, one more from the model (enum values only, validated, one repair)
             template · presenter role (dominant / secondary / small / hidden) and side · visual role (fullscreen /
             dominant / secondary / side_panel / hidden) and share · board size · camera · motion level (none / subtle
             / moderate) · emphasis · timing · transition
       ─► explicit choices on top: the screenplay's composition, then the user's (Visual Review)
       ─► each candidate rendered by Phase 13 (cinematic.build_plan), validated (Phase 13 rules + readability),
          tightened, repaired (bounded), scored; the best valid one wins; otherwise the safe fallback
       ─► the Phase 13 plan + composition record {source, decision, locked choices, reasons, repairs, intent, ai}
          ─► the Phase 13 page (preview = playback = export) · Visual Review (scene inspector)
```
Priority: hard constraints > the user's choices (Visual Review) > the screenplay's composition > lesson settings > the
composer's decision > the safe fallback. A lesson set to no motion keeps every camera still, whoever chose the move. Composing never generates media and never calls the AI media cache.

### Scene intent (`scene_intent.py`)
- **Purposes:** intro, transition, definition, explanation, example, diagram, formula, code, process, comparison, quiz,
  summary, recap, demonstration, decided by an ordered rule list (the order is part of the explanation): quiz scene →
  quiz; title / chapter card → intro or transition; simulation / AI video → demonstration; recap; summary / takeaways;
  the first scene → intro; code; comparison (a "vs / versus / compare" title, a table of two or more columns, two lists);
  formula; definition; process (a list of three or more steps, ordered or with step words in the narration); example; a visual with little text →
  diagram; otherwise explanation.
- **Content flags:** definition, formula, example, code, diagram, chart, image, video, animation, table, process, quiz,
  list, from the board's HTML (parsed, never interpreted) and the visual plan.
- **Density** (low / medium / high) and **reading time** from words, code lines, table cells and formulas.
- **Media:** the visual's kind (image, diagram, chart, video, animation, …), aspect (the asset's own size from the
  library; defaults per kind otherwise) and orientation; the presenter's type, availability (an AI presenter without
  a clip is not available) and transparency.
- **Priority** (the elements in the order they deserve attention, e.g. formula → visual → text → presenter) and
  **weights** for visual, presenter and text; **ambiguity** when two primaries compete (formula and diagram, code and a
  table, a comparison with a visual, dense text with a visual and a presenter).

### Decisions, candidates and scoring (`composer.py`)
- **Rules per purpose** (first choice first; one to three per purpose): introduction → presenter large beside the hero
  title (with a side visual: the presenter beside the visual); definition /
  explanation / example / process → the text with the presenter beside it, or beside its visual (a definition's visual
  supports its words); diagram → the diagram large, the presenter small in the corner; formula → the formula board, the
  presenter small; code → the code with the presenter away and a still camera; comparison → both sides symmetric, the
  presenter small beside them, a still camera; quiz → the question leads, the presenter beside it; summary → the key
  points with the presenter; demonstration → the visual takes the stage. High density: no camera motion and a smaller
  or hidden presenter preferred.
- **Scoring** (deterministic, weights in code): educational fit of the template for the purpose, hierarchy (one clear
  primary element; a standing figure counts half), readability (how much text would shrink), presenter role against
  its weight, density, motion appropriateness (the rules never move the camera for code, quizzes or comparisons, and a
  moving camera there scores lower), consistency (the presenter keeps the lesson's side; three identical layouts in a
  row are avoided when an equal choice exists), balance (no mostly empty card). Ties go to the candidates' order (an AI
  suggestion first, then the rules' order): never random.
- **Media-aware layout:** the visual's share of the width follows its own shape at the height it gets (a square picture
  a square box, a wide picture a wide box, never stretched), within its role's bounds; a wide picture in a column gets a
  card of its own shape; short text gets a card sized to it, centred beside its visual or in its band; label chips go
  below a compact card.
- **Timing:** the presenter enters after the title in an introduction and after the question in a quiz; in a diagram
  scene the visual right after the board, the presenter later; labels one after another with time to read each; the
  formula emphasised at the scene's first `[SYNC]` reveal and the camera leaning in then; the visual emphasised when
  the narration points at it ("look at this diagram").
- **Explicit choices:** a layout chosen by the user or the screenplay keeps its own arrangement (its presenter role as
  designed, e.g. "Presenter + visual": the presenter beside the content); the composer still sizes the visual and
  repairs. The screenplay's layout, presenter side, visual side, camera, shot, focus and transition apply; a user's
  visible presenter side brings back a presenter the screenplay hid.

### Validation, repair and fallback
- **Hard constraints** (nothing overrides them): every Phase 13 rule (boxes on the frame, nothing important
  overlapping, the subtitle band clear, the camera never cropping) and readability (the board's text, estimated from the
  page's own type sizes, never shrinks below 80 %; code by its widest line too).
- **Repairs**, one step at a time, recorded in the plan: a still camera (when the camera is the problem); the presenter
  steps back, then small; the visual from dominant to secondary; the presenter away; the visual to a side panel. A
  user's presenter, visual or camera choice that breaks a hard constraint is repaired and the inspector says so ("your
  choice could not be kept"); a layout or motion choice that cannot work leaves only the safe fallback.
- **Fallback** (explicit choices are not applied to it, and the inspector says so): the main content centred, the
  presenter small if there is room, the title at the top, the subtitles at the bottom, a still camera; and as the last
  resort the Phase 13 layout. A lesson never fails because of the composer.

### AI-assisted composition (optional)
- **Mode:** "Composition: Automatic / AI-assisted" (Automatic by default: no model call at all).
- **Only ambiguous scenes** are asked (at most 6 per lesson, in parallel, within a 25 s budget: a late answer is not
  waited for; a scene past the sixth is recorded as not asked). The brief is the structured intent plus short excerpts,
  declared data, not instructions.
- **Provider-independent:** Phase 11's `call_model` for Gemini or OpenAI (the page's provider choice); the composer's own
  stand-in model on test servers.
- **Output:** one JSON object with enum values only (template, presenter role and side, visual role, text priority,
  camera, motion, emphasis; the presenter side and the text priority are checked but not used: the lesson and the
  presenter plan decide the side). Parsed, checked against the enums, one
  bounded repair (the malformed answer is sent back, not the scene), otherwise the rules decide. Unknown fields are
  ignored: their values are never used or stored (only their names are listed in the AI record); the model never
  emits code, HTML, CSS or coordinates. The validated suggestion becomes one more candidate, scored and constrained like
  the others (a small bonus); it never hides the scene's visual and never moves the camera more than the lesson allows.
  Failure, timeout or no key leaves the rules in charge, with the reason recorded.
- **Kept with the lesson** (the plan's `composition.ai`, keyed by the intent hash, provider, model and composer
  version) and reused on the next plan and by Visual Review's Keep and Change (a review never asks the model);
  "Regenerate composition" asks again, about that scene only.

### Visual Review and the scene inspector (extended)
- "Composition: Automatic ✓ · Presenter + visual" (or AI-assisted, Your choice, As the screenplay asks, Safe layout),
  up to 4 short factual reasons, what was adjusted, and the AI outcome.
- Overrides: layout, presenter (side or hidden), presenter size, visual size, visual side, camera, motion, transition,
  background; "Automatic" gives a choice back to the composer (offered for the user's own choices; a screenplay choice
  is labelled "as the screenplay asks"). A "Composition" filter lists the compositions alone.
- Keep and Change compose the scene as the lesson does (its neighbours, the AI suggestion it kept), so what is approved
  is what was shown.
- **Regenerate composition:** decides the composition again (in AI-assisted mode with a fresh suggestion); never media;
  an approval is withdrawn (a regenerated composition needs a new look); the user's choices stay (and the message says
  so).
- Compositions are never approved automatically; the fingerprint now includes the composer's version and, when a
  model's suggestion decided the composition, the model, so an approval made for another composer's plan re-opens.

### Comparison layout
A `comparison` template (data): the board with a table at full width and equal columns, or two lists side by side, the
presenter small beside it, a still camera.

### API
| Endpoint | Change |
|---|---|
| `POST /api/cinematic/plan` | composed by the Intelligent Scene Composer; settings `composer` (rules / ai), `composer_provider`, `composer_model`; plans carry `composition` |
| `POST /api/cinematic/regenerate` | one scene's composition decided again (no media; an approval withdrawn) |
| `POST /api/cinematic/review` | overrides also `presenter_size`, `visual_size`, `motion`; `"auto"` removes a choice |
| `GET /api/cinematic` | `composer`: modes and whether AI-assisted composition can run, per provider |

No database change; the composition lives in the lesson's plans.

### Files changed
- **New:** `scene_intent.py`, `composer.py`, `tests/test_composer.py`, `tests/composer.test.js`,
  `tests/composer_browser_check.mjs`, `tests/helpers/cinematic-dom.js` (the fake DOM, moved out of `cinematic.test.js`).
- **Changed:** `cinematic.py` (`build_plan` renders a decision; `phase13_decision`; the comparison template; layout
  shares, compact cards and picture-shaped visuals; the composer in the fingerprint; code boards full width without a
  presenter; Keep and Change composed in the lesson's context; the new endpoint and settings), `cinematic.js` (the
  composition summary, the Composition setting, the regenerate call, a stricter text fit), `review.js` (the
  inspector's composition block, overrides with "Automatic", regeneration, the Composition filter), `index.html`
  (composer settings, the review adapter, comparison and inspector styles), `presenters.py` (a malformed visual plan is
  no longer a crash), `tests/mascot_browser_check.mjs` (stubs the new `GET /api/cinematic` call),
  `tests/test_cinematic.py` (one expectation updated: an explicit layout now keeps the visual when it can give it room),
  `tests/cinematic.test.js` (uses the shared fake DOM), `tests/cinematic_browser_check.mjs` (scene B asks for the
  presenter + visual layout it checks).

### Results
Full silent regression on the final code (Chrome with `--disable-audio-output`, speech stubbed), after the fixes from
the end-of-phase review:

| Suite | Result |
|---|---|
| Backend (unittest) | **373/373** (337 before + 36 Phase 14) |
| Frontend (node --test) | **140/140** (133 before + 7 Phase 14) |
| Phase 1 mascot | 27/27 (its API stubs now include `GET /api/cinematic`; see Issues) |
| Phase 2 export | 30/30 |
| Export with library assets | 35/35 |
| Phase 3 assets | 26/26 |
| Phase 4 visual router | 24/24 |
| Phase 5 AI cache | 11/11 |
| Phase 6 Visual Review | 17/17 |
| Phase 7 recording performance (720p) | 18/18 (28.5 fps effective, 0 stalls, page 127.6 fps) |
| Phase 8 providers | 12/12 |
| Phase 9 recovery | 14/14 |
| Phase 10 Manim sandbox | 17/17 |
| Phase 11 source documents | 18/18 |
| Phase 12 presenter | 20/20 |
| Phase 13 cinematic | 24/24 |
| **Phase 14 browser check** | **17/17** |
| Static checks | 6 inline scripts and all JS files parse; browser checks parse; Python modules compile |

The first full run (before the review fixes) had two failures, both fixed and passing in the run above: the Phase 1
mascot check (the login box opened on the new status request) and the Phase 13 check's "presenter + visual" scene (an
explicit layout got the diagram scene's small presenter). Both are described under Issues.

### LLM validation
**Not done for real.** No text model is configured here (no Gemini or OpenAI key). AI-assisted composition was verified
only with the composer's stand-in model (valid, malformed-then-repaired, malformed, fabricated and failing answers),
and with a real provider name and no key (unavailable, the rules decide). No real request was made.

### Visual quality validation
The mini-lesson (introduction, definition, explanation, diagram, formula, code, comparison, quiz, summary; the lesson's
own diagrams, the Aadhi Teacher) was composed automatically, rendered in real Chrome at 1280×720 and 1920×1080, and
every screenshot inspected over four rounds of fixes (the first renders were not accepted; see Issues):
- **Introduction:** the hero title and subtitle, a short text card, the teacher large beside them; a slow push-in.
- **Definition:** the leaf diagram in a card of its own (wide) shape, the definition in a card sized to it and centred
  beside it, the teacher beside both; the text leads, the diagram supports it.
- **Explanation:** a compact text card with the teacher beside it.
- **Diagram:** the diagram in the largest area, the key points beside it, the teacher small in the corner, the label
  under the diagram; the camera leans in once the narration points at it.
- **Formula:** F = ma dominant on a compact board with its explanation line, the labels below the card, the teacher
  secondary; the camera leans in at the formula's reveal.
- **Code:** the code whole and highlighted, no presenter, a still camera.
- **Comparison:** the table at full width with equal columns (358 / 358 px), the teacher small beside it, a still camera.
- **Quiz:** the question leads, the answers and the countdown, the teacher coming in after the question; the reveal
  plays through.
- **Summary:** the key points in a compact card with the teacher wrapping up.

Measured in every scene at both sizes: nothing overlaps, and no text shrinks below 80 % (the page's own fit reported
0.84–1.0). A user's choice that would have made text unreadable was repaired and said so. **Preview versus export:** a
recorded frame of the diagram scene matches the preview (mean difference 5.9 of 255 on a 32×18 grid; 5 in the final run), and the recording
ran through all nine compositions.

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- **Found by looking at the renders:**
  - A square diagram got a wide box with empty bands; a wide diagram got a tall, narrow card. The visual's width and
    height now follow its own shape.
  - A one-sentence definition sat in a tall, mostly empty card beside its diagram. Short text now gets a card sized to
    it, centred beside the visual.
  - A definition scene gave the diagram the dominant role and squeezed the definition into a narrow column. In a
    definition the words lead; its visual supports them.
  - The formula's explanation line was clipped: the label row was cut out of the compact card, and the page's fit
    check missed it (a board that centres its content hides overflow at both ends). Labels now go below a compact card,
    and the page's fit also measures the content against the board's inner height. The formula's height estimate was
    too low and now uses the measured size.
- **Found by the tests:**
  - The composer ignored the Phase 12 presenter side, including a side the user set in Visual Review.
  - The composer could hide Aadhi (the mascot): its hierarchy counted his whole studio region, and it penalized the
    still camera his clip forces.
  - A board's code was also counted as prose, so code scenes looked twice as tall and one fell back to the safe layout.
  - Paragraph breaks were not counted, so the text estimate was too generous (13 paragraphs "fitted" beside a
    full-size presenter).
  - A code board without a presenter was 80 % wide; it now uses the full content width so wide code stays readable.
  - The start screen said AI-assisted composition was available because a stand-in model exists on the test server,
    while the page would use Gemini, which has no key. The note now describes the provider the page would use.
  - A presenter choice made where the Presenter Director leaves the presenter out was recorded silently as "dominant".
    It is now recorded as hidden, with a note that the choice cannot apply.
- **Found by an independent code review and a fact-check of this document against the code** (two read-only review
  agents, at the end of the phase; each finding verified, fixed and covered by a new test):
  - The 25 s AI budget was recorded but not honoured: the request still waited for every model call. A late answer is
    no longer waited for, and a scene past the sixth is recorded as not asked.
  - Regenerating one scene could ask the model about other scenes and skip the one requested (past the sixth). It now
    asks about that scene only.
  - Keep and Change in Visual Review re-composed the scene alone, without its neighbours or the AI suggestion, so the
    approved plan could differ from the one shown (and the suggestion was lost). They now compose it as the lesson does,
    without asking the model.
  - An approval survived when the AI's suggestion replaced the rules' composition. The fingerprint now names the model
    when its suggestion decided.
  - An AI answer could hide the scene's visual, raise the motion above the lesson's and move the presenter off the
    lesson's side (its bonus outweighed the consistency penalty). It can no longer do any of these.
  - A screenplay's hidden presenter outranked the user's "left" / "right"; the user's choice now brings the presenter back.
  - The screenplay's shot and focus were dropped by the composer; they apply again.
  - A layout chosen explicitly got the composer's presenter role for the scene's purpose (the Phase 13 check's
    "Presenter + visual" scene showed the presenter small under a wide card). An explicit layout now keeps its own
    arrangement.
  - "Automatic" was offered (and labelled "your choice") for a screenplay's choice, where it could not undo it. It is
    now offered for the user's own choices only.
  - The fallback set a user's choices aside silently; the inspector now says so. Regenerating around a user's changes
    said "it needs your review"; it now says the choices are kept.
  - Smaller: the list of ignored field names from a model is capped; a malformed visual plan no longer crashes the
    last-resort layout.
  - The document's own claims were corrected where the code differed (candidate counts, the repair order, tie-breaking,
    timing, density, the process rule).
- **Test fixtures and expectations** (none weakened):
  - Two fixture narrations put `[SYNC]` after the text it reveals; the screenplay rules put it before.
  - The Phase 13 check's scene B now asks explicitly for the presenter + visual layout it exercises (the composer
    would choose the diagram focus for that short-text scene; its own choices are checked by the Phase 14 check).
  - One Phase 13 expectation changed with the behaviour: an explicitly chosen quiz layout now keeps the scene's visual
    (it gets a share) instead of dropping it with a warning. A layout with truly no room still warns, and is tested.
  - The Phase 1 mascot check failed in the full run: the page now asks for the composer's status
    (`GET /api/cinematic`) once signed in, and that check signs in with a stand-in token, so the refusal opened the
    login box over the page. The check stubs every API it doesn't test; this one was added to its list (the same fix
    Phase 12 made for `/api/presenters`).
- **Review:** the Visual Review list mixes three items per scene; a "Composition" filter was added.

### Known limitations
- **Text size is estimated** from the page's type sizes (verified against real renders: every scene's text fits without
  shrinking below 80 %); the page's own text fit remains the safety net.
- **Aadhi (the mascot)** keeps his studio constraints (no small presenter, a still camera while he is on screen).
- **The AI suggestion** only chooses among the existing building blocks; how a concept is taught visually is Phase 15.
- **Consistency** covers the presenter's side and repeated layouts; lesson-wide styling is Phase 17.

### Deferred to later phases
Phase 15 (AI Visual Director), Phase 16 (presenter/visual synchronization), Phase 17 (styling system), Phase 18 (quality
engine), Phase 19 (editor), Phase 20, Phase 21. The intent model, the decision object and the candidate scoring are the
extension points.

### Final status
**Complete.**
- **Verified end to end:** scene intent, candidate decisions, scoring, repairs and the fallback on the mini-lesson and
  the ten required cases; the compositions rendered by the Phase 13 page at 1280×720 and 1920×1080 (nothing
  overlapping, no text below 80 %); explicit choices, "Automatic" and regeneration in Visual Review; the export
  recording the same compositions as the preview.
- **Not verified:** AI-assisted composition with a real Gemini or OpenAI model (no key here; the stand-in model and
  the unavailable path were tested).
- **Unchanged by default:** the Classic scene style renders every lesson exactly as before; Automatic composition makes
  no model call.
- **Nothing committed or deployed.** No database change (the composition lives in the lesson's plans).
