Measured: resolving a scene's style takes well under 1.5 ms on the server (200 scenes in 46–84 ms); a style switch makes
no generation request (only the style store and the re-plan); every `--st-*` token on the page matches `styles.py` in all
four styles.

### LLM validation
Not applicable: Phase 17 makes no model call (the user picks the style; no AI style assistance was built). No AI media was
generated (the library diagrams were reused; AI backgrounds are generated only on request and none was).

### Visual quality validation (all four styles rendered and inspected)
A representative lesson (introduction, definition, process, diagram with the library's leaf diagram, formula with symbol
labels, code with its output, comparison, presenter-led explanation, summary; the Aadhi Teacher, and one scene with
Aadhi in his studio) was planned and played in real Chrome at 1280×720 in each style (silent). Every scene was captured
and inspected; the first round was not accepted (see Issues).
- **Academic:** warm ivory paper with faint rules, ink-navy serif titles under a short crimson bar, white cards with thin
  edges, crimson step numbers, ticks and highlighted words, captions in a dark box. It reads like a well-set textbook page:
  structured, calm, strong hierarchy, no decoration competing with the content.
- **Cinematic Education:** the page's existing look (deep violet, gold, glow emphasis), identical to a lesson saved before
  Phase 17 (the same scene rendered from an old plan matches).
- **Children's Education:** warm cream and sky blue with a soft dot pattern, rounded white cards, orange step numbers and
  ticks, a rounded presenter frame with the "Aadhi Teacher" name card, slightly larger text. Friendly and clear, not
  chaotic: one accent, one pattern, generous spacing.
- **Corporate Training:** crisp navy with a faint grid, sky-blue accent, flat cards with small radii, a presenter card
  with a name card. Professional and aligned without being sterile.
- **Across styles:** the leaf diagram is the same library asset in every style, only its frame changes; code panels stay
  dark and readable; formulas and their labels follow the style; synchronization moments, camera and composition are the
  same in every style (only the look differs). With Aadhi presenting, the studio stays (he is filmed in it) and the
  style's title card sits on its own surface.
- **Overrides:** Corporate Training with accent teal, large text and a plain background: teal headers and bar, the grid
  gone, every board still fits.
- **Readability measured in the page** (lowest contrast per style, body text / titles): Academic 8.1 / 14.3, Cinematic
  Education 8.75 / 16.0, Children's Education 4.69 / 13.0 (an orange table header), Corporate Training 7.74 / 13.0.
- **Preview versus export:** the exported frames carry each style's colours and match the preview (mean difference 1.0
  Academic, 1.2 Corporate Training, 1.5 Children's Education on a 32×18 grid, the preview's control bar excluded);
  scanning the exported videos at 20 fps around every scene change found no white or grey flash.

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- **Found by integrating (each pinned by a test):** the entrance length of a style (one value) would have changed an old
  lesson's fades and slides (two values): the default family keeps the page's own lengths (`auto`); the Visual Review
  summary could not tell an old lesson from an explicit Cinematic Education choice: the look carries `legacy`; the
  default family's visual frame tokens were empty, so a "card" diagram frame looked like "plain".
- **Found by the backend test agent:** one unknown key dropped all of a scene's (or a saved lesson's) style choices: each
  bad key is now dropped on its own.
- **Found by the end-of-phase security audit (a read-only agent; each fixed and covered by a test):**
  - `/save-history` answered 500 for a saved style whose family was a list or an object; the resolver had the same latent
    type errors (a list family, a list version, an object typography): all are checked (Invalid or the default).
  - The token value check was a deny-list (no `url`, no `expression`): `image-set('https://…')`, `cross-fade(…)`,
    `var(…)`, `attr(…)` and `env(…)` passed it. It is an allow-list now, on the server and on the page: only rgb/rgba,
    hsl/hsla, gradients, clamp, calc, min, max, cubic-bezier and drop-shadow; ASCII only; quotes only around font names.
  - `/review` answered 500 for a malformed saved review record (older code, now on the scene-style path): ignored.
  - A malformed alpha crashed the colour parser; a very long colour made the page's parser slow: both bounded.
- **Found by the end-of-phase correctness review (a read-only agent; each fixed and covered by a test):**
  - Picking a style pre-filled the lesson's transition and camera motion: those are part of the composition fingerprint,
    so every Visual Review approval re-opened, and a deliberate still camera became a moving one. Picking a style now
    changes only the style; its suggestions are offered with a button.
  - The side chart took the colours of the previous scene's look (it is drawn before the scene's look is applied), restyled
    an old lesson's chart and flattened pie charts to one colour: it reads the current scene's plan, never a legacy look,
    and pie / doughnut / polar charts keep their own colours.
  - Overrides on a lesson without a style were shown and applied but never saved: they wait for a chosen style.
  - Undoing a scene's accent dropped that scene's earlier approval: a style-only change keeps an approved layout approved.
  - A style chosen while a save was on its way went to the previous copy of the lesson: it follows the new one (also for
    the save made before a first export, found by the document's fact-check).
  - The presenter's name card was a fixed overlay (it drifted during a camera move, could sit in the subtitle band under
    Aadhi and showed before an AI clip arrived): it is inside the presenter's layer, never for Aadhi, never while the
    presenter is hidden or missing.
  - The contrast fix always moved toward white on darkish backgrounds, even where white could not reach the ratio; the
    accent's marks (bullets, ticks, step numbers) and dates on their badge were not checked (Children's orange marks were
    2.3:1): the fix moves toward the end that reads, the guard checks both, and Children's accent is a deeper orange.
  - Titles over picture backgrounds in the light styles could land dark text on a dark picture: the non-default styles put
    the title and label chips on the style's surface over pictures and clips.
- **Found by a read-only fact-check of this document:** "Keep" on a scene whose approval carried its own accent or
  background (e.g. after the approval went stale) dropped those choices: they are kept (approved, the layout unchanged).
- **Found by looking at the renders (four styles × every scene):** keywords in code took the board's highlighted-word
  colour (Prism's `.token.keyword` shares the class), dark crimson on the dark code panel in Academic (1.9:1): code has its
  own accent per style, checked against the code panel (7.9–14:1); the code's Output card lost its bottom border (also in
  the old look): fixed.
- **Test expectations changed with the behaviour** (none weakened): one accessibility test expected an unreadable accent
  fill to be kept as the user's (now: the fill is made readable too, because it draws marks); the browser check's first
  version expected the pre-fill (now: the suggestion button).

### Known limitations
- **16:9 only.** The project has no other output format (the composition frame, safe areas and export are 16:9); the
  main sizes scale with the frame (vw / clamp) but a 9:16 or 1:1 layout would need new frame geometry, not a style.
- **Cinematic mode only.** Classic scenes keep their original look (the style system styles composed scenes).
- **What the page draws itself is styled; media is not.** Library pictures, AI images and clips, Manim renders and Aadhi's
  studio clips are framed, never recoloured; the graph (JSXGraph), 3D, GIF and clip side panels keep their own dark look in
  every style; code panels stay dark (Prism's dark theme) in every style.
- **Lesson HTML with inline colours:** on light styles a colour written in the lesson's HTML that would not read is
  replaced by the style's text colour (the page's guard); inline backgrounds are left as written.
- **AI style assistance** (a model recommending a style) was not built: the user picks among the four.
- **Presenter:** the style frames the presenter and may add a name card; the drawn teacher's colours, Aadhi's look and AI
  presenters are the presenter's identity (Phase 12) and never change with the style.
- **Browser support:** the CSS relies on `:has()`, `max()` and clip-path view transitions (Chrome 111+), as the page's
  existing cinematic features already do.

### Deferred to later phases
Phase 18 (quality engine: automated quality scoring, cross-scene consistency checks and correction), Phase 19 (editor),
Phase 20, Phase 21. The style registry, the tokens and the effective style in each plan are the extension points Phase 18
can evaluate.

### Final status
**Complete.**
- **Verified end to end:** four versioned style families with central tokens (colours, typography, spacing, shapes,
  surfaces, backgrounds, motion, transitions, camera preference, presenter / visual / caption frames), resolved once per
  scene on the server and applied by the one cinematic page; the representative lesson rendered and inspected in all four
  styles; the user's choices (bounded), scene accent / background through Visual Review, accessibility over decoration
  (contrast checked and enforced, readable sizes, reduced motion), versions and a fingerprint, the lesson's style saved,
  stored in place and restored, old lessons unchanged, preview and export in the same style, no media regenerated, no
  approval re-opened by a style switch.
- **Unchanged:** the Classic style; Phases 12–16's responsibilities (presenter identity, composition, direction,
  synchronization, rendering); the Visual Router, the Asset Library, the AI media layer, its cache identity and recovery;
  no Phase 18 work (no quality scoring, consistency checking or correction).
- **Nothing committed or deployed.** No database change (the style lives in the lesson JSON and each plan).

---

## Phase 18 — AI Video Quality & Consistency Engine

### Objective
Make a multi-scene lesson look and behave like one intentionally designed video: evaluate the whole lesson and each scene
against the project's own contracts (presenter, Phase 17 style, typography, colour, terminology, formulas, code,
diagrams, camera, motion, backgrounds, density, timing, media, preview / export), explain what is inconsistent in plain
words, and repair only what is safe — through the existing systems, never a parallel one. Phase 19 (editor) was not
started.

### Baseline (start of Phase 18)
Backend 552/552, frontend 211/211, Phase 1 27/27, Phase 2 30/30, export with library assets 35/35, Phase 3 26/26,
Phase 4 24/24, Phase 5 11/11, Phase 6 17/17, Phase 7 18/18, Phase 8 12/12, Phase 9 14/14, Phase 10 17/17,
Phase 11 18/18, Phase 12 20/20, Phase 13 24/24, Phase 14 17/17, Phase 15 23/23, Phase 16 17/17, Phase 17 21/21.

### Audit (before Phase 18; two read-only analysts plus the orchestrator)
- Every scene already carries its plans and fingerprints (composition, direction, synchronization, style look, visual
  slots, presenter plan, Visual Review records); validators exist per scene (plan validity, text fit, synchronization,
  accessibility) but nothing compared scenes across a lesson, there was no terminology registry, no lesson-level
  evaluation and no quality UI; the code language and single-`$` formulas were not recorded.
- Media metadata (asset kind, size, shape, provenance, runs) can be read without touching files; the existing helpers that
  check assets mark them missing (they write) and must not be used by a read-only report.
- Only Phase 17's scene accent / background changes are approval-safe; changing the lesson's motion, transitions or
  background re-opens approvals; recovery must not be hooked.

### Architecture
```
 the lesson as planned (scenes + cinematic_plan + visual_direction + visual_plan + presenter_plan + visual_review)
   + the lesson settings (Phase 17 style, motion, transitions, presenter) + the concept map
   + (server) the lesson's media metadata: one asset query, one runs query, plain file checks (nothing marked)
   + (server) the plans the current planning gives now (compose_lesson, no model: ai cached_only)
        │
        ▼
 quality.py — the Quality & Consistency Engine (deterministic, read-only, versioned: quality_rules@1)
   Lesson context (its derivations computed once per report, from the existing modules)
   ├── quality_style      style · typography · colour · background (Phase 17 authoritative; validate_plan; text fit)
   ├── quality_media      presenter · media (Phase 12 presenter contract; Asset Library / AI runs metadata)
   ├── quality_education  terminology · concepts · formulas · code · diagrams (registry from the lesson's own words)
   ├── quality_timing     timing · camera · motion · density (Phase 16 / 13 plans checked, never re-timed)
   └── core               preview / export (the stored plan = what the current planning gives = what an export records)
        │  findings → report (status, per-dimension, per-scene, issues, registry, limitations, fingerprints)
        ▼
 POST /api/quality/lesson (never saves, generates, approves or touches recovery)
        │
        ▼
 Visual Review (review.js): the lesson's Quality panel, per-scene chips and blocks; "Fix automatically" = the existing
 planning again (approval-safe); a suggestion = Visual Review's own composition review; pre-export warnings
```
- **One source of truth per concern.** Style tokens, contrast and the accessibility guard come from `styles.py`; plan
  validity from `cinematic.validate_plan`; text fit from `composer.text_need`; the narration's length from Phase 16's
  `narration_model`; synchronization validity from `sync_director.validate`; scene understanding from
  `visual_director.understand` and `scene_intent.board_facts`; presenter profiles from `presenters.py`. Nothing is
  re-implemented; a check compares the stored result with what its own system says.
- **Nothing per frame, nothing persisted.** The report is derived on demand (about 40–60 ms for an 8–11-scene lesson on the
  server, recomposition included; 120–150 ms with the HTTP round trip). No table, no field in the lesson, no history entry: running a check changes nothing.

### The quality model
- **A finding** carries: a stable `id` (rule + scene + element + evidence key), the `rule` (`<family>.<what>`), the
  `dimension` (presenter, style, typography, colour, terminology, concepts, formulas, code, diagrams, camera, motion,
  backgrounds, busy scenes, timing, pictures and clips, preview and export), the `severity`, the `scene` (0-based in the
  data, 1-based in words) and `element`, a plain-words `message` for an educator, the `evidence` (small structured data:
  what was compared), the `repair` (kind, class, action, label), the `repair_status` and the scene's `fingerprint`.
  (Dimension keys: presenter, style, typography, colour, terminology, education, formulas, code, diagrams, camera,
  motion, background, density, timing, media, preview_export; the words above are their labels.)
- **Severity** (deterministic; each rule declares one): *info* — intentional or harmless (a scene accent chosen in Visual
  Review, a presenter hidden on purpose), never counted as a problem; *notice* — a possible inconsistency worth a look;
  *warning* — a readability or consistency problem; *error* — a concrete contract violation (a stale plan, a missing
  background, a moving camera in a lesson without motion); *blocking* — the lesson cannot be exported as reviewed (only
  deterministic facts: a visual the lesson shows whose file is gone).
- **The report:** `rules`, `version`, `fingerprint`, `status` (good / review / attention / blocked: blocking → blocked,
  error → attention, warning or notice → review, else good), `summary` (scenes, counts per severity, things to look at,
  automatic repairs), `dimensions` (each: pass or its worst severity, count), `scenes` (each: fingerprint, status,
  counts), `issues` (most serious first, one per id), `registry`, `limitations` (what could not be checked, e.g. media
  when the lesson is not saved) and the families that ran. Not one opaque score.
- **The consistency registry** (derived only from the lesson): the style (id, family, version, legacy, tone, accents per
  scene), captions, background kinds; the presenter contract (profile, type, positions, roles, switches) and media
  (sources, providers, whether files were checked); terminology (term groups with every written form and the scenes
  using it), concepts, abbreviations (where spelled out), formula symbols and meanings, code languages (and whether the
  page colours them), diagram labels; camera moves and still scenes, transitions (lesson kind, used kinds), pacing
  (estimated seconds per scene and total).

### Fingerprints, rules version, stale reports
- **Scene fingerprint:** the whole scene as planned (its texts, plans, direction, visuals, presenter, Visual Review
  records), its position and the number of scenes, and every lesson setting — with numbers normalised (a plan that went
  through the browser is the same plan) and volatile parts left out (review timestamps, AI records, notes, signed links, a
  report kept with a scene).
- **Lesson fingerprint:** the rules version, the settings, the concept map, every scene fingerprint, the media digest and
  whether the plans were recomputed.
  A report whose rules or fingerprint differ from the current ones is stale (`is_stale`); the page knows the same through
  a light key of what the checks read and shows "The lesson changed since this check".
- **Incremental:** a scene's own findings are reused while its fingerprint and its own media are unchanged (a small
  in-process memo, never persisted; a result whose checks failed is never kept); cross-scene checks always run (they are
  cheap). Editing one scene re-checks that scene only; a lesson setting change, or adding / moving scenes, re-checks the
  scenes it affects (every setting is part of each scene's fingerprint).
- **Versioned rules:** `quality_rules@1`. New rules make old reports stale; lessons are never regenerated for it.
### The checks (81 rules in four families and the core)
- **Style, text, colour, background** (`quality_style`, 20 rules): each saved look against what Phase 17 resolves now
  (stale, tampered, missing, mixed styles or versions, original look mixed with a chosen style → error, automatic
  re-plan); readability through Phase 17's own guard on the stored look (`styles._accessibility`; the code-keyword pair
  included; a failure a re-plan cannot fix has no repair; adjustments Phase 17 made are a notice); plan validity
  (`cinematic.validate_plan`, mapped to camera, text, presenter, diagrams, motion, timing or preview / export); text that does not fit its board (`composer.text_need`) or would drop under 80 % at the style's
  size (warning, a suggestion that gives the board room); crowded boards; title casing and heading levels; background
  changes nothing asked for, backgrounds that fell back (one lesson-level finding when the lesson setting caused it); a
  scene's own accent / background, a background chosen in the screenplay or kept for Aadhi's studio, a picture background
  behind a light style (info) and three or more scene accents (notice, suggestion: back to the lesson's).
- **Presenter and media** (`quality_media`, 18 rules): who presents each scene, read the way the page plays it, against the
  lesson's presenter — an unexplained switch is an error, while a presenter removed or moved in Visual Review, a size or
  position chosen there or in the screenplay, and a clip chosen there are intentional (info, at most one per scene); side flips
  not asked for (notice, suggestion back to the usual side); size jumps; Aadhi on a background other than his studio; an AI
  presenter without its clip. With the lesson's media looked up (one asset query, one runs query, plain file checks —
  nothing marked): a shown visual whose file is gone (blocking), kind / empty file / aspect / provenance problems, visuals
  not generated yet, failed runs, a missing background or presenter clip; providers mixed across AI visuals (info). A
  removed visual is never reported; nothing ever suggests regenerating.
- **Terminology, concepts, formulas, code, diagrams** (`quality_education`, 18 rules): a terminology registry from the
  lesson's own emphasised words (keywords, the defined term, table headers, headings, labels, titles, concept titles) —
  capitalisation differences in emphasised places, hyphen / space variants, abbreviations never spelled out or spelled out
  two ways, a concept named differently across its scenes; formulas in every form MathJax accepts (single `$…$` included):
  a symbol given two meanings, symbols never explained, notation differences; code: a language the page does not colour,
  tabs mixed with spaces, lines over 70 characters, blocks over 18 lines, mixed languages (info); diagram labels whose
  casing differs from the board, one asset labelled differently in two scenes, AI pictures (their content is not read:
  info). Content is the author's: these findings never repair anything; they say which scenes use which form.
- **Timing, camera, motion, density** (`quality_timing`, 23 rules): Phase 16's plans checked against Phase 16 / 13 rules,
  never re-timed — invalid synchronization, moments or entrances after the scene's end, moments aimed at something the
  plan does not show, transition lengths, moving transitions or camera moves in a lesson without motion (errors,
  automatic re-plan); a duration far from the narration's and a scene transition other than the lesson's (notices,
  automatic re-plan); captions too long for two lines, more to read than the narration gives
  time for, three or more transition kinds, camera moves on reading scenes, switching moves without a reason, the same
  layout and move again and again, busy synchronization, overloaded or near-empty scenes, two dense scenes in a row
  (notices / warnings; suggestions only where an existing composition choice helps, e.g. a smaller presenter).
- **Preview and export** (core, 2 rules): a cinematic scene without a plan, and a stored plan whose layout or timing
  differs from what the current planning gives (the preview and an export would not show what was reviewed) — compared
  on the layout and the moments themselves, never on hashes that carry the style's colours or on signed links.
- **One cause, one finding:** findings in a scene fixed by the same re-plan (or the same suggestion) are folded into the
  most important one (a stale plan first), the others named in its evidence; what the export's own warnings already list
  (missing pictures, a background that fell back) is not repeated there.

### Repairs (detect → suggest → repair)
- Every finding's repair is classified by what it would change: **presentation** or **timing** (derived plans),
  **composition**, **visual**, **presenter**, **content**.
- **Automatic ("Fix automatically")** only for a re-plan of a derived plan (presentation / timing) that keeps every
  approval: the page asks the existing planning for fresh plans (nothing saved by that call) and applies them only to the
  scenes the finding names. A re-plan that would change an approved scene's composition inputs (e.g. the lesson's motion or
  transitions changed since) is offered as a suggestion that says the approval will be asked again; it is never automatic.
- **Suggestions ("Apply suggestion")** go through Visual Review's own composition review (the scene's accent / background
  — approval-safe since Phase 17 — or a composition choice such as a smaller presenter, which becomes the user's choice).
- **Never repaired:** wording, terminology, formulas, code, media replacement, presenter identity — the finding tells the
  author where to look. Nothing regenerates media; nothing writes a Visual Review record behind the user's back.

### Visual Review and the page
- **The Quality panel** (top of Visual Review): "✓ Quality: good", "⚠ N things to review", "⚠ N things need attention" or
  "✕ The lesson cannot be exported as reviewed", the scenes checked, the areas with something to look at (the others
  folded into "✓ N areas look fine"), the findings grouped by scene — each with an icon and words for its severity
  ("Worth a look", "Please check", "Needs fixing", "Blocks the export"; never colour alone), the message, "Show scene", and
  a fix or suggestion button only where one exists; "Check again"; "The lesson changed since this check" when the lesson
  changed after the report (fix buttons disabled until checked again); limitations as small notes. Info findings, rule
  ids, raw severity names, repair classes, evidence and fingerprints only with `?visualDebug`.
- **Per scene:** a chip in the review list ("⚠ 2"), a Quality block in the scene's inspector, a "Quality" filter.
  Approvals and statuses are never changed by quality: a warning never revokes an approval; an approval goes stale only
  through the existing fingerprints when the reviewed thing itself changes.
- **Before an export:** the report runs once more on what will be recorded; its warnings and errors (at most 12) appear
  under their own heading ("The quality check found things to review"); a missing picture or a background that fell back
  is already in the export's own list and is not repeated; the export goes on (it has always warned rather than blocked).

### Phase 17 integration
The style system stays the authority: the quality engine resolves styles with `styles.resolve` and checks readability
with Phase 17's own guard; it never builds tokens, picks a style or stores one. A style switch shows as one finding (a
stale look, automatically re-planned without touching approvals or media); a Phase 17 scene accent is info; many scene
accents are a notice with an approval-safe suggestion. The code-keyword contrast fix and the Output card fix are guarded.

### AI assistance (optional, bounded)
Only for grouping ambiguous terms (an abbreviation never spelled out paired with the term it may stand for; two names
used for one concept), only in AI-assisted mode, only when an API request asks for it (`assist`; the page's own checks
never do), one call plus at most one repair call per lesson within 20 s, at most 8 pairs; the answer must name listed
pairs with true / false; a failed or late answer is not kept (a later request asks again); results are notices
"suggested by the assistant", never errors, never authoritative; ordinary reports and the pre-export check never call a
model. Verified with the stand-in model only.

### Files changed
- **New:** `quality.py` (core, API), `quality_style.py`, `quality_media.py`, `quality_education.py`, `quality_timing.py`;
  tests `tests/test_quality.py`, `tests/test_quality_style.py`, `tests/test_quality_media.py`,
  `tests/test_quality_education.py`, `tests/test_quality_timing.py`, `tests/test_quality_integration.py`,
  `tests/quality_review.test.js`, `tests/quality_wiring.test.js`, `tests/quality_browser_check.mjs`.
- **Changed:** `server.py` (the quality router), `review.js` (the Quality panel, scene chips and blocks, the Quality
  filter, "Show scene", a presenter decision re-plans the composition), `cinematic.js` (`CinematicApi.quality`),
  `index.html` (the review adapter: report, re-check, stale key, repairs on the named scenes, suggestions through the
  composition review; the pre-export quality check; the panel's styles), `export.js` (the findings under their own heading;
  a user's Cancel is not logged as an error), `scene_intent.py` (its formula pattern bounded).

Silent regression (Chrome with `--disable-audio-output`, speech stubbed, no real AI provider call). Fixes from the final

quality check ran on the final code, and the one environment-sensitive failure was re-run alone:

- **Phase 3 (25/26):** "the Assets button opens the library with the shared Aadhi clips" counts the shared clips on the
  library's first page (200 items, newest first). The development database now holds 198 test uploads from earlier
  phases' runs, so the 13 shared clips fall past the first page; the same check's filter ("videos from Aadhi") lists them
  and another account sees all 13. Phase 18 does not touch the asset library; the database was left as it is (it may hold
  the user's own uploads). It is a Phase 3 limitation worth noting: a library with very many uploads shows the shared
  clips only through a filter or search.
- **Phase 9 (13/14 in the full run):** one WebSocket connection error on the check's own server, which the check stops and
  restarts on purpose (just outside the window it excludes); re-run alone: 14/14.

Measured: a report for an 8–11-scene lesson in about 40–60 ms on the server with recomposition (120–150 ms through HTTP);
32 scenes in about 110 ms; 60 scenes through the API in about 0.5 s with recomposition; editing one scene re-checks only
that scene.

### LLM validation
**Not done for real.** No text model key exists on this machine. The optional terminology assistance was verified only
with the stand-in model (valid, malformed-then-repaired, fabricated and failing answers, a slow answer within the budget,
no model available) and never runs unless an API request asks for it in AI-assisted mode. No real request was made.

### Visual quality validation (rendered lessons inspected)
- **Coherence across a lesson, four styles:** the representative lesson (introduction, definition, process, diagram with
  the library's leaf diagram, formula with symbol labels, code with its output, comparison, presenter-led explanation,
  summary; the Aadhi Teacher; one scene with Aadhi in his studio) was rendered in real Chrome at 1280×720 in Academic,
  Cinematic Education, Children's Education and Corporate Training and every scene's frame was inspected side by side:
  within each style the cards, title bars, typography, accent, presenter frame and name card, captions and backgrounds
  stay the same from scene to scene; the diagram is the same library asset framed by the style; code panels stay dark
  and readable; nothing overlaps; no scene's text is crowded. The Phase 18 report on the same lessons: "✓ Quality: good" in
  every style (one info: the planner's gentle camera moves in consecutive scenes).
- **The quality review in the page** (the Phase 18 browser check, 11-scene lesson including a quiz): the panel's headline,
  areas, grouped findings with icon and words, scene chips, the scene's quality block shown by "Show scene", the stale note
  after a style change, "Fix automatically" (only the named scene re-planned, approvals and every review record unchanged,
  nothing generated), a suggestion applied through Visual Review (it became the user's change), the export prompt with the
  findings under their own heading, and reduced motion (no camera move, no motion finding) were all seen in screenshots.
- **A problem lesson** (an abbreviation never spelled out, keyword casing, Java code, a style switch not re-planned, a
  stale plan, an overloaded scene) lists exactly those six problems in plain words, grouped by scene — "⚠ 6 things need
  attention" (eleven findings before one-cause folding).
- **Preview versus export:** the exported frame matches the preview (mean difference 1.1 of 255 on a 32×18 grid, the
  preview's controls excluded); the clean lesson's export asked nothing (its own quality check was good).
- **Seen, not changed:** in the preview only (not the export), the player's progress line crosses the captions — the
  same as in Phase 17.

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- **Found by the integration test agent:** a style-only switch was also reported as a stale layout and timing on every
  scene (since Phase 17 the plan hash and the synchronization fingerprint carry the style's background colours): the
  preview / export comparison now compares the layout and the moments themselves, and a scene's fingerprint covers its
  layout and timing (a plan edited without its hash still counts as changed). A core test that could not catch it was made
  strict.
- **Found by the browser check (real lessons through the page):**
  - Every scene of a good lesson was reported stale: plans that went through the browser come back with 1.0 written as 1
    (JSON in JavaScript), so the hashed comparison never matched. Numbers are normalised before anything is hashed.
  - "Show scene" left the scene's quality block scrolled out of view; the panel's CSS did not match its markup (bullets,
    a plain grey toggle); the planner's own default camera motion was reported as a notice (now info: recorded, not a
    problem); a presenter moved in Visual Review left the composition stale until re-planned (a presenter decision now
    re-plans, as a visual decision already did); a suggested re-plan had no button; the export listed quality findings as
    "could not be prepared" (they now have their own heading) and logged the user's own Cancel as an error.
- **Found by the adversarial audit (read-only agent; each fixed and covered by a test):**
  - Lessons with a picture, clip or AI background were reported stale on every scene, for good: the stored background
    link is signed and expires. Signed links (and other volatile parts) never take part in a comparison or fingerprint.
  - Crafted lesson text could make the report slow for minutes (patterns that backtrack on a 20,000-character word, a
    title of spaces, "[A:" repeated, "\[" repeated in a board): every pattern over lesson text is bounded, every text a
    check reads is bounded (titles 300, narration 12,000, HTML 30,000 characters; the recomputed plans use the same bounded
    scenes), a lesson's text is capped (3 million characters, else 413) and the scenes' own checks stop at a 20 s budget
    (the rest reported as not checked).
  - "Fix automatically" could take approvals away when the cause was a lesson setting the user changed (motion,
    transitions, typography are composition inputs) and re-planned the whole lesson: an automatic repair now applies fresh
    plans only to the scenes it names, and a re-plan that would ask an approved scene again is a suggestion that says so.
  - One cause gave several findings (a stale plan plus its timing, motion and presenter symptoms; two suggestions of the
    same change): folded into one finding.
  - Stale reports could go unnoticed (the scene fingerprint missed inputs such as Aadhi's position, quiz text, the concept,
    the look's CSS; the page's key missed others and was taken after the request): the fingerprint now covers the whole
    scene and every setting (only volatile parts left out), the page's key follows the same rule and is taken before the
    request, and each scene's memo key uses only its own media (another scene's run no longer re-checks it); the memo is
    thread-safe.
  - The optional AI assistance ran inside every report in AI-assisted mode (the export's pre-check waited for it): it now
    runs only when the request asks for it; a failed or late answer is retried by a later request.
  - Duplicated logic: the style family's own contrast pairs were replaced by Phase 17's guard itself; repairs a re-plan
    cannot perform (an unreadable current style, presenter size differences) no longer offer one; a fallback background
    caused by the lesson setting is one finding, not one per scene; HTML and CSS code is no longer said to be shown without
    colours (the page's Prism bundle colours them).
- **Found by the final security / correctness review (read-only agent):** odd but valid plans (synchronization events
  that are a number, a 600-level nested target, an infinite transition length) could make the report fail or send invalid
  JSON: the core's own checks run inside the same safety net as the families, nesting and non-finite numbers are bounded,
  and evidence never echoes them; a failed check's empty result was reused without its "could not run" note (never kept
  now); a suggested re-plan said "your approvals are kept" (it now says the scene will be reviewed again); the code
  message named only Python and JavaScript as coloured.
- **Found by the family agents' own reviews:** the style family's stale-look check and the core's stale-plan check
  reported a style switch twice (the style family owns it now); the media lookup used the server's asset library by
  default rather than the router's; the scene fingerprint missed the presenter mode.
- **Test expectations changed with the behaviour** (none weakened): tests that simulated a stale plan by changing only its
  hash now change the layout itself (a hash alone is not a layout); tests expecting a symptom next to a stale plan read it
  in the folded finding's evidence; the camera-motion test expects info; the presenter-size test expects no repair.

### Known limitations
- **What is not read:** the content of pictures, clips and AI media (only their metadata: kind, size, shape, provenance,
  generation state), mathematical correctness, whether code runs, and the real length of the voice (durations are the
  narration model's estimates).
- **Terminology is pattern-based:** terms are compared where the lesson emphasises them (keywords, the defined term,
  headers, headings, labels, titles); genuinely different names for one thing are found only by the optional assistant
  (AI-assisted mode, on request). Common symbols (x, y) can draw an "unexplained symbol" notice.
- **Text fit uses the composer's estimate,** not a measurement in the browser; the page's own fit still shrinks a board
  that would overflow.
- **Presenter:** no screenplay field names a presenter, so only a Visual Review decision explains a different presenter
  for a scene. AI runs are matched to scenes by their stored scene number (it can drift after scenes are moved).
- **The report is derived on demand,** not stored; a report kept by the page is compared with the lesson by a key, not by
  the server's fingerprint.
- **Export:** findings are shown, not enforced (the export has always warned rather than blocked).
- **Load:** the endpoint has no rate limit (any signed-in user); a large lesson can take seconds (the scenes' own checks
  stop at 20 s); in AI-assisted mode an API request with `assist` can make up to two model calls per new set of term pairs.
- **Real AI:** the optional terminology assistance was verified with the stand-in model only (no text model key here).
- **16:9 only** and the Classic style gets the checks that apply to it (text, terminology, formulas, code, timing); the
  composition, style and preview / export checks need the cinematic style.

### Deferred to later phases
Phase 19 (advanced editor: timeline, manual editing), Phase 20 (end-to-end studio), Phase 21. The quality contract
(rules, findings, registry, fingerprints) is the extension point.

### Final status
**Complete.**
- **Verified end to end:** a deterministic, versioned quality report for the whole lesson and each scene (81 rules in
  four families and the core), plain-words findings with severity, evidence, scene and repair; a consistency registry;
  fingerprints that mark old reports stale and reuse unchanged scenes' results; one cause reported once; automatic repairs
  only where approvals and media stay untouched, suggestions through Visual Review, content left to the author; the
  Quality panel, scene chips and blocks, the pre-export check; no false alarms on good lessons in all four Phase 17 styles;
  crafted problems found; nothing written, generated or approved by a check; recovery untouched; rendered lessons
  inspected in all four styles; preview and export matching.
- **Not verified:** the optional AI terminology assistance with a real model (stand-in model only; no key here).
- **Unchanged:** Phases 13–17's systems and responsibilities (style, composition, direction, synchronization, rendering,
  presenter, Visual Router, Asset Library, AI cache, recovery, Visual Review's authority); no Phase 19 editing features.
- **Nothing committed or deployed.** No database change (the report is derived on demand and never stored).

---

## Phase 19 — Advanced Video Editor

### Objective
Let an educator refine an already generated lesson by hand — scene order, timing, narration, presenter, visual, text,
background, transitions, camera, captions, visibility, duplication, deletion, insertion, splitting, style — through an
editing layer over the existing lesson, without a second renderer, planner, synchronization, style, quality, review,
asset or export system. Phase 20 (end-to-end studio) was not started.

### Baseline (start of Phase 19)
Backend 760/760, frontend 239/239, Phase 1 27/27, Phase 2 30/30, export with library assets 35/35, Phase 3 25/26 (the
development database's library page, see Phase 18), Phase 4 24/24, Phase 5 11/11, Phase 6 17/17, Phase 7 18/18,
Phase 8 12/12, Phase 9 14/14, Phase 10 17/17, Phase 11 18/18, Phase 12 20/20, Phase 13 24/24, Phase 14 17/17,
Phase 15 23/23, Phase 16 17/17, Phase 17 21/21, Phase 18 22/22.

### Audit (before Phase 19; two read-only analysts plus the orchestrator)
- Scenes had no identity (only their position); AI runs and several routes find a scene by position; `/save-history`
  always creates a new history entry and nothing updated a lesson in place; no revision or stale-save protection; a
  scene's duration followed its narration (no control); no scene visibility, no per-scene captions, no undo; most layout
  choices already had an approval-safe channel (Visual Review's composition overrides); the stage is the viewport itself,
  so the editor sits around the live stage (one renderer) rather than in a separate preview.

### Architecture
```
 the lesson (payload.scenes + payload.editor) ── GET /api/editor/{id} (scenes with ids, revision, generating)
        │
        ▼
 editor.js  EditorModel over the page's own `slides` (spliced in place) — commands {type, scene_id, before, after},
            undo / redo, transactions, replay by scene id, Autosave (debounced, held during drags), Draft (this browser)
        │ every change
        ▼
 editor_ui.js  EditorWorkspace around the live stage: top bar · scene list · timeline (scene, narration, presenter,
               visual, caption tracks; transition markers; playhead) · inspector
        │ adapter (index.html)
        ▼
 existing systems: the planner (planLessonCinematic; Phase 16 retimes from the narration) · Visual Review's composition
 review (layout choices) · Visual Review's session (visual choose / remove) · Asset Library picker · Phase 17 styles ·
 Phase 18 quality · the page's playback (renderSlide; hold / hidden / mute / captions) · the export (records the page)
        │
        ▼
 PUT /api/editor/{id} (in place, revision checked)  ·  "Save version" = /save-history (a history entry on purpose)
```
Preview = the live stage; the export records the same page; so whatever the editor shows is what is exported.

### The editing model (override / refinement layer)
- **Scene identity:** every scene has `scene_id` (`s-` + 12 hex). An old lesson gets ids when opened (derived from the
  lesson and the position, the same on every load) and the editor stores them at once, before any edit; a duplicate,
  inserted or split scene gets a new id.
- **Per-scene editor data `scene.edit`:** `hidden`, `min_seconds` (hold at least; up to 600 s, the inspector takes
  0.5–600 s), `captions: "off"`,
  `narration_muted`, `origin` (inserted / duplicated / split) and `from`, `original` (the generated value of a source
  field the editor changed: title, subtitle, narration, html, labels — so "Generated" vs "Edited" is shown and Revert is
  exact). Absent = the generated scene; old lessons need none.
- **Lesson-level `payload.editor`:** `version`, `captions.visible`, `generated_order` (to show moved scenes); kept by every
  save.
- **What goes where (only what the architecture honours):** order / duplicate / delete / insert / hide / split → the
  scenes array and `scene.edit`; duration → `min_seconds` (the page holds the scene; narration is never sped up; the plan
  carries it as presentation timing, outside every approval fingerprint); narration → the scene's text (Phase 16 retimes
  it through the planner); mute and captions → `scene.edit` / `editor.captions`; camera, transition, presenter size /
  side / hidden, visual size / side, background kind, scene accent / background density → Visual Review's composition
  overrides (the existing route: the user's choice, approval rules unchanged; Phase 17's style choices keep approvals);
  the visual itself → Visual Review's own choose / remove (its approval rules); title / subtitle / labels → the scene's
  fields (content: the composition approval reopens, as for any content change); the lesson's style itself stays in
  Phase 17's settings (the start screen), not in the editor. The board's own HTML is part of the contract (its generated
  value is kept for Revert) but the inspector has no field for it.
- **Splitting** only at the narration's own `[PAUSE]` markers (a deterministic boundary): the two scenes share the board,
  the second gets a new id. Without pause markers splitting is not offered (no model is asked to cut text).
- **Insertion:** a blank scene, a picture from the Asset Library (the existing picker), a duplicate. No generation.
- **Undo / redo:** commands are plain JSON (no media): a drag or a typing visit is one step; undo of a layout choice
  sends the previous value through the same route (and gives back an approval the user had given to that same layout).

### Saving, revisions and recovery
- **In place, never a history entry per edit:** `PUT /api/editor/{id}` stores the edited scenes and editor data in the
  same lesson row, debounced (a drag saves when it ends). "Save version" is the existing `/save-history` (a history
  entry on purpose; the editor then keeps editing that version).
- **Stale saves refused:** the revision token is the lesson row's `updated_at` (every in-place writer changes it); the
  write is a compare-and-set (no lost update between the check and the write). A stale save (another tab, a generation
  that finished, a review elsewhere) reloads the newer lesson and replays the unsaved commands on it by scene id; an edit
  whose scene is gone is reported, never silently dropped.
- **Structural edits wait for generation:** while the lesson has AI runs in progress (results are attached by scene
  position), moving, adding, removing or splitting scenes is refused (server 409; the editor polls the status and shows
  why); property edits are allowed. An old lesson's ids are stored when the editor opens (the server's own scenes,
  unchanged), so its edits are judged by id too.
- **Visual Review writes a scene by its position:** the editor saves its own edits first and sends nothing if they could
  not be saved; the review and regenerate routes (composition, direction, visuals, presenter) refuse (409) a scene whose
  id differs from the scene saved at that position, and return the lesson's revision; replies are applied to the scene
  by id.
- **Autosave and drafts:** a save 1.5 s after the last edit (one at a time; a drag saves when it ends); undo keeps 200
  steps and typing in one field is one step. Unsaved edits are kept in this browser per lesson
  (`aadhi.editor.draft.<id>`) and offered back when the editor opens (replayed by scene id; what cannot be applied is
  said; layout choices are not replayed, Visual Review already saved them).
- **Validation (nothing a client sends is trusted):** ownership (404), at most 200 scenes, valid unique ids, the editor
  data contract (unknown keys dropped, types and ranges enforced; 422), texts bounded (title and subtitle 2,000,
  narration 50,000, board HTML 300,000 characters; 422), no NaN / Infinity (422), the scenes at most 8 million characters
  (413), and every library asset the scenes refer to — in any field or form — usable by the user (422). A lesson keeps at
  least one visible scene (the last one can be neither hidden nor deleted). After a save the lesson's
  asset references are recorded again (an asset still used is never deletable); deleting a scene never deletes an asset,
  a cache entry or a file.

### Playback (one renderer: the live page)
- **Minimum duration:** the scene holds at least `min_seconds` (timed excluding pauses); a longer narration is never cut,
  never sped up; a silent or muted scene lasts at least 5 s (its minimum if longer).
- **Hidden scenes** are skipped by the automatic advance, the player's previous / next, the scrubber and progress, the
  export's preparation and its chapters; the lesson-wide AI generation skips them too. (The editor's own ←/→ and ⏮/⏭
  step through every scene, hidden ones included, so they can be edited.)
- **Muted narration:** the scene plays without speech (no TTS request), its captions empty.
- **Captions:** off for the lesson or for one scene — the caption line is hidden and left out of the export's subtitles.
- **The editor never starts playback on its own:** while the lesson is paused, choosing or editing a scene shows it still
  (its board revealed, its narration not started); Play starts it properly.

### Phase 18 quality and Visual Review
- **Targeted invalidation:** a property edit changes only that scene's quality fingerprint (only its findings are
  re-checked; the others are reused); a structural edit re-checks the scenes whose place changed (a fingerprint includes
  the scene's place and the scene count, since some checks compare a scene with its neighbours; a report takes tens of
  milliseconds). In the editor, a scene's findings follow it by scene id and are marked "Changed since the check" only
  when that scene (or its place) changed. A duration shorter than the narration is
  a notice (`timing.hold_shorter`); a hidden scene's findings are recorded as info (it is not played).
- **Approvals:** presentation edits (duration, captions, mute, visibility) never touch an approval; a scene accent or
  background density never withdraws one (Phase 17's rule; on a scene with no composition review it is recorded as the
  user's change, "✎ Changed"); layout choices go through Visual Review as the user's choice; a new visual goes through Visual Review (its
  approval rules); a duplicated scene starts with no approvals (it was never reviewed); moving scenes recomposes their
  neighbours, whose composition approvals follow their own fingerprints (shown per scene in the editor).

### The workspace
Top bar (back, lesson, undo / redo with what they undo, the save state — Saved / Saving… / Unsaved changes / Couldn't save —
Retry / a conflict note —, the lesson's quality, Preview (hides the panels), Save version, shortcuts help). Scene list
(index, title, type, length, approval mark, quality chip with icon and words, Hidden / Edited / Moved / New). Timeline
(time ruler, scene blocks sized by their play time — labelled with it, as in the list — and draggable, narration /
presenter / visual / caption tracks, transition markers, hidden scenes collapsed and striped, a playhead that snaps to
scene starts, play / pause, previous / next, current time / total, zoom − / Fit / +). Inspector (Scene, Timing,
Narration, Text, Presenter, Visual, Camera, Transition, Background, Style, Captions, Quality; the first four open, the
others collapsed; "Generated" vs "Edited" with Revert; no ids, providers or fingerprints unless `?visualDebug`). Shortcuts
(only while the editor is open, never while typing): Space play / pause, Ctrl/Cmd+Z undo, Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z or Ctrl+Y redo,
Delete delete the selected scene (confirmed), ←/→ previous / next scene, Alt+←/→ move the scene, Esc (leaves a field, then
closes the confirmation, help, Preview, the inspector, the editor), ? help; off while another overlay is open. Tablet:
the inspector becomes a bottom sheet; small screens (≤ 700 px): the scene list, the inspector and the play controls (no
stage preview, no timeline tracks, a note says so).
Statuses are always an icon and words; visible focus; reduced motion respected.

### Files changed
- **New:** `editor_api.py` (scene ids, the editor data contract, `GET` / `PUT /api/editor/{id}`, `GET /api/editor/{id}/status`,
  the scene identity check the review routes use), `editor.js` (`AadhiEditor`: the editing model, undo / redo, Autosave,
  Draft), `editor_ui.js` (`AadhiEditorUI.EditorWorkspace`), `editor.css`; tests `tests/test_editor_api.py`,
  `tests/test_editor_playback.py`, `tests/editor.test.js`, `tests/editor_ui.test.js`, `tests/playback_edit.test.js`,
  `tests/editor_wiring.test.js`, `tests/editor_browser_check.mjs`.
- **Changed:** `server.py` (the editor router and its static files; `/save-history` keeps the editor data and refuses
  NaN / Infinity; the lesson-wide AI generation skips hidden scenes), `cinematic.py` (the scene's minimum duration in the
  plan as presentation timing; the review and regenerate routes check the scene's identity and return the lesson's
  revision),
  `visuals.py` and `presenters.py` (the same identity check and revision), `quality.py` (a hidden scene's findings are
  info), `quality_timing.py` (timing follows the minimum duration and the played order; `timing.hold_shorter`),
  `index.html` (the 🎬 Editor button; the editor's adapter over the existing systems; playback: minimum duration, hidden
  scenes, muted narration, caption visibility, still preview; plans applied only to the scenes they were made for;
  per-scene quality by scene id), `tests/quality_wiring.test.js` and `tests/test_quality.py` (the per-scene bookkeeping;
  hidden scenes).

### Results
Silent regression (Chrome with `--disable-audio-output`, speech stubbed, no real AI provider call; the editor check runs
its own server with the fake provider and voice):

| Suite | Result |
|---|---|
| Backend (unittest) | **803/803** (760 before + 43 Phase 19) |
| Frontend (node --test) | **342/342** (239 before + 103 Phase 19) |
