| Phase 2 export | 30/30 |
| Export with library assets | 35/35 |
| Phase 3 assets | 25/26 (see below) |
| Phase 4 visual router | 24/24 |
| Phase 5 AI cache | 11/11 (and 11/11 again on the final code) |
| Phase 6 Visual Review | 17/17 (and 17/17 again on the final code) |
| Phase 7 recording performance (720p) | 18/18 (28.6 fps effective, 0 stalls, page 129.2 fps) |
| Phase 8 providers | 12/12 (re-run alone on the final code; see below) |
| Phase 9 recovery | 14/14 |
| Phase 10 Manim sandbox | 17/17 |
| Phase 11 source documents | 18/18 |
| Phase 12 presenter | 20/20 |
| Phase 13 cinematic | 24/24 |
| Phase 14 composer | 17/17 |
| Phase 15 direction | 23/23 |
| Phase 16 synchronization | 17/17 |
| Phase 17 styles | 21/21 |
| Phase 18 quality check | 22/22 |
| **Phase 19 editor check** | **52/52** on the final code, run alone (44/44 in the full run, before the section below was added) |
| Static checks | inline scripts and all JS files parse; browser checks parse; Python modules compile |

- **Re-runs on the final code:** fixes from the documentation fact-check (the regenerate routes' identity check, an old
  lesson's ids kept when the editor opens) and the final visual QA (opaque panels, the timeline block's label) landed
  while the full run was under way; the backend and frontend suites were run again on the final code, and the checks
  that had already run with the routes before the change (Phase 5, Phase 6) were run again. The Phase 13–19 checks
  started their own servers after the change. The editor check was then extended (narration, split, mute, labels and
  background density done in the page, 8 checks) and run again alone on the final code: 52/52.
- **Phase 3 (25/26):** the same as in Phase 18 — the shared Aadhi clips fall past the library's first page (200 items,
  newest first) because the development database holds many test uploads from earlier runs; the filter lists them.
  Phase 19 does not touch the asset library.
- **Phase 8 (6/7 in the full run):** the page did not finish loading within the check's 30 s while unit tests were
  running beside it (the check stopped there; no assertion about providers failed). Re-run alone on the final code three
  times: 12/12 twice; once it stopped early (3/4) because the "Generate AI video" button was still being redrawn
  when the check clicked it (the check calls `skipIntroSequence()` before the intro has started, the timing issue the
  Phase 19 browser agent noted in other checks; no request pattern differed and no provider assertion failed). A
  timing weakness of the check, recorded here; the check was not changed.

### LLM validation
**Not applicable.** The editor makes no model call: splitting uses the narration's own pause markers, insertion uses a
blank scene, a library picture or a copy, and every edit is applied by the existing deterministic systems. No real AI
request was made.

### Visual quality validation (the edited lesson rendered and inspected)
- **The lesson:** nine scenes (introduction, definition, process, the library's leaf diagram, formula, code with its
  output, comparison, presenter-led explanation, summary; the Aadhi Teacher; Academic style), opened in real Chrome at
  1280×720, edited through the workspace as a user would, saved, reloaded, previewed and exported.
- **The edits:** the code scene moved before the formula by dragging its block, given a 14 s minimum, a still camera and a
  teal accent; the formula's captions turned off; the comparison hidden; the explanation's presenter moved to the left with
  a slide transition; a scene duplicated, a library picture and a blank scene inserted and then deleted again; the
  diagram's visual chosen from the Asset Library through Visual Review's session; a title typed (shortcuts stayed quiet)
  and reverted; a stale save from another writer (refused, reloaded, replayed, both changes kept). In the final run also:
  a pause typed into the definition's narration, the scene split there (two scenes sharing the board, the second new,
  "Split from the scene before") and the split undone, the narration reverted, the narration muted (the timeline's
  narration lane "Muted") and unmuted, labels typed and reverted, a "rich" background density through the composition
  review and undone — each saved in place and given back, so the preview and export below show the lesson as edited
  above.
- **Preview (the live page):** the played order follows the edit and skips the hidden scene; the code scene holds
  14.0 s; the formula scene shows no caption; the explanation's presenter is on the left; the inserted picture loads on
  the stage. **Export:** chapters and the scene log follow the edited order (no hidden scene), the subtitles have no cue
  for the captions-off scene, and the frames match the preview (mean difference 1.1, 1.7 and 1.8 of 255 for the code,
  diagram and explanation scenes, the preview's controls excluded). Every frame was looked at: the code panel is readable,
  the diagram is the library asset, the presenter is not cut, nothing overlaps, the captions sit clear of the board.
- **Quality after the edits:** "✓ Good" — four info findings only (the user's accent, presenter side and transition
  recorded as intentional; the hidden scene's reading time recorded as info, since it is not played).
- **The workspace:** the scene list, timeline (blocks sized by play time, transition markers, presenter / visual /
  caption tracks, the hidden scene collapsed and striped), inspector, top bar and the 600 px layout were inspected in
  screenshots; the panels are opaque (the stage no longer shows through their text).

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- **Found by the browser check (the real page):**
  - Choosing a scene, seeking or editing while the lesson was paused started playback (the page's narration start marks
    the lesson as playing): a paused editor now shows the scene still (its board revealed, its narration not started, a
    voiced AI video paused); Play starts it properly.
  - A new scene lost the selection about 300 ms after Duplicate / Insert (the editor followed the stage's scene even when
    paused): the selection follows the stage only while the lesson plays. In one run this made Delete remove the original
    diagram instead of its copy.
  - The first click after a timeline drag was ignored (the dragged block is redrawn, so its own click never cleared the
    guard): the guard now ends with the drag.
  - The editor's shortcuts could act while Visual Review was open over it; a hidden scene's finding made the top bar say
    "1 thing to review"; the timeline block showed its time with the transition into it (15 s against the list's 14 s);
    the scene list showed faintly through the inspector sheet on small screens.
- **Found by the security and correctness audit (read-only agent; each fixed and covered by a test):**
  - **Data loss:** Visual Review writes the scene at a position; after a failed editor save (structure locked during a
    generation) a camera change overwrote another scene on the server. The editor now saves first and sends nothing
    otherwise, and the review routes refuse (409) a scene whose id differs from the scene saved at that position.
  - Replies applied by a position taken before the request (plans, directions, approvals landing on other scenes after a
    move): replies are applied by scene id, and a plan made for an older order is discarded and asked again.
  - Lost updates: the save is a compare-and-set on the revision; the review routes return the new revision.
  - The structure lock was never refreshed (runs finishing or starting while the editor is open): the status is polled
    and structural commands wait (also when a draft or a stale save is replayed).
  - A draft replay was silent about edits it could not apply (now said); undoing a layout change lost an approval the
    user had given to that same layout (now given back).
  - Hardening: a huge integer (`1e400` written out) caused a 500; NaN / Infinity were stored (then every read failed);
    no size limit (30 MB of board HTML accepted); an old lesson's first save could reorder during a generation; asset
    references in other forms (`/api/assets/<id>/content`, upper-case ids, `presenter_plan.media_asset_id`) skipped the
    access check; a lesson with invalid editor data could never be saved again (now dropped on load); an inserted
    picture showed a broken image until reloaded; an old lesson got new random ids on every load (now derived from the
    lesson and position); the last visible scene could be deleted; a moved scene's quality findings about its old
    neighbours still looked current.
- **Found by the documentation fact-check (read-only agent, against the code):** Visual Review's "regenerate" buttons
  (composition and direction) still wrote a scene back by its position without the identity check (now refused with 409
  like the review routes, before any planning, and they return the revision); an old lesson's first save was refused
  while media was being generated when it also carried a property edit (its ids are now stored when the editor opens,
  before any edit); split, mute, narration, labels and background density were covered by unit tests only (the browser
  check now does them in the page). The drafts were corrected where they were imprecise (minimum durations of silent
  scenes, the quality fingerprint after structural edits, the inspector's sections, the small-screen layout).
- **Found while wiring:** the model wrote subtitles and labels the server refused (a subtitle up to 800 characters, label
  objects `{text, at}` now accepted).
- **Test expectations changed with the behaviour** (none weakened): the quality wiring tests follow the per-scene
  bookkeeping; the workspace test expects an undo to say it is an undo (`via: 'undo'`).

### Known limitations
- **The preview is the live stage.** The editor's panels sit over the full-screen stage, so its edges (the left of the
  title, a presenter on the right) can be under the scene list or the inspector while editing; "Preview" hides the
  panels and closing the inspector widens the view. Fitting the stage into the centre would mean transforming the shared
  renderer, which the export also records.
- **Splitting** is offered only where the narration has pause markers (no model cuts text); both parts share the board.
- **Structure during generation:** moving, adding, removing or splitting scenes waits while the lesson has AI runs in
  progress (they are attached by scene position); property edits are allowed.
- **Quality re-checks after a structural edit:** a scene's fingerprint includes its place, so inserting, deleting or moving
  a scene re-checks the scenes after it (a report takes tens of milliseconds); a property edit re-checks only that scene.
- **Approvals after a move:** moving scenes recomposes their neighbours, whose composition approvals follow their own
  fingerprints (shown per scene); nothing is approved or generated by the editor.
- **Trust:** `/save-history` and the review routes keep their earlier trust (the editor's own route validates fully;
  `/save-history` cleans the lesson's editor data, and invalid scene editor data is dropped when the editor loads);
  assets are still checked where they are used. Drafts stay in the browser after logout (the commands, not media).
- **Lesson style** is changed in Phase 17's settings on the start screen; the editor changes a scene's accent and
  background density. The board's HTML has no field in the inspector (title, subtitle, narration and labels do).
- **"Show in Visual Review"** opens Visual Review for the lesson, not at the selected scene. Giving an approval back on
  undo is remembered for the editing session only.
- **No real-time collaboration** (two tabs are kept consistent by revisions and conflicts, not merged live), no
  frame-accurate trimming inside a scene, no audio editing beyond muting, 16:9 only.

### Deferred to later phases
Phase 20 (end-to-end studio), Phase 21. The editing model (commands by scene id, `scene.edit`, `payload.editor`) is the
extension point.

### Final status
**Complete.**
- **Verified end to end:** an editing layer over the generated lesson — scene identity, reorder (drag, buttons, keys),
  duplicate, delete, insert (blank, library picture, copy), split at pause markers, hide, minimum duration, narration,
  mute, captions, title / subtitle / labels, presenter, visual, camera, transition, background, scene accent; undo /
  redo; autosave in place with revisions and conflict recovery; drafts; Phase 18 quality per scene; Visual Review's
  approval rules kept; the real page edited, saved, reloaded, previewed and exported in Chrome, the export following
  every edit and matching the preview.
- **Not verified:** nothing that needs a real AI provider (the editor makes no model call).
- **Unchanged:** Phases 13–18's systems and responsibilities (one renderer, planner, synchronization, styles, quality,
  Visual Review, Asset Library, AI cache, recovery, export); no media is generated, approved or deleted by the editor.
- **Nothing committed or deployed.** No database schema change (the editor data lives in the lesson's JSON).

---

## Phase 20 — End-to-End AI Educational Video Studio

### Objective
Turn Phases 1–19 into one workflow a user can follow from source material to an exported video — create, import,
analyse, write the lesson, review, media, presenter, compose, synchronise, style, edit, quality, Visual Review, preview,
export, reopen — without having to know the internal systems. No second document parser, media router, media cache,
provider layer, job system, presenter, synchronization, style, quality, review, asset library or exporter was built; the
new server code is the orchestration itself (the lesson-writing run with its screenplay check, the derived state, the
checkpoints) and the classic "Generate Video" path (`/generate-script`) is kept as it was.
Phase 21 (UI/UX refinement) and the final sample-video phase were not started.

### Baseline (start of Phase 20)
Backend 803/803, frontend 342/342, Phase 1 27/27, Phase 2 30/30, export with library assets 35/35, Phase 3 25/26 (the
development database's library page), Phase 4 24/24, Phase 5 11/11, Phase 6 17/17, Phase 7 18/18, Phase 8 12/12,
Phase 9 14/14, Phase 10 17/17, Phase 11 18/18, Phase 12 20/20, Phase 13 24/24, Phase 14 17/17, Phase 15 23/23,
Phase 16 17/17, Phase 17 21/21, Phase 18 22/22, Phase 19 52/52.

### Audit (before Phase 20; three read-only analysts plus the orchestrator)
- **Frontend:** no workflow state; a freshly written lesson played at once (no review step); media generation lived in
  three places with different links to the lesson (the stage's lazy generation, Visual Review, and a lesson batch that ran
  only during an export); six save sites each created a new history entry; a new lesson could inherit the previous one's
  id and editor data; the export history was not filtered by lesson; quality lived only inside Visual Review.
- **Backend:** the screenplay was written by the page streaming `/generate-script` (lost if the tab closed, no stand-in
  for tests, no recovery); no lesson-level state; every in-place writer read, changed and rewrote the lesson without
  comparing its revision, so a background result or a review could overwrite an editor save made in between; results
  attached by scene position; `project_id` unchecked on the per-scene generation endpoints; exports did not record which
  version of the lesson they show.
- **Tests:** no stand-in could write a screenplay, so no keyless end-to-end test from a document to an export existed.

### Architecture
```
 Studio (studio.js) ── adapter (index.html) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   Content   → Document Assistant (Phase 11: parse, structure, traceability, edits, lesson input)            │
   Lesson    → POST /api/studio/lessons: a durable "lesson_script" run (Phase 9 runs, leases, recovery) with   │
               the page's own prompt (getSystemPrompt) → the model (source_documents.call_model; the stand-in   │
               on test servers) → check_screenplay → trace → save_lesson (the /save-history path) → the lesson   │
   Visuals   → the Visual Router plan + the existing lesson batch (Phases 4/5/8/9: library first, the cache,     │
               Visual Review decisions; nothing made twice); per-scene attention items: Retry · Choose existing  │
               (Visual Review) · Continue without                                                                │
   Presenter / Style → the existing Phase 12 / Phase 17 panels, moved into the Studio while it shows them         │
   Review & edit → Visual Review (Phase 6) · the editor (Phase 19, over the live stage, prepared paused) ·        │
               the quality check (Phase 18, recorded as a checkpoint)                                            │
   Preview / Export → the live page (one renderer) · the export (Phase 2/7), linked to the lesson's fingerprint   │
                                                                                                                 │
 GET /api/studio/lessons/{id}: the lesson's state DERIVED from what is stored (payload, runs, exports,           │
 checkpoints) — never a second state machine; PUT …/checkpoint records the user's decisions in payload.studio ───┘
```
The single source of truth stays the lesson (payload.scenes with `scene.edit`, Visual Review's records, `payload.editor`,
`payload.cinematic_style`), as in Phase 19: preview, quality, review and export all read it.

### The Studio workflow (studio.js, the adapter in index.html)
- **Entries:** "🧭 Studio" on the start screen and on the player bar (the lesson pauses while the Studio is open).
- **Home:** Create a lesson — *From a document* (the start screen's file chooser, then the Phase 11 Document Assistant:
  structure, issues, traceability, edits; "Use for Lesson Generation" hands the prepared source to the Studio, then a short
  "Name your lesson" step) or *From structured content* (paste text; or open a `.json` lesson file, saved as a lesson of
  its own). *Your lessons*: each with its stage (icon and words), Open / Continue; lessons still being written can be
  followed.
- **Writing progress:** the run's real stages only (Understanding the source ✓ when a source was prepared · Writing the
  lesson (the model's answer and its format check) · Checking the lesson (traceability, scene ids) · Saving the lesson);
  Cancel; on failure "We couldn't write the lesson from this content. Your source is safe." with Try again. On completion
  the lesson opens (no autoplay; the URL keeps `?project_id=`). A lesson that finishes while the Studio is closed is
  listed on Home (not opened by itself); after a reload, Try again goes Home to choose the content again.
- **The lesson's seven stages:** 1 Content (where the scenes come from: from your document / AI-written / edited by you;
  Confirm structure) · 2 Lesson (review and edit the scenes in the editor; Looks right) · 3 Visuals & presenter (ready /
  being made / need attention; Prepare visuals; per-scene attention items with Retry · Choose existing · Continue without;
  the presenter panel) · 4 Style (the style panel; "Changing the style never re-makes pictures or clips") · 5 Review & edit
  (Visual Review, the editor, the quality check: "✓ Quality looks good" / "⚠ N things to review", marked changed since the
  check when the lesson changed) · 6 Preview (the whole lesson from the start, on the live page) · 7 Export (the export;
  the latest video and whether it matches the lesson as it is now; your videos). Each stage: ✓ Done · ● In progress ·
  ○ Not yet · ⚠ Needs attention, one truthful line, its actions (the rail is worked out in the browser from the server's
  state). No provider, model, run id or fingerprint is shown unless `?visualDebug`. Errors are plain sentences with
  "Try again"; nothing is ever shown as a stack trace. When no lesson writer is set up on the server, Home says so before
  the user names a lesson.
- **Stepping aside:** the editor, the preview and the export need the live stage, so the Studio closes while they are
  open; the editor hands back to it on close (the preview and the export do not; the 🧭 button opens it again). Visual
  Review opens above it, and the Studio refreshes when Visual Review closes and when the page becomes visible again. The
  Preview stage is marked as started in this browser when the preview starts.

### Writing the lesson (studio.py, studio_screenplay.py)
- **A durable run:** `POST /api/studio/lessons` (`system_prompt` = the page's own prompt, `text` or a prepared
  `source`, names, provider, model, the chosen style) creates an `AIGenerationRun` of kind `lesson_script`, modelled on
  Phase 11's analysis run: lease, heartbeat, cancel, and `ai_recovery` continues it after a restart (a run that had already
  saved its lesson finishes without asking the model again). An identical request while one is running joins it.
  `GET /api/studio/runs/{id}` / `POST …/cancel` follow and cancel it (owner only).
- **The model:** `source_documents.call_model` (the text-model call Phase 11 uses), now with the time a whole lesson needs
  (600 s) and, for Gemini, the output room the page's `/generate-script` gives (65,536 tokens; OpenAI keeps its default).
  An unavailable writer is a plain 503; on test servers (`AI_FAKE_PROVIDER=1`) an unconfigured provider uses the stand-in,
  recorded as such. Lesson writing runs in its own small pool of threads (3; `LESSON_WRITER_THREADS`), never the shared
  one; a user can have two lessons being written at once (a third is refused in plain words, 429; an identical request
  joins the running one). A prepared source's text is cut at 262,144 characters (as the classic path does). Cancel stops
  the run at once; a model call already under way finishes in the background and its answer is discarded.
- **The screenplay contract:** `check_screenplay` takes the format the page's prompt asks for and its own parser accepts
  (an object with scenes or a bare list; 1–200 scenes; at most 8 million characters) and repairs what it can rather than
  refusing: unknown scene types and quizzes the player cannot ask become content scenes, keys the application owns (plans,
  review records, ids) are removed, texts are bounded, NaN refused; one bounded repair request for a malformed answer.
- **The stand-in (test servers only):** a deterministic writer built only from the source text — title, sections as
  content scenes, definitions highlighted, steps as a process list, formulas kept for MathJax, code as highlighted blocks,
  tables as comparisons, a "Diagram:" line as a side-panel picture whose keywords come from that line (the Visual Router
  can match a library asset), Question/Answer material as a quiz (its other choices are the source's own terms), a
  presenter-led explanation and a summary; tables as a plain table; narration with `[SYNC]` / `[PAUSE]` like real lessons.
  It adds no facts: its words come from the source (the title may come from the names the user gave).
- **Traceability:** `trace` marks every scene `scene.source = {origin: "source" | "ai", refs, coverage}` by deterministic
  word overlap with the text the writer received (it works for real models too); "edited by you" comes from Phase 19's
  `scene.edit.original`. The lesson keeps `source_document` (Phase 11 ids) and `payload.studio.generated` (run, provider,
  model, time).
- **One save path:** `save_lesson` (the `/save-history` body, unchanged behaviour) saves a written lesson with scene ids,
  `payload.editor.generated_order` and `payload.studio`.

### The lesson's state (derived) and checkpoints
- `GET /api/studio/lessons/{id}`: names, revision, `fingerprint` (sha256 of what the video shows — scenes, editor data,
  style; not the lesson's names, concept map or companion sheet — with volatile parts removed the way Phase 18 removes
  them: every `url` key, link tokens, review and creation times, AI records, notes, `scene.source`), source, origins, media (needed /
  ready / generating / attention / failed, with per-scene items), Visual Review counts, style, editor counts, checkpoints,
  quality (with `stale` when the fingerprint changed since the check), exports (latest, and whether the newest finished
  video matches the current fingerprint) and `stage`, derived in this order: draft (no visible scenes) · exporting · generating
  (a media run is active) · needs attention (a run needs a person, a visual failed with nothing in its place, or a current
  quality check is blocked) · completed (the newest finished video matches the lesson) · ready to export (a current quality
  check is good or has things to review — findings never block the export, as before) · review.
- `GET /api/studio/lessons`: the user's lessons (at most 50) with their stages, lessons still being written (at most 10),
  and whether a lesson writer is set up on this server. Each lesson's summary is cached by its revision and the state of
  its runs and exports (a call with 50 large lessons went from about 2–6 s to under 0.1 s); the list works stages out
  without the library check the lesson's own state does, so the two can differ for a visual whose library asset is gone.
- `PUT /api/studio/lessons/{id}/checkpoint` (structure, lesson, visuals, quality, export; the UI sets structure, lesson
  and quality; `null` removes one): the user's decisions, kept in `payload.studio.checkpoints` with the fingerprint of the
  lesson they were made on — the page sends the fingerprint it read before running the quality check, so a lesson changed
  meanwhile is recorded as already changed (compare-and-set; never a new history entry).
- `/save-history` keeps `payload.studio` (cleaned) and every later save of an opened lesson carries it and its
  `source_document`; a newly written lesson never inherits the previous lesson's id, editor data or source.

### User edits always win (editor_api.update_lesson)
- Every server-side in-place writer of a lesson — a background visual, presenter clip or background attaching, Visual
  Review's, the presenter's, the composition's and the direction's review routes, regenerate, directions, the style — now
  goes through `update_lesson`: it re-applies its change to the freshly loaded lesson and writes only if the lesson's
  revision is still the one it read (`UPDATE … WHERE updated_at = …`), else reloads and re-applies; after four lost races
  it gives up (409) rather than overwrite; NaN / Infinity are refused (422) before anything is written. The editor's own
  save keeps its Phase 19 compare-and-set. Revisions always move forward (`next_stamp`). A background attach that changes
  nothing writes nothing. (The page's older save paths — "Save version", the raw script editor, the save before an export
  of an unsaved lesson — still create a history entry each, as before.)
- **A review never reverts newer content:** the review routes used to replace the saved scene with the page's copy, so a
  decision from an older tab reverted an edit saved meanwhile. Now (`reviewed_scene`) the saved scene is the base — its
  texts, board, editor data — and the page's copy contributes only the review's own records (per slot) and the media the
  page made in the preview; a page plan showing nothing never replaces a saved one that shows something. A lesson the
  editor never saved (no scene ids) behaves as before.
- **Scene identity for background results:** the lesson batch, the page's AI videos and its Manim renders record the
  scene's id; a finished result finds its scene by id (`scene_for_run`; nothing lands if the scene is gone) and fills a
  slot only if it is still empty, checked on the very payload being written. (Pictures the page makes while preparing a
  scene are not linked to the lesson: they are cached and the plan finds them; presenter clips attach by position, and
  moving scenes waits while the lesson has runs in progress, Phase 19.)
- **Precedence (as implemented):** the user's explicit choice (Visual Review decisions, editor edits, the chosen style) →
  approved changes → the stored lesson plan → AI suggestions (the router, the composer, the director) → defaults. A
  background job never replaces a visual the user chose, an editor edit, or the style.

### Media, presenter, style, editor, quality, review
- **Media:** "Prepare visuals" plans the lesson (library first, the AI cache, Visual Review decisions, Manim renders kept)
  and starts the existing lesson batch for what is still missing (images, or images and videos, per the start screen's
  AI Visuals choice; off: library and existing media only; presenter clips are made in Visual Review). Batch runs
  continue with the page closed and after a restart. Items that need a person or failed are listed per scene: Retry (a run
  waiting for a person: its Phase 9 retry; a failed AI video: made again for that scene; a failed picture: the batch again;
  a presenter clip: Visual Review at that scene), Choose existing (Visual Review at that scene), Continue without (the
  user's decision recorded in Visual Review: the visual is removed, so it is not made again). A failed presenter clip or
  background is listed but not counted (the lesson plays with its fallback). Style, camera, layout, captions, the quality
  check and reopening never generate media; preparing the stage (for the editor or playback) may make missing AI side
  pictures in the Auto modes, as playback always did (cached, then reused).
- **Presenter and style:** the existing panels (Phase 12 presenter, Phase 17 styles with their versioned contract) are
  moved into the Studio's stages while shown and put back afterwards; a style change keeps approvals and media as before.
- **Editor:** opened from the Studio over the live stage, prepared paused (no intro, nothing spoken) on the current scene
  (the first that plays when the lesson was just opened); back to the Studio on close. Fixed on the way: a still preview could outlive the pause on a silent or muted
  scene and swallow the next scene's narration once playback started (the flag is now cleared whenever the lesson plays).
- **Quality:** the Phase 18 check, run from the Studio as from Visual Review; its result (status, counts) is recorded as
  the "quality" checkpoint with the fingerprint, so a reopened lesson knows whether it is still current.
- **Visual Review** stays authoritative (approve, change, remove, choose from the library, another AI version); nothing
  is approved automatically.

### Preview, export, reopen
- **Preview** is the live page (one renderer): from the start with its intro, or — when the stage already shows the
  lesson (after the editor) — playing from the current scene.
- **Export** is the existing exporter; the recording's timeline now carries the lesson link `{project_id, fingerprint,
  revision}` (taken from the server after any pending editor save), kept only for the export's own lesson. The video
  history opens on "This lesson" (with "All videos") and says "Matches the current lesson" / "Made before your latest
  changes".
- **Reopen:** a lesson opened from the Studio (or `?project_id=`) restores its scenes, editor data, style, source link and
  Studio checkpoints; its state (media in progress, attention items, quality currency, latest export) is derived again
  from the server, and runs still going continue on the server (Phase 9).

### Security
- New routes check ownership (another user's lesson, run, source or export: 404); inputs bounded (prompt 60,000, text
  262,144 characters, the request read in pieces and refused past 1.5 MB, characters that cannot be stored refused with a
  plain 422; checkpoint values bounded); the stand-in exists only with `AI_FAKE_PROVIDER=1` (a stand-in run left in a
  database is failed, not continued, by a server without it); recovery never writes a second lesson for one run and a
  cancel that arrives after the lesson was saved keeps it; per-scene generation endpoints refuse another user's
  `project_id`; an export's lesson link must name its own lesson; the Studio's UI uses text only (no HTML from the
  server).

### Multi-agent work
Three read-only analysts mapped the frontend journey, the backend and the tests before the plan (the integration contract
in the orchestrator's notes). Four implementation agents with sole ownership of their files: **B** (studio.py,
studio_screenplay.py, server.py; minimal additive edits to ai_providers.py, ai_media.py, manim_jobs.py), **M**
(editor_api.py, visuals.py, presenters.py, cinematic.py: override-safe writes), **K** (exports.py, export_outputs.py,
export.js: the lesson link and history), **L** (studio.js, studio.css). The orchestrator wired index.html and
visuals.js, integrated, and fixed what the reviews found. Then **N** (the end-to-end browser check, the only agent running
browsers), **P** (a read-only security / failure audit with probes against a throwaway database) and a read-only
documentation fact-check. The orchestrator inspected the rendered frames of both lessons itself.

### Files changed
- **New:** `studio.py`, `studio_screenplay.py`, `studio.js`, `studio.css`; tests `tests/test_studio.py`,
  `tests/test_studio_screenplay.py`, `tests/test_lesson_writes.py`, `tests/test_export_lesson_link.py`,
  `tests/studio.test.js`, `tests/studio_wiring.test.js`, `tests/export_history.test.js`, `tests/studio_browser_check.mjs`,
  and two representative source documents `tests/fixtures/studio/photosynthesis.txt`, `newtons_second_law.txt`.
- **Changed:** `server.py` (save_lesson, the Studio router and run kind, the Visual Router planner for the Studio,
  ownership of `project_id` on the per-scene generation endpoints, `scene_id` on them and in the lesson batch, static
  routes), `editor_api.py` (update_lesson, reviewed_scene, next_stamp, LessonChanged), `visuals.py`, `presenters.py`,
  `cinematic.py` (writers through update_lesson; scenes found by id; the style route returns the revision),
  `exports.py`, `export_outputs.py`, `export.js` (lesson link, history filter), `ai_providers.py` / `ai_media.py`
  (`scene_id` carried in run requests), `manim_jobs.py` (render `scene_id`), `source_documents.py` (call_model's output
  room and timeout), `visuals.js` (`sceneId` on per-scene videos), `cinematic.js` (wide formulas and tables fitted),
  `index.html` (the Studio entries and adapter; a new lesson's state reset; source and Studio record kept by every save;
  scene ids on generation requests; the export's lesson hooks and caption reset; planner replies checked against layout
  decisions; the skill tree refitted; the quiz explanation's room; caption sentences; a still preview cleared once
  playing), `tests/test_styles.py` (the style reply now includes its revision).

### Results
Silent regression (Chrome with `--disable-audio-output`, speech stubbed, no real AI provider call; the Studio and editor
checks run their own servers with the stand-in writer, media and voice). Everything ran on the final code (no code changed
during the full run):

|---|---|
| Backend (unittest) | **879/879** (803 before + 76 Phase 20) |
| Frontend (node --test) | **394/394** (342 before + 52 Phase 20) |
| **Phase 20 Studio check** | **69/69** (two lessons through the real Studio, plus a partial-failure scenario) |
| Phase 1 mascot | 27/27 |
| Phase 2 export | 30/30 |
| Export with library assets | 35/35 |
| Phase 3 assets | 25/26 (see below) |

- **Phase 3 (25/26):** unchanged since Phase 18 — the shared Aadhi clips fall past the library's first page (200 items,
  newest first) because the development database holds many test uploads from earlier runs; the filter lists them.
- **The Studio check** (`tests/studio_browser_check.mjs`, about 33 minutes): lesson 1 from `photosynthesis.txt` through the
  whole workflow — Studio → From a document → Document Assistant (structure, traceability) → the durable lesson run
  (real progress steps) → Content (from your document: 12 scenes) → Prepare visuals (one picture made, nothing made twice;
  a library match counted as ready) → presenter and Corporate Training style from the mounted panels (no generation on the
  style change) → cinematic compositions and synchronization present → the editor (reorder, an 18 s minimum, a library
  picture, presenter side, camera and transition, undo, redo, saved in place, paused throughout) → quality from the Studio
  (findings shown; one edit marks only that scene changed since the check) → Visual Review (approve; counts agree) →
  Preview (the whole lesson from its first scene, hidden scene skipped; seek) → Export (the file checked with ffprobe; the
  subtitles and chapters in the edited order; no stale caption) → history "This lesson · Matches the current lesson" →
  reload: edits, approvals and the quality state as they were, stage ✓ Video ready; replaying it keeps the match. Lesson 2
  from `newtons_second_law.txt` pasted as structured content: written, styled, previewed, exported (its diagram is the
  library cart; nothing generated). Overrides: a Visual Review choice survives the lesson batch; a style chosen while the
  batch runs stays. Partial failure (the stand-in failing every AI picture): the two failed pictures listed per scene;
  Retry makes it again through the batch; Continue without removes it in Visual Review; Choose existing resolves it in
  Visual Review. Nothing is generated except by Prepare visuals or Retry; no page errors; no provider names in the Studio,
  the editor or the export panel (Visual Review's Phase 8 provenance line and the presenter panel's collapsed advanced
  settings excepted); all silent.

### LLM validation / real providers
**Not done for real.** No text, image or video provider key exists on this machine. Lessons were written by the
deterministic stand-in writer on test servers (`AI_FAKE_PROVIDER=1`), pictures made by the stand-in image provider, voice
by the stand-in tone (`FAKE_TTS=1`). The real-provider path of the lesson writer (`call_model` with Gemini / OpenAI) has
no test. Two audit probes let one real Gemini client call through with a dummy key ("k") while checking that a production
server refuses the stand-in; with no valid key it could only fail, and no lesson or media came from it.

### Visual quality validation (two lessons, rendered and inspected)
- **Two lessons from different sources** (biology: photosynthesis, 12 scenes; physics: Newton's second law, 12 scenes —
  introduction, definitions, explanation, process steps, a diagram (a stand-in picture; the library's cart), a display
  formula, Python code, a comparison table, a presenter-led explanation, Aadhi's intro, a quiz, key takeaways, a summary,
  captions, transitions) were written, edited (lesson 1), styled (Corporate Training; lesson 2 its own), previewed and
  exported to video in real Chrome at 1280×720, and the frames of every representative scene were inspected in the preview
  and in the exported video, three rounds in all. The first rounds found the defects listed below (a cut-off formula and
  table, an overflowing or tiny skill tree, a cut-off quiz explanation, a caption cut at "0.", a stale first subtitle, a
  false definition in the stand-in's text); each was fixed and the lessons rendered again.
- **The final videos:** the formula is whole (made smaller to fit its narrow board beside the presenter); the comparison
  table shows all its columns; definitions are boxed and highlighted; the process steps are numbered and readable; the code
  panel is dark with Prism colours; the skill tree fits its card in every scene; the quiz shows its four choices (the
  source's own terms), the right answer and its explanation inside the board; the presenter (Aadhi Teacher) keeps its side
  and name card; captions are the narration's sentences, in the style's caption box; the style (cards, typography, accent)
  stays the same from scene to scene; scene order, hidden scenes and the 18 s hold follow the edits. Source meaning is
  kept (every scene traces to the source; the stand-in adds no facts).
- **Preview versus export:** the exported frames match the preview's (mean difference 1.8–3.0 of 255 for lesson 1 and
  2.8–4.5 for lesson 2, the preview's controls excluded); both videos are VP9 1280×720 with audible Opus audio, as long as
  their lessons, with no scene flash, no missing media and the scenes in the edited order.
- **Seen, not changed:** a narration sentence long enough for two caption lines can touch the presenter's name card (the
  composer keeps one caption line clear; as in Phases 13–19).

### Issues found and fixed during the phase
- **Found by the security / failure audit (read-only agent, probes against a throwaway database; each fixed with a test):**
  a Visual Review decision sent from an older copy of a scene reverted an edit saved meanwhile (now merged on the saved
  scene); no limit on lessons being written at once (two per user; a dedicated writer pool so media and export work never
  waits); the body limit was bypassed by a chunked request; a lone surrogate gave a 500; a stand-in run left in a database
  would have been completed by a production server; recovery could write a second lesson; a cancel after the save hid
  the saved lesson; the home list re-derived every lesson on each poll (now cached: ~0.1 s for 50 large lessons instead of
  2–6 s); the quality checkpoint could claim a check of content it never saw; `/render` carried no scene id; NaN could be
  stored by an in-place write. No HIGH finding: every new and changed route refuses another user's lesson, run, source or
  export.
- **Found by the documentation fact-check (read-only agent):** a lesson made with the classic Generate button inherited
  the previous lesson's Studio record (its checkpoints); Retry and Continue without failed for failed visuals (the run's
  resolve only accepts runs waiting for a person), and a dismissed visual was queued again by the next batch (Continue
  without is now Visual Review's removal); a failed presenter clip was counted as needing attention; the "no lesson writer
  on this server" notice was never shown; the Prepare visuals button promised presenter clips the batch never makes.
- **Found by the end-to-end browser check (the real Studio, two lessons):** the Visuals stage read the server's
  "still needed" count as the total (it said "No pictures or clips to make" with one ready); the Style stage updated only
  on the next refresh; an undo of a layout change was overwritten by an older lesson re-plan still on its way (a re-plan
  whose scenes' layout decisions changed meanwhile is now planned again); the Studio's Visual Review counts skipped scene
  visuals with nothing to generate; Preview after the editor only resumed the still scene (it now plays the lesson from
  its first scene); a long formula was cut off and a table overflowed on a narrow board (now fitted to its box, never below
  45 %); the exported subtitles began with a caption left on screen by an earlier preview; replaying a lesson after its
  export made the video "no longer match" (the planner's re-derived direction was part of the fingerprint); a visual
  matched from the library counted as still needed (the Studio now asks the Visual Router, which never generates); a
  provider's bracketed detail showed in a failed item.
- **Found by the orchestrator's inspection of the rendered frames:** the skill tree drawn at full size over its card's
  edge in the export (it was fitted once, before its panel had its size) or left tiny in another scene — now refitted
  whenever its panel's size changes; the quiz's revealed explanation cut off at the board's bottom (the finished countdown
  kept its room); captions cut a number at its decimal point ("Acceleration 0." for 0.5 m/s²); the test stand-in took
  "Every green plant is …" for a definition (a Definition box and a nonsense quiz choice).
- **Found while integrating:** `studio.js` / `studio.css` were not served (static routes added); a still preview could
  outlive the pause on a silent or muted scene and swallow the next scene's narration once playback started.
- **Test expectations changed with the behaviour** (none weakened): the style route's reply has a revision; the Studio's
  attention count leaves out failed presenter clips (listed, not counted, as on the server); its Visual Review counts are
  Visual Review's own; the panel tests' media counts use the server's meaning ("still needed"); the stand-in's lesson 1
  introduction is presenter-led (it no longer has a false definition).

### Known limitations
- **No real provider was used** (no keys here): the lesson writer's real-model path, real pictures, clips, presenters and
  voices are untested in this phase; the lessons' wording in the rendered videos is the test stand-in's (built from the
  source sentences), not a model's.
- **The UI is functional, not refined** (Phase 21): the Studio is an overlay over the existing page; the classic start
  screen and its "Generate Video" path remain; Visual Review keeps its Phase 8 "AI provider" provenance line, and the
  presenter panel's collapsed "Advanced presenter settings" list the providers (Phase 12).
- **Captions longer than one line** can rise into the presenter's name card (the composer keeps one caption line clear;
  existed before Phase 20).
- **The export's opening chapter is always "Introduction"** (Phase 7), so a lesson whose first scene is also called
  Introduction has two chapters of that name.
- **Steps that need the page open:** the voice, presenter speech timings, the composition planning and the recording run
  in the browser, as before; only lesson writing and media generation continue with the page closed.
- **Pictures the page makes while preparing a scene** are cached but not linked to the lesson by scene id; presenter
  clips attach by position (moving scenes waits while runs are in progress).
- **Drafts of the Phase 11 analysis:** the Document Assistant does not list `$$…$$` display equations as formulas (Phase
  11's structural analysis); the stand-in still keeps them in the lesson.
- **Single-user consistency:** two tabs are kept safe by revisions, compare-and-set and the review merge, not merged live;
  two decisions on the same slot at once: the later wins.

### Deferred to later phases
Phase 21 (UI/UX refinement: navigation, simplification, responsive and accessibility polish) and the final sample-video
phase (not started; no sample video or sample workflow was made). The Studio's adapter, the derived lesson state and the
checkpoints are the extension points.

### Final status
**Complete.**
- **Verified end to end through the real application:** create → import a source (a document through Phase 11's assistant,
  or pasted structured content) → analyse (structure, traceability) → write the lesson (a durable, recoverable server run
  with the page's own prompt; source-traced scenes) → review the scenes → prepare media (the Visual Router and the
  existing lesson batch; library and cache first; nothing made twice) → presenter → compose and synchronise (Phases 13–16,
  unchanged) → style (Phase 17, no media remade) → edit (Phase 19) → quality (Phase 18, recorded with the lesson's
  fingerprint) → Visual Review (authoritative; nothing approved automatically) → preview (the live page) → export (the
  existing exporter, linked to the lesson's version) → reopen (edits, approvals, style and quality state kept; runs
  continue on the server). User edits win over every background write (compare-and-set, the review merge, scenes found by
  id). Two lessons were rendered and their videos inspected; preview and export match.
- **Not verified:** any real AI provider (no keys on this machine): the lesson writer's real-model path, real pictures,
  clips, presenters and voices; the lessons' wording is the test stand-in's.
- **Unchanged:** Phases 1–19's systems and responsibilities; the classic start screen and "Generate Video" path are kept.
- **Nothing committed or deployed.** No database schema change (the Studio record lives in the lesson's JSON).

---

## Phase 21 — UI/UX Refinement, Simplification & Product Polish

### Objective
Make the working Phase 20 product feel like one finished educational application — "Aadhi — Educational Video Studio" —
that an educator can use without knowing providers, caches, runs, fingerprints or rendering internals: clear navigation,
one design system, plain words, useful empty / loading / success / error states, keyboard and screen-reader support,
intentional tablet and narrow layouts, and the known Phase 20 issues resolved. No new generation system, engine, state
machine or feature. What the exported video records changed only where it was designed to: captions that fit their band,
and one plain card in place of the developer text the stage used to show (and record) for a missing visual; the chapter
list's opening may now be called "Opening". The final sample-video phase was not started.

### Baseline (start of Phase 21)
The full Phase 1–20 regression that closed Phase 20, on the unchanged code: backend 879/879, frontend 394/394, Phase 20
Studio 69/69, Phase 19 editor 52/52, every other suite green except the known Phase 3 25/26 (the development database's
library page).

### Audit (before Phase 21; five read-only analysts)
- **Product architecture:** no way back Home once a lesson was open (Assets, history and every setting became
  unreachable); a failed classic generation left its overlay covering the start screen; developer output was drawn on
  the stage and so recorded into videos ("[System Debug]…", raw scene JSON, generated code, provider error text, manual
  workflow file paths); duplicate entry points (Generate Video vs the Studio, Analyze & Prepare vs "From a document",
  View History vs "Your lessons", four ways to the videos, two script editors, the Info modal vs Visual Review, Restore
  Previous, Play Demo playing the previous lesson); unlabelled icon buttons; provider/model jargon on the start screen;
  mixed names (Presentation, Script, Slide, Video for a lesson).
- **Design system:** ~850 hard-coded colours, 36 radii, 61 font sizes, ~33 button classes in 8 families, 10 modal
  implementations, no shared tokens; focus styles on few controls; sub-12 px text; all-caps headings; the lesson's video
  style leaking into the product UI (Visual Review's layout frame, the control bar on light styles, the Studio's own
  `--st-*` token names colliding with Phase 17's).
- **Security / performance:** no cross-user access through any route; but a Giphy key in the page, the previous user's
  lesson visible after switching accounts in one tab, lesson p5 code in an unsandboxed same-origin frame, unescaped
  server text; the Studio's lesson state up to ~1 s on large lessons (polled every 4 s while media is made).
- **Known issues:** the two-line caption touching the presenter's name card (a fraction-of-height caption zone vs a
  fixed-pixel caption), the duplicate "Introduction" chapter, the shared Aadhi assets past the Library's first page, the
  Phase 8 "AI provider" line in Visual Review.
- **Browser (five viewports, real Chrome):** the editor's inspector and timeline overlapping at 1024 and 820 px; a
  reopened lesson's start card a dead end; the player bar running off-screen on tablets; overlays not managing focus;
  contradictory stage statuses; titles shown twice; reduced motion ignored outside the stage.

### Navigation and information architecture
- **One product top bar:** Home · Create · Library · Videos · Settings (accessible names; hidden while an export
  records; out of the way while a lesson plays). Home stops a playing lesson cleanly, closes it and opens the Studio on
  "Your lessons"; Create opens the Studio's "How would you like to start?"; Library and Videos open the existing panels;
  Settings opens the settings dialog.
- **Start screen:** the product name, one primary "Create a lesson", "Open your lessons", a compact drop zone and "See
  an example lesson" (always the built-in example). The classic one-step generation stays available under Settings →
  Advanced; a document goes through the Document Assistant into the Studio's naming step and a `.json` lesson file opens
  as a lesson (unless the classic one-step generation is switched on in Settings → Advanced).
- **Lesson start card** (a reopened lesson): Open in Studio · Play the lesson · Export or see this lesson's videos ·
  Review the visuals · Back to Home — no dead end.
- **Settings dialog** (the former start-screen controls, moved, not copied): General (account, sign out; "Add a user"
  for the admin), Lesson defaults (presenter and style panels — the Studio still mounts them into its stages), Visuals &
  AI, Voice, Advanced (the AI writer's service and model, how AI videos are made with "Start the AI video service", the
  classic generation, developer downloads; the raw lesson-file editor and the scene list only with `?visualDebug`).
- **Player bar:** every button named; a keyboard-accessible More menu (the practice sheet, reload, manual recording, and
  with `?visualDebug`, "Developer tools…" opening Settings → Advanced); the four lesson tools (Review · Edit · Studio ·
  Export) named on wide screens (1280 px and up) and tablets (641–1100 px), icons only (still named for screen readers)
  in between and on phones; opaque (legible on light lesson styles); wraps on tablets; its progress line no longer
  crosses the captions. Like a video player's controls it shows while the lesson is paused and, while it plays, on
  activity (the pointer moving, a tap, a key or a focus move; hidden 3 s later, but kept under the pointer, with
  keyboard focus in it or while its More menu is open) — no longer whenever the pointer is on the page, nor always on
  touch screens, where it covered the caption band. While the bar shows, the caption is lifted 8 px above it (a
  transform from the bar's measured height, so the caption fit still measures the caption at its own place) and the
  presenter's name card, which the lifted caption would cover, steps aside until the bar goes. Preview only: never while
  an export records (the bar is hidden then).
- **Consolidated / removed:** the View History modal (Studio Home lists lessons), Restore Previous and the dead start-up
  restore code (Studio Continue), the duplicated video entries (one "Your videos" panel, opened from the top bar, the
  player bar, the start card and the Studio's Export stage), the raw editor and Info modal moved to Advanced / debug.

### Design system (ui.css)
- One token layer loaded first (`--ui-*`: surfaces, text, muted, primary gold, accent, success / warning / danger /
  info, border, focus, disabled; spacing 4–32 px; radius 6–18 px and pill; three shadows; one type scale with a 12 px
  floor; control sizes; the documented z-index ladder) and opt-in `.ui-*` classes (buttons: default, primary, danger and
  link, small and large; a focus ring; visually-hidden text). Only `:root` and `.ui-*` selectors, literal values (never
  the lesson style's `--st-*` or the stage's tokens) — a static test checks it, and studio.css, editor.css and
  product.css are each checked for the lesson style's tokens.
- Adopted by the Studio (its colliding `--st-*` names renamed), the editor (its colour tokens mapped), Visual Review,
  the Library, the export panel, the Document Assistant, the start screen, the control bar and the dialogs
  (`product.css`, loaded after the page's own styles, holds the product-level overrides). Product colours never follow
  the lesson style (measured identical across the four Phase 17 styles). Sentence case instead of uppercase headings and
  buttons; visible focus on every control; text ≥ 12 px; reduced motion for the product UI (the stage keeps its own
  rules: it is the video).

### The Studio
- **Home "Your lessons":** one "Create a lesson"; lessons as plain rows (the title wraps; a chip in words: Being
  written, Ready to edit, Generating visuals, Needs attention, Ready for review, Ready to export, Exporting the video,
  Video ready; when it was updated; Continue). Empty: "You haven't created a lesson yet. Turn a document or your notes
  into an educational video." Loading and error states never claim there are no lessons.
- **Create:** "How would you like to start?" — Upload a document · Paste your notes · Open a lesson file, each saying
  what is accepted and what happens next; the title is prefilled once (no more "X — X"; names are also joined without
  repeats wherever the page shows them).
- **Writing:** the run's real stages (Understanding your content · Writing the lesson · Checking the lesson · Saving the
  lesson), "You can safely leave this page" with Continue later, an in-panel "Stop writing this lesson? Nothing has been
  saved yet." confirmation, "Something needs attention" with Try again / Back to your lessons; on a server without a
  lesson writer, writing is disabled with the note in place (documents and lesson files stay available).
- **Seven stages:** five states in icon + words (✓ Complete · ● In progress · → Ready · ⚠ Needs attention · ○ Not
  started), the open stage marked, any stage reachable directly, the suggested next stage labelled "Next step", one
  contextual primary action each; a reopened lesson opens on its next useful stage; consistent with the lesson's own
  state (a finished lesson never shows "Not started"); at 1280×720 all seven fit, at ≤ 900 px a compact stepper, at ≤
  600 px a "Stage N of 7" select.
- Polling pauses while the tab is hidden; the remembered run is kept per user (a hash of the user only).

### The editor
- The same look as the Studio (shared tokens, buttons, status pills); text ≥ 12 px; no all-caps; the selected scene
  shown by shape as well as colour.
- **Layouts per width:** > 1100 px scene list | stage | inspector with the timeline below; 901–1100 px the same,
  narrower (icon-only secondary buttons, names kept for screen readers); 701–900 px the inspector in its own row below
  the timeline (the ruler and scene row stay; a note says the detail tracks are hidden meanwhile) and a two-row top bar;
  ≤ 700 px one column (the scene list and the inspector) with a short note. The transport row is always visible; "Show /
  Hide inspector" brings the inspector back after Esc.
- **Contextual inspector:** the scene's lead section right after "Scene", and open (Presenter for presenter-led scenes,
  Visual for picture scenes, Text for formula and code scenes); a hidden presenter is one "Show presenter" line;
  sections that cannot apply are left out; camera, transition, background and style grouped under "Look and motion".
- "Back to the Studio" when opened from the Studio; save states and errors in plain words (what happened, the edits are
  safe, what to do); a modal dialog with focus kept inside and returned on close; a danger confirmation for deletion.

### Visual Review and quality
- **Origin, not provider:** a plain "Origin" line (Made with AI · Made with AI (backup) · Will be made with AI · From
  your library · Made with AI earlier, from your library · From the shared Aadhi library); the Phase 8 "AI provider"
  line, model and renderer only with `?visualDebug`. The Phase 8 / 9 browser checks verify the provenance in the debug
  view and through the API, and that normal mode shows none.
- States in icon + words (Needs review · Approved · Changed · Removed), plain filters ("Made with AI", "From the
  library", "Animations"), user-facing "Animation" / "Animated picture" instead of "Manim" / "GIF search", styled
  primary buttons, plain errors (what happened, the lesson is safe, what to do), focus moved in, kept inside and
  returned.
- **Quality (Phase 18 engine untouched):** "✓ Quality looks good" / "⚠ N things to review", a one-line help, findings by
  scene with "Show scene", "The lesson changed since this check — Check again"; rule ids and fingerprints only in debug.
- Visual Review's layout preview uses neutral colours (it showed the last played scene's style).

### Library and videos
- **Shared Aadhi assets found again:** the Library asks for the shared assets and the user's own at the same time (the
  existing `scope=system` / `scope=mine` filters, same search and filters) and shows "Shared Aadhi assets" first when
  browsing (the user's files first when picking); ownership and storage unchanged, no data deleted; the user's 200
  newest items keep their page. This makes the Phase 3 shared-clips check pass legitimately on the development database.
- Narration clips hidden by default ("All except narration"; "All files", Pictures, Videos, Sounds available); asset
  ids, MIME types, hashes and copy buttons only in debug; plain empty / loading / error states.
- **Videos panel:** "Your videos"; once a video is ready, Download is the main action and "Export again" secondary; "✓
  Export complete" with the next step; statuses in icon + words; the format as "WebM video"; plain errors ("Your lesson
  changes are saved. Try the export again."); the preview shows the first frame.
- **Chapters:** the opening chapter is "Introduction" unless a scene already has that title, then "Opening" (or "Lesson
  start", "Opening 2" … when those are taken too; compared ignoring case and spacing; no merging: every scene chapter
  keeps its real start).
- Both panels move focus in, keep it inside, close on Esc and return focus to their opener.

### What the stage shows for a missing visual (designed for the video)
- The stage used to draw developer output where a visual was missing, failed or still being made — red monospace error
  dumps, "[System Debug]" text, the manual-workflow card with its prompt and file path, "Compiling Manim Video..." — and
  so recorded it into videos. It now shows one plain card (`visualNotice`: a title, one sentence, an action when there
  is one), every value escaped; the technical details appear under it only with `?visualDebug`, and never while
  recording.

### Captions that fit their band (designed for the video)
- The composition reserves the bottom 15 % of the frame for captions (108 px at 720p), but captions are sized in pixels:
  a two-line caption reached into the presenter's name card and the board's lower edge. `captionFit` / `watchCaptions`
  (cinematic.js) measure the caption before paint and, only when it would leave its band, lower it (down to an 8 px /
  1.5 % gutter) and, if still needed, scale its font (never below 85 %), through two inline variables read by
  `stage-fit.css` (cinematic scenes only). A caption that fits — every one-line caption and the usual captions at 1080p
  — is drawn exactly as before; compositions, plans, approvals and styles are unchanged; preview and export run the same
  code.

### Security and performance
- The Giphy key is gone from the page (GIFs go through the server's `/get-gif`); lesson p5 code runs in a sandboxed
  frame (scripts only, no same-origin); signing out clears the saved login, the editor's unsaved drafts, the old shared
  Studio keys (the Studio's own keys are per user, under a hash of the user) and the previous person's picture /
  presenter choices, and the page starts again; another person signing in clears the same (their new login kept) and
  starts the page again if it held the previous person's lesson; the browser no longer keeps a backup copy of the lesson
  (it held signed links); server and provider text is never rendered as HTML.
- The Visual Router plans behind the Studio's lesson state are cached until the lesson, the visuals the router may
  choose from or the AI cache change (refreshed every 10 minutes); pdf.js and mammoth load only for the document kind
  that needs them; the unused page-level p5 script is gone.

### Multi-agent work
Five read-only analysts audited the product before any change (architecture and journeys, the design system, security
and performance, the known issues, and the browser at five viewports in real Chrome); the orchestrator wrote the
contract (rules, terminology, the never-recorded list, one owner per file) and the token layer `ui.css`. Six
implementation agents with sole ownership of their files: **C** (app shell and navigation: index.html, app.js,
product.css), **D** (the Studio: studio.js, studio.css), **E** (the editor: editor_ui.js, editor.css), **F** (Visual
Review and quality: review.js, visuals.js), **K** (videos and the Library: export.js, export_outputs.py, assets.js) and
**S** (stage-safe fixes and panels: cinematic.js, presenter.js, sources.js, stage-fit.css, source_analysis.py). The
orchestrator integrated, added the planner cache in server.py, and fixed what the reviews found. Then a read-only code
review of the integrated result, **N** (the Phase 21 browser check and the adaptation of the earlier browser checks; the
only agent running browsers), **Q** (a read-only product audit as an educator), a read-only inventory of the changed
files and a read-only documentation fact-check. The orchestrator inspected the screenshots at every viewport itself.

### Results
Silent regression (Chrome with `--mute-audio --disable-audio-output`, speech stubbed, no real AI provider call; the
Studio, editor and Phase 21 checks run their own servers with the stand-in writer, media and voice; the Phase 1–4 checks
use the development server, whose data was kept). Everything ran on the final code after the last change, in three
lanes: the Studio, editor and Phase 21 checks and the development server's checks in one, the other checks with servers
of their own in a second (separate ports), and the timing-sensitive checks (presenter, cinematic, recording performance)
alone at the end. An earlier full pass had found the paused-lesson defect below (Studio 68/69, reproduced alone); its
presenter and cinematic failures were timing (each passed when run alone). In this pass the visuals and AI cache checks
timed out while the two lanes ran together (waits of 15 s and 30 s under the load) and passed when re-run alone, with
nothing else running; those are the results below.
